# Decision Log

Canonical record of trustee-ratified decisions that bind the project's operational posture. New decisions are appended in reverse chronological order (newest at top). Decisions, once ratified, are never edited in place; corrections or reversals are recorded as new entries that reference the prior one.

The scope of this log is narrower than ADRs: ADRs cover architectural decisions; this log covers **trustee-ratified operational decisions** — runbook sign-offs, backup-engineer authorizations, substrate-validation outcomes, escrow openings, and other governance actions that the Trustee Panel records as durable commitments.

## Index of decision types

- **A-* assumptions** — pre-launch assumptions from PRD §10 carried into trustee ratification (e.g., A-13: backup-engineer retainer authorization).
- **Story 0.1 — Operational Runbooks** — sign-offs, execution-path selection, substitute-engineer authorization.
- **Story 0.2 — Credential Escrow** — sealing-mechanism + custodial-location selection, §2.10a envelope-class separation control, dry-run + table-top outcomes, ADR ratification.

### Decision 2026-08-14-108: Niyamavali §8.4a's "Statutory rights (DPDPA)" row is DISPOSITIONED — the only row in the table that had none, recorded as PARTIALLY mechanized in three parts and deliberately LEFT IN the stated-but-unmechanized list; both locales reproduced verbatim, because `docs/legal/` is untracked and this entry is its only durable copy

**Decision type:** A disclosure correction to a ratified instrument, landing **after** its mechanism. ⛔ **This entry answers NO Trustee-Panel question and discharges no escalation.** Escalations 1, 2, 3, 5, 7, 8, 9 and 10 remain open and unanswered.
**Status:** **Author-committed** (BigDev, Solo Builder, 2026-08-14). The disposition states what the tree now does; it ratifies nothing and confers no authority.
**Author:** BigDev, Solo Builder
**Ratifying trustees:** — (no ruling sought or given)

**Context:**
Story 10.21's Finding 7 established that §8.4a's mechanization-status note dispositions **four** rows — escalation justification, prior sanction, notice and opportunity, portal access — and that the **"Statutory rights (DPDPA)"** row was **not among them**. The row asserts that statutory rights *"Survive, exercised through an identity-verified administrative process"*. Until Story 10.21 landed, **no such process existed**: every DPDPA surface was member-session-gated, and the terminated-member notice promised a helpline route in shipped copy that the system could not honour.

⛔ **The ordering of this entry is deliberate and is itself the point.** §8.4a's own rule reads: *"A row is moved out of this list **only once the enforcing mechanism and its test are in place** — a disclosure that runs ahead of its mechanism is the same defect as one that trails it."* Story 10.21's governance entries (`2026-08-14-106`, `2026-08-14-107`) committed **first**, the implementation landed **next**, and this disclosure lands **now**. An earlier draft of the story required this edit in the first governance commit, which would have mandated the exact defect the rule names.

**Decision:**

1. **The row is dispositioned as PARTIALLY MECHANIZED, in three parts, because the parts differ in kind.** A single verdict would have been false in one direction or the other.

   **(a) Mechanized.** An identity-verified administrative route exists. A request is filed through the helpline as a helpdesk ticket (the existing Story 10.1 create primitive, `category: 'other'` + a data-rights subcategory — ⛔ no new category, `DEFAULT_ROUTING_POLICY` byte-identical). A **separate authority**, deliberately held apart from the authority to file the request, may **build** the member's data export off-session and may **execute erasure** on a member who holds no session. Each act is attributable to a **named staff actor** (a missing display name blocks the act), carries the **originating request**, and is recorded on the member's own event history so a replay distinguishes an operator-executed erasure from a member self-service one.

   **(b) Not mechanized, and NOT claimed.** **Correction** has no mechanism on any surface, for **any** member — not only for terminated ones. ⛔ The disposition says so in the instrument rather than letting the row's presence imply a fourth working right.

   **(c) Not yet settled.** How a built export may be **delivered** to a member who has no portal is an open question before the Trustee Panel (Escalation 1, `2026-08-14-106`). Until it is answered the export is **assembled and released to no one**.

2. **⛔ The row REMAINS in the stated-but-unmechanized list.** Moving it out would claim a completeness the tree does not have, and would violate the note's own rule while purporting to satisfy it. It moves out only when correction is mechanized and delivery is ruled and built.

3. **⭐ The ratified text of the added disposition bullet, reproduced verbatim in both locales.** Appended to the mechanization-status note under §8.4a in `docs/legal/niyamavali.md` and at the structurally identical position in `docs/legal/niyamavali.hi.md`. **Both files are untracked; this reproduction is the record.** The Hindi text is a co-equal governing instrument, not a translation artifact.

   **English —**

   > · **Statutory rights (DPDPA)** — ⚠ **partially mechanized, and the parts are stated separately because they differ in kind.** This row had **no disposition at all** until now, and had therefore been asserting an administrative process that did not exist. **Mechanized:** an identity-verified administrative route exists — a request is filed through the helpline as a helpdesk ticket, and a separate authority, held apart from the authority to file it, may **build** the member's data export off-session and may **execute erasure** on a member who holds no session. Each act is attributable to a named staff actor, carries the originating request, and is recorded on the member's own event history. **Not mechanized, and not claimed:** **correction** has no mechanism on any surface, for any member — not only for terminated ones. **Not yet settled:** how a built export may be **delivered** to a member who has no portal is an open question before the Trustee Panel; until it is answered, the export is assembled but released to no one. ⛔ This row therefore **remains in this list**, per this note's own rule — a disclosure that runs ahead of its mechanism is the same defect as one that trails it.

   **Hindi —**

   > · **वैधानिक अधिकार (DPDPA)** — ⚠ **आंशिक रूप से यंत्रीकृत, और भाग पृथक्-पृथक् कहे गए हैं क्योंकि वे प्रकार में भिन्न हैं।** इस पंक्ति का अब तक **कोई निपटान नहीं था**, और इसलिए यह एक ऐसी प्रशासनिक प्रक्रिया का दावा करती रही जो अस्तित्व में नहीं थी। **यंत्रीकृत:** एक पहचान-सत्यापित प्रशासनिक मार्ग विद्यमान है — अनुरोध हेल्पलाइन के माध्यम से एक हेल्पडेस्क टिकट के रूप में दर्ज होता है, और उसे दर्ज करने के प्राधिकार से पृथक् रखा गया एक अलग प्राधिकार, सत्र के बिना ही सदस्य का डेटा-निर्यात **निर्मित** कर सकता है तथा ऐसे सदस्य पर **मिटाना (erasure)** निष्पादित कर सकता है जिसके पास कोई सत्र नहीं है। प्रत्येक कार्य एक नामित कर्मचारी-कर्ता को अभिलिखित (attributable) होता है, मूल अनुरोध को वहन करता है, और सदस्य के अपने घटना-इतिहास पर दर्ज होता है। **अयंत्रीकृत, और इसका दावा नहीं किया गया:** **सुधार (correction)** का किसी भी सतह पर, किसी भी सदस्य के लिए — केवल समाप्त सदस्यों के लिए नहीं — कोई तंत्र नहीं है। **अभी अनिर्णीत:** निर्मित निर्यात ऐसे सदस्य को, जिसके पास कोई पोर्टल नहीं है, किस प्रकार **सुपुर्द** किया जा सकता है, यह ट्रस्टी पैनल के समक्ष एक खुला प्रश्न है; जब तक इसका उत्तर नहीं मिलता, निर्यात संकलित होता है किंतु किसी को जारी नहीं किया जाता। ⛔ अतः यह पंक्ति इस सूची में **बनी रहती है**, इसी टिप्पणी के अपने नियम के अनुसार — अपने तंत्र से आगे चलने वाला प्रकटन उतना ही दोषपूर्ण है जितना पीछे रहने वाला।

4. **[Author-committed] What this entry does NOT do.** It does not amend §8.4a's comparison table — **no row's text changes**, only the mechanization note gains a bullet. It does not amend §8.4, §8.5, §8.6 or §8.7. It does not version the Niyamavali or set an effective date. It does not authorise the `termination_access_block` flip, which remains a separate Panel-exclusive act. It does not discharge any standing Trustee Panel obligation and adds none. ⛔ **No existing Decision body is edited** ([[feedback_supersede_never_reinterpret]]); `2026-08-14-106` and `2026-08-14-107` stand as committed.

**Per-clause provenance** *(mandatory under Decision `2026-08-09-095`)*:

| Clauses | Provenance |
|---|---|
| **1–4** | **Author-committed in full.** Clause 1 states what the tree does after Story 10.21; clause 2 applies §8.4a's own stated rule; clause 3 reproduces text authored by BigDev; clause 4 is a statement of non-effect. ⛔ **No clause carries ratified authority**, and no Panel ruling was sought or given for any of it. |

**Open follow-ups:**
- **The row's remaining two parts are owed, and both already have owners.** Correction is Story 10.21's **AC-R2** (blocked on **Escalation 2**); delivery is **AC-R1** (blocked on **Escalation 1**). *Re-trigger:* when either escalation is ruled, this disposition must be re-stated to match — ⛔ a stale disposition is the same defect the row had before today.
- **⚠ The disposition is verifiable only against a running system.** Every claim in clause 1(a) is backed by a test in this repository, but `docs/legal/` is untracked, so nothing mechanically ties this text to those tests. If clause 1(a)'s claims and the tree ever diverge, **the tree is authoritative and this text is the defect.**

**References:**
- Decision **`2026-08-14-106`** — Story 10.21's authoring rulings and Escalations 1–9
- Decision **`2026-08-14-107`** — Escalation 10, the trustee-authority recipient
- Decision **`2026-08-10-097`** clauses 9–10 — where §8.4 was amended and §8.4a authored
- Decision **`2026-08-12-100`** — the precedent for updating §8.4a's mechanization disclosure in place
- `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/10-21-off-portal-dpdpa-access.md` — Finding 7 (the undispositioned row), AC8
- `.gitignore:68` — why clause 3 reproduces the text rather than citing it

### Decision 2026-08-14-107: Story 10.21's Escalation 10 is RAISED AND LEFT OPEN — an off-portal DPDPA request whose action requires **Trustee** authority has **no operational recipient**, because the Trustee Panel holds governance authority and **no helpdesk capability**; three adjacent sub-questions are recorded **CLOSED on evidence** so the Panel is not asked what the model already answers

**Decision type:** A **finding of fact** about the shipped routing/RBAC model, plus **one escalation raised**. ⛔ **This entry answers NO Trustee-Panel question**, and it decides nothing about who should hold the authority ([[feedback_closure_language_precision]] — raising an escalation is not discharging it).
**Status:** **Author-committed** (BigDev, Solo Builder, 2026-08-14). Every clause below is a trace result or a statement of non-effect; **no clause is trustee-ratified**. Recorded **before** implementation ([[feedback_governance_commits_precede_implementation]]).
**Author:** BigDev, Solo Builder
**Ratifying trustees:** pending — for Escalation 10 (see *Open follow-ups*)

**Context:**
Decision `2026-08-14-106` recorded Story 10.21's authoring rulings and left its escalations open. It establishes the **intake** route (`category: 'other'` + `sub_category: 'dpdpa-data-rights'`, clause 1) and the **fulfilment capability** (`member.data_rights` → `pariwar_admin` only). It does **not** establish where an individual DPDPA request goes **operationally** when the requested action requires **Trustee** authority.

A focused trace of that question was run on 2026-08-14 against `b860523`, deliberately **without** assuming that "Trustee" means `state_trustee` and **without** assuming a Trustee Panel destination — the project distinguishes Trustee Panel governance from RBAC roles, and routing is registry/policy-driven. The trace covered: existing helpdesk routing for data-rights requests; the `state_trustee` bundle; the Trustee Panel's authority and whether it has an operational queue; the routing-policy registry's category/subcategory mechanisms; and whether an existing role already supplies the required operational recipient.

⚠ **The trace ran AFTER `106` was committed.** Escalation 10 is therefore **absent from `106`**, and ⛔ `106` is **not edited** to insert it ([[feedback_supersede_never_reinterpret]]). This entry is additive and supersedes nothing.

**Decision:**

1. **⭐ Three sub-questions are recorded CLOSED on evidence, and are ⛔ NOT put to the Panel.** Escalating a settled question wastes a governance act and invites a ruling that contradicts shipped ratified text.

   **1a. "Trustee" is NOT `state_trustee` — closed twice over, independently.**
   *By ratified text:* Niyamavali §8.7 states it outright — *"The Trustee Panel is **not** the 'State Trustee panel' of Part 9. Part 9, Stage 2 names a State Trustee panel — a geographic body voting by majority under R9. The Trustee Panel constituted here is a **trust-wide governing body, not a geographic office**"* (Decision `2026-08-10-096` clause 9, both locales).
   *By structure, independently:* `state_trustee` (`packages/domain/src/rbac/roles.ts:362-369`) is `scopeCeiling: 'state'`, holds `claim.approve`, `member.suspend` (⚠ DEPRECATED, successor `member.moderate`), `member.view_validity`, `niyamavali.review`, `tc.approve` — and holds **neither `helpdesk.respond` nor `helpdesk.create`**. A `state` ceiling can never satisfy a `pariwar`-dimension check: `scopeWithinCeiling` (`packages/domain/src/rbac/scope.ts:132-137`) is a pure numeric compare over `CEILING_RANK` (`:73-76`, spread from `GEO_RANK` at `:65`) with **no resolver parameter**, so `pariwar:1 >= state:2` is false — the §RANK-ORDER note at `:89-91` states this exact example. ⛔ **No geo-tree resolver would lift this** ([[project_rbac_geo_scope_containment]]).

   **1b. The Helpdesk Operator may intake / verify / route but may NOT execute — already ruled, and NOT re-opened.** Story 10.21's AC3 grants `member.data_rights` to `pariwar_admin` **only**, ⛔ expressly not `helpline_operator`, on the stated ground that *filing a request and executing it on a member with no session are different authorities*. Escalation 10 asks a question **above** this boundary and never beneath it.

   **1c. The routing MECHANISM is adequate and needs no change.** Per-Pariwar versioned overrides plus a free-token `sub_category` (`packages/contracts/src/helpdesk/category.ts:48` — `z.string().min(1).max(64)`, no allow-list) already express any destination a ruling could name. ⛔ `106` clause 1's byte-identical `DEFAULT_ROUTING_POLICY` posture stands and is unaffected.

2. **⛔ FINDING — `routed_to_role` is INERT. Routing carries NO authority whatsoever.** It is written once at ticket creation (`packages/domain/src/helpdesk/project.ts:227`), stored `notNull` (`packages/domain/src/schema/helpdesk_tickets.ts:148`), and read in exactly **one** place: an **optional, caller-supplied** `routed_to_role` query parameter on the responder queue read (`packages/domain/src/helpdesk/read.ts:71,90`; `apps/api/src/modules/helpdesk/handlers.ts:346`). **No transition, no detail read and no permission check consults it.** Every `helpdesk.respond` holder in the Pariwar can see, pick up, reply to and resolve **any** ticket in that Pariwar regardless of where it routed. ⇒ **Routing is advisory — SLA attribution and a "my queue" convenience — and is not an access-control destination.**

3. **⛔ FINDING — the registry does NOT constrain `target_role` to the seeded role catalog.** `validateRoutingPolicyRules` (`packages/domain/src/helpdesk/registry.ts:184-189`) checks `target_role` only for *non-empty* and *≤ `MAX_TARGET_ROLE_LENGTH`*. So a per-Pariwar override naming a role that cannot act — or a **typo** — validates and publishes. Combined with clause 2, the resulting ticket displays a destination no one can act on, with **no error anywhere**. This is the same silent-misroute class `106` clause 1 avoided for categories, reachable through a different door.

4. **⭐ FINDING — the Trustee Panel has governance authority and NO operational queue.** `trustee_panel` **is** a real seeded role — the thirteenth bundle (`roles.ts:583-627`, Story 10.18, Decision `2026-08-10-096` clause 4) at `scopeCeiling: 'pariwar'`, so **unlike** `state_trustee` it *could* satisfy a pariwar-dimension check. But its permissions are exactly **`member.moderate` + `member.restore_terminated`**. ⛔ It cannot see the responder queue, open a ticket, reply or resolve. A `grep` for `trustee_panel` across `packages/domain/src`, `apps/api/src` and `packages/contracts/src` returns only `rbac/{roles,permissions,scope}.ts` and `member-moderation/routes.ts:43` — **no helpdesk module at all.**

5. **⛔ FINDING — the DPDPA request routes to the one role forbidden from executing it.** `106` clause 1 routes every data-rights request through the `other` catch-all, whose default rule targets **`helpline_operator`** at `dimension: 'pariwar'` (`registry.ts:62`) — precisely the role clause 1b bars from fulfilment. Per clause 2 nothing moves it to a role that can, and nothing complains. ⚠ **This was previously seen and left undispositioned:** Story 10.21's final validation pass recorded it as finding **F6** — *"the role half is described in Finding 5 and dispositioned nowhere"* — and triaged it non-blocking. **Escalation 10 absorbs F6's role half.** F6's **SLA** half remains dispositioned as *"carried knowingly, pending Escalation 5"* and is ⛔ not re-opened here.

6. **⚠ RECORDED, NOT A DEFECT — the `actor: 'trustee'` pin is a COARSE label, not a Panel attribution.** `106` clause 5 pins the off-portal erasure event to `actor: 'trustee'` while `member.data_rights` is held by `pariwar_admin`, which is expressly **Trustee-Lite** (`roles.ts:104,108,113`). This is **not** a false attribution: `memberActorSchema` is `z.enum(['member','system','trustee'])` (`packages/domain/src/member/audit-shape.ts:19`) with no finer staff label, and the in-family precedent is `member/moderation/write.ts:315`. Recorded **only** so no later reader mistakes the event payload for evidence that a Panel act occurred. ⛔ No change to `106` clause 5 is implied or authorised.

7. **⛔ NOTHING IS DECIDED about who holds Trustee authority over a statutory right.** Clauses 1–6 are findings. The question they expose is raised as **Escalation 10** below and is the **Trustee Panel's** to answer. ⛔ The implementation agent does not choose it, and a routing default is not an answer to it.

8. **[Author-committed] What this entry does NOT do.** It does not amend the Niyamavali and authors no clause text. It does not mint, remove or re-grant any permission key, and ⛔ **does not grant `member.data_rights` to `trustee_panel`.** It does not change `DEFAULT_ROUTING_POLICY`, publish any per-Pariwar override, or alter `routed_to_role`'s semantics. It does not re-open `106` clause 1 (intake) or the AC3 operator boundary. It does not authorise the `termination_access_block` flip, and it **discharges no standing Trustee Panel obligation** — it **adds one**. ⛔ **No existing Decision body is edited**, per [[feedback_supersede_never_reinterpret]]; `2026-08-14-106` stands exactly as committed, and this entry supersedes nothing in it.

**Per-clause provenance** *(mandatory under Decision `2026-08-09-095`)*:

| Clauses | Provenance |
|---|---|
| **1–8** | **Author-committed in full.** Clauses 1–6 are trace results re-derived from the tree at `b860523`; clause 7 is a statement that the substantive question is untaken; clause 8 is a statement of non-effect. ⛔ **No clause here carries ratified authority.** The ratified material clause 1a *cites* — Niyamavali §8.7 — was ratified by Decision `2026-08-10-096` and is quoted, not re-ratified. |

**Open follow-ups:**
⛔ **One escalation is raised here and is NOT answered by this entry.**

- **Escalation 10 — the trustee-authority recipient. BLOCKS Story 10.21's AC-R3.** Posed as **three** questions, because a binary *"route it to the Panel or not"* form would smuggle in its own answer — the defect Escalation 1 was re-posed to avoid.
  **(i)** Does **any** off-portal DPDPA action require **Trustee Panel** authority — specifically **erasure of a terminated member**, which sits adjacent to the Panel-exclusive `member.restore_terminated` (Niyamavali §8.4; Decision `2026-08-10-097` clause 1)? ⭐ If **none** does, that is a complete answer: AC-R3 closes with a recorded disposition and **no code changes**.
  **(ii)** If some action does, **who is the operational recipient?** ⚠ The Panel has governance authority and **no operational queue** (clause 4). The options are (a) grant the `trustee_panel` bundle the fulfilment capability, (b) keep execution with `pariwar_admin` as Trustee-Lite and require a recorded Panel authorisation as a caller precondition, or (c) rule that Trustee authority attaches to the **decision** and never to the **execution**. ⛔ Story 10.21 does not choose among these.
  **(iii)** ⚠ **If a *routed* destination is named, the Panel should know it carries NO enforcement** (clauses 2–3): `routed_to_role` is an advisory filter no authorization path reads, and `target_role` is not constrained to the seeded catalog. Enforcement must land in a **permission grant and/or a caller precondition**, never in the routing policy alone.
  ⭐ **The Panel should know the sequencing is free**, exactly as for Escalation 1: `termination_access_block` is DEFAULT OFF and its flip is gated on Story 10.21 landing, so **no member can be terminated-with-access-ended while this is decided** — the off-portal arm is unreachable in production until after the flip.
  ⚠ **Scope of the block, stated precisely:** Escalation 10 blocks **AC-R3 only**. It removes **no** un-blocked scope from Story 10.21 — the un-blocked set remains AC1–AC4, AC7–AC15 (minus AC11's `consumed`-status arm) plus AC5's off-portal-build half. But it **is a release-gate input**: ⛔ the gate must not be read as dischargeable while it is open.
  *Owner:* **Trustee Panel.** *Re-trigger:* **immediate** — it blocks AC-R3 and is due **before the `termination_access_block` flip**, ⛔ never at a later epic ([[project_r7_fact_producer_unbuilt]] — a deferral naming an epic expires unowned).

- ⚠ **Panel-queue accounting by enumeration, not arithmetic.** Across Story 10.21 the owners are: **Trustee Panel (or Panel + Counsel)** — Escalations 1, 2, 3, 5, 8, 9, **10** (seven). **A named successor story, ⛔ not the Panel** — 4, 6 (two). **Ambiguous by construction** — 7 (enters the queue only if the Panel takes it). The standing queue stood at **NINE** after Story 10.20 (`deferred-work.md:168`). ⛔ **Do not add these numbers to that count** — state the queue by enumeration, as a count, **not as progress**.

- ⚠ **A `roles.ts`-catalog constraint on `target_role` is NOT proposed here.** Clause 3 records the gap; whether the registry should reject unknown target roles is a design question with replay consequences for already-published overrides, and it is **not** raised as an escalation because no story owns it yet. *Re-trigger:* the first story that changes `validateRoutingPolicyRules`, or the first per-Pariwar override authored for `dpdpa-data-rights` — whichever comes first.

**References:**

- Decision **`2026-08-10-097`** clause 1 (restoration from termination is a Panel act), clause 12 bullet 4 (the flip gate)

- `packages/domain/src/rbac/scope.ts:132-137` — `scopeWithinCeiling`, the pure numeric compare with no resolver parameter; `:73-76` `CEILING_RANK`; `:89-91` the §RANK-ORDER note. ⚠ `roles.ts:583-627`'s comment cites `:113-118` for this, which is **stale** — that range is a comment block

### Decision 2026-08-14-106: Story 10.21's authoring rulings — intake reuses `other` + a free subcategory with `DEFAULT_ROUTING_POLICY` **byte-identical**, delivery and correction are split into **ruling-gated AC-R1/AC-R2**, `helpdesk_ticket_id` is **optional** in the shared strict event payload — and **five escalations are RAISED AND LEFT OPEN**, so the `termination_access_block` release gate remains **OPEN**

**Decision type:** Engineering-substrate decision **+** the governance dispositions that accompany it. ⛔ **This entry answers NO Trustee-Panel question.** Five escalations are raised here and deliberately left unresolved; the entry records that they are owed, not what they decide ([[feedback_closure_language_precision]] — raising an escalation is not discharging it).
**Status:** **Author-committed** (BigDev, Solo Builder, 2026-08-14), ruled at Story 10.21 authoring and hardened across six validation passes. Recorded here **before** implementation ([[feedback_governance_commits_precede_implementation]]).
**Author:** BigDev, Solo Builder
**Ratifying trustees:** pending — for Escalations 1, 2, 3, 5, 8, 9 (see *Open follow-ups*)

**Context:**
Niyamavali §8.4 is ratified and the terminated-member notice already promises a helpline route in shipped copy, both locales (`packages/i18n/locales/{en,hi}/common.json:341`). **No such process exists.** Every DPDPA surface is member-session-gated. Story 10.21 builds the process and is a **release gate**: the `termination_access_block` flag ships DEFAULT OFF and its flip is gated on this story landing (Decision `2026-08-10-097` clause 12 bullet 4; Decision `2026-08-10-098` clause 2 — ⚠ **not** `097` clause 6, whose literal text reads permissively and is a known mis-citation trap).

Authoring surfaced **nine findings**, three of which invalidate the obvious implementation: erasure is blocked by a lifecycle guard predating the overlay model; the export assembler returns `records: []` for the member's entire contribution life; and correction has **no mechanism anywhere, for any member**.

**Decision:**
1. **Intake reuses the Story 10.1 create primitive UNCHANGED.** A data-rights request is filed through the existing `POST /api/v1/p/:pariwarId/helpdesk/tickets` with `category: 'other'` + `sub_category: 'dpdpa-data-rights'`. ⛔ **No new category, no enum migration, and `DEFAULT_ROUTING_POLICY` stays byte-identical** — a diff on that constant or on `default-policy-hash.test.ts`'s `EXPECTED_HASH` is a failed acceptance criterion, not a rebase artifact. Rationale: a newly-minted category is absent from every per-Pariwar override authored before today and would **silently mis-route** to that Pariwar's generic `other` desk under the wrong SLA, with no error anywhere (`helpdesk/routing.ts:108-111` Phase-2 fallthrough; `registry.ts:209-210` makes the catch-all mandatory in every published policy).
2. **The golden-hash guard's own prescribed remedy is a defect and is NOT followed.** `default-policy-hash.test.ts` advises bumping `DEFAULT_ROUTING_POLICY_VERSION`; doing so would make every already-published version-2 override resolve to the default document and every version-1 ticket **un-replayable** (`registry.ts:12-13`, `:118`). Recorded as Escalation 4 against a named successor story. This story's design avoids needing the bump.
3. **Delivery and correction are SPLIT OUT as ruling-gated AC-R1 / AC-R2.** An earlier draft asserted staff-mediated delivery *"is required as the fallback under either ruling"* and built it. **That assertion was unfounded** — it is precisely one of the questions being asked. Both now require a ratified decision-log entry cited by decision id before implementation. ⛔ The implementation agent does not choose the PII delivery posture and does not decide what discharges the release gate.
4. **`helpdesk_ticket_id` is OPTIONAL** in `RtbfAnonymizedPayloadSchema` — `z.string().uuid().optional()`, `.strict()` **retained**, `.passthrough()` forbidden. Required would break every member self-service RTBF at runtime: the member path emits a four-field payload (`rtbf/handlers.ts:112-117`) parsed before insert (`member/project.ts:78`). Follows the same-module precedent `KycCompletedPayloadSchema` (`member/events.ts:40-42`). ⚠ **Consequence recorded:** optionality moves the provenance guarantee out of the schema and into the caller — the off-portal handler **requires** the field and fails closed without it.
5. **The off-portal erasure event is pinned to `actor: 'trustee'` and `trigger: 'member_data_rights.rtbf_fulfilled'`**, following the member family's dotted-namespace convention for staff-initiated acts (`member/moderation/write.ts:314-315`, `grounds.ts:245`). ⛔ Not the member exemplar's `actor: 'member'` / `trigger: 'rtbf_request'`, which copied verbatim would write a **false actor attribution**.
6. **Erasure legality lives in the CALLER, never the reducer** (the Story 10.20 WS-D shape). The `member.rtbf_anonymized` arm widens to every `MEMBER_LIFECYCLE_STATES` label except `anonymized` — eight `from` states, derived from the enum by a totality test. ⛔ **No `terminated` lifecycle state is introduced**; that is the rejected model the whole 10.16–10.23 correct-course exists to prevent.
7. **The `other` rule's SLA (24h / 5 business days) is inherited KNOWINGLY, not endorsed** — Escalation 5 declares the statutory response horizon legally unverified. ⛔ No different number is invented to look compliant.
8. **No `dpo` role is minted.** FR-99 activates at the MeitY threshold, DPO appointment is OQ-7 (open), and minting a role bundle for an unconstituted body is the inert-capability defect Story 10.18 exists to teach.
9. ⛔ **The §8.4a "Statutory rights (DPDPA)" disposition does NOT ride this commit.** It lands in or after the `story(10.21):` implementation commit. The governance-first convention orders **decisions**; the §8.4a row is a **claim about what the tree enforces**, and committed ahead of its mechanism it asserts a compliance that does not exist — the exact defect §8.4a's own rule names (`docs/legal/niyamavali.md:211`, `:218`).
10. ⛔ **The `termination_access_block` flip is NOT authorised by this entry and is not this story's act.** Landing 10.21 *discharges the gate's precondition*; the flip remains a separate, Panel-exclusive act requiring its own decision-log entry.

**Open follow-ups:**
⛔ **Five of these BLOCK work and are unresolved. Nothing below is answered by this entry.**
- **Escalation 1 — delivery of the export artifact. BLOCKS AC-R1.** Posed as three questions, not a binary: (i) is staff-mediated delivery — a staff actor obtaining a member's assembled, decrypted Tier-1 export — permitted **at all**? (ii) if an OTP-verified member grant is the primary, is staff-mediated permitted as a fallback for a member who no longer controls the registered mobile, and under what conditions? (iii) if permitted at all, is it permitted as the **v1 primary**? *Owner:* Trustee Panel. ⭐ The Panel should know **the sequencing is free** — the flag is DEFAULT OFF and its flip is gated on this story, so no member can be stranded without a route while this is decided.
- **Escalation 2 — correction. BLOCKS AC-R2.** The right is unmechanized for **all** members, not only terminated ones (no member-profile write path exists on any surface). **What discharges the release gate** — three mechanized rights plus a recorded, staff-executed correction process, or all four mechanized first? *Owner:* Trustee Panel.
- **Escalation 3 — moderation-record disclosure.** Is `decision_note_ciphertext` (plus 10.20's three further Tier-1 columns and `actor_display`) owed to the data principal under the access right? *Owner:* Trustee Panel + Legal Counsel. ⚠ Counsel is **not engaged** — every field in `docs/legal-counsel-engagement/` is `<PENDING>`; record as **un-attested** if it arrives on Panel attestation alone ([[feedback_record_unattested_no_backfill]]).
- **Escalation 4 — the default-policy versioning defect.** *Owner:* a named successor story, ⛔ never an epic ([[project_r7_fact_producer_unbuilt]]). *Re-trigger:* the first story needing to change the default routing policy's rules.
- **Escalation 5 — the statutory response horizon.** Whether 24h / 5 business days satisfies the DPDPA window. *Owner:* Trustee Panel + Legal Counsel.
- **Escalation 6 — the inoperative RTBF cascade.** `0033:40` and `schema/data_exports.ts:19` both state RTBF removal happens via `ON DELETE CASCADE`; Story 3.12 shipped a **soft** delete, so the cascade has never fired. ⚠ The class is wider than `data_exports`. *Owner:* a named successor story, ⛔ never an epic.
- **Escalation 7 — the export section contracts structurally forbid records. BLOCKS AC5's content half.** `records: z.array(z.never())` + `_status: z.literal(...)` (`contracts/data-export/data-export.ts:61-87`), contract-validated on every build. The record **shapes were never specified** — a data-contract decision with PII consequences. *Owner:* a named successor story **or** this story re-scoped by ruling — Panel/PO decides which.
- **Escalation 8 — `claim_history.json` has no subject predicate. BLOCKS AC5's claim arm.** Claims are about a *deceased* member; the requesting member appears in ≥6 roles across 7 tables, and "non-empty records" is satisfied by joins that would export **another member's** identity, nominee bank details or medical disclosure. ⚠ FR-95 never names claim history (`prd.md:1233`), so its scope is unspecified from every direction. *Owner:* Trustee Panel + Legal Counsel.
- **Escalation 9 — `consumed`-row status semantics. BLOCKS AC11's `consumed` arm only.** Flipping a `consumed` `data_exports` row to `expired` erases the record that the member **actually downloaded their export** — a fact AC11 separately promises to keep. The **zeroing** is uncontroversial; the **status change** is contested. *Owner:* Trustee Panel + Legal Counsel.
- ⚠ **Panel-queue accounting by enumeration, not arithmetic:** Panel (or Panel + Counsel) — 1, 2, 3, 5, 8, 9. Named successor story, **not** the Panel — 4, 6. Ambiguous by construction — 7. The standing queue stood at **NINE** after Story 10.20 (`deferred-work.md:168`); state the new count by enumeration and as a count, not as progress.
- **Session revocation is a shipped defect wider than this story.** RTBF revokes nothing and `requireMemberSession` is a stateless JWT verify, so an erased member reaches any session-only route for the remainder of a ~15-minute TTL. Story 10.21 closes only the `data_exports` surface. *Owner:* a named successor story.

**References:**
- `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/10-21-off-portal-dpdpa-access.md` — story file (nine findings, seventeen ACs, nine escalations)
- `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/epics.md:4037-4056` — Story 10.21, minted ACs
- `docs/legal/niyamavali.md:188,192-218` — §8.4 statutory-rights survival; §8.4a and its mechanization-status rule
- `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/prds/prd-TWT-2026-05-22/prd.md:1231-1244` — FR-95 / FR-96 and the terminated-member carve-out
- Decision `2026-08-10-097` clause 12 bullet 4, and Decision `2026-08-10-098` clause 2 — the flip-gate posture (⛔ **not** `097` clause 6)
- `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/deferred-work.md:4043-4047` — the Story 10.19 flip record

### Decision 2026-08-13-105: Story 10.28's seven rulings — `ResolvedReportScope` becomes **multi-valued** (`values: readonly string[]`, one source of truth, every consumer a compile error), authorization over N nodes is **ALL and never ANY**, an **empty value set DENIES at the source**, and the `state` narrowing arm is **"Closed by [edit]" with NO successor minted** on zero-consumer evidence

**Decision type:** Engineering-substrate decision **+** the governance dispositions that accompany it. **Not** a Trustee-Panel matter — no policy, eligibility or cadence commitment moves here ([[feedback_architecture_vs_prd_boundary]]); this rules the cardinality of Story 10.7's report-scope model and disposes of two limitations, one of which is closed **without an owner**.
**Status:** **Author-committed** (BigDev, Solo Builder, 2026-08-13), ruled at Story 10.28 authoring — *"D1–D7 ruled as recommended"*, with Escalations 1–4 dispositioned in the same pass. Recorded here **before** implementation ([[feedback_governance_commits_precede_implementation]]).
**Author:** BigDev (Solo Builder)
**Ratifying trustees:** n/a — not a Trustee-Panel matter (see decision type)
**Supersedes:** nothing. Decision `2026-08-12-102` (which **minted** Story 10.28 and ruled its existence **unconditional**) and Decision `2026-08-13-104` are **not** edited, re-read or superseded ([[feedback_supersede_never_reinterpret]]); this entry rules the questions Story 10.28's authoring raised.

**Context:**

Story 10.7 shipped `ResolvedReportScope` as `{ dimension, value: string | null }` — **single-valued**. `resolveActorReportScope` picks the broadest dimension across an actor's grants with a **strict-`<`** tie-break, so a second grant at the *same* dimension never displaces the first:

```
reports/scope.ts:86   if (best === null || broadnessRank(candidate.dimension) < broadnessRank(best.dimension))
                      strict `<`  ⇒  the SECOND same-dimension grant never displaces the first
                      ⇒  best = whichever grant the `grants` array happened to iterate first
```

An actor holding `member.export_roster` at **`{district,'Patna'}` AND `{district,'Gaya'}`** — two legitimate `district_admin` grants, both inside the `district` ceiling — therefore resolves to **one** of them, and both district-narrowable templates emit `AND cp.district = <one>`.

⛔ **The resulting failure is SILENT.** No error, no warning, no partial-export signal: the Gaya members are simply absent from a CSV the administrator will read as complete. That silence — not the cardinality as such — is what this story exists to remove.

⚠ **Cardinality and ancestry are ORTHOGONAL, and this is the third story in a row that must not conflate them.** Story 1.18 shipped the geo-tree resolver (ADR-0038) and this limitation **outlived** it: ancestry is one actor reaching nodes *beneath* a grant; multi-node is one actor holding grants at *several sibling* nodes. Story 1.18 could have absorbed this cheaply once the ancestry work was in hand and **deliberately did not** (1.18 AC6; D6's *"unless it falls out cheaply"* branch closed), because a permanent owner born already-discharged is a contradiction.

**Decision:**

1. **D1 — ARM A: `value` is REPLACED by `values: readonly string[]`. ⛔ Not augmented.** `ResolvedReportScope` becomes `{ dimension, values }`, carrying **every** node the actor holds the key at, at the winning dimension. ⭐ **One source of truth, and the compile errors ARE the migration aid** — the four read sites (`assemble.ts:55-57`, `_shared.ts:69-70`, `handlers.ts:350-351`, `reports-export.ts:177`) cannot be silently missed. ⛔ **REJECTED — keeping `value` and adding an optional `values`:** two sources of truth for one fact, where a consumer reading the old field compiles, passes, and silently keeps today's bug. That is precisely the shape this story exists to delete. **Sub-rulings, all ruled as stated:**
   - **(i)** `global` ⇒ `values: []`, and the invariant **`dimension === 'global' ⇔ values.length === 0`** is asserted by a unit test. `global` is the one dimension whose canonical target value is null (`rbac/scope.ts:236`).
   - **(ii)** **Sort + dedupe AT THE PRODUCER** (`resolveActorReportScope`), never at each consumer — determinism for SQL, for tests, and for D4's audit attribution.
   - **(iii)** `pariwar` ⇒ `values: [pariwarId]` (unchanged in substance).
   - **(iv)** ⭐ **No null filter and no `!` assertion.** `grantScopeWellFormed` (`reports/scope.ts:38-43`) already guarantees `scopeValue` non-null for every non-`global` dimension, so the set is built by narrowing through the existing guard. ⛔ A defensive `.filter(Boolean)` added "just in case" would **swallow a future well-formedness regression** instead of failing closed.
2. **D2 — authorization over N nodes is ALL, never ANY.** `checkPermission` takes **one** `ResourceLocator` (`rbac/check.ts:209`, `:262`), so N values mean N calls and **EVERY** one must pass (AND). For `global`, one call with `value: null`. Every value in the set came from a grant that already passed the bundle + ceiling + active-Pariwar filters (`reports/scope.ts:64-72`), so **ALL passes by construction today** — which is exactly why requiring ALL costs nothing now, and why it fails **closed** if `resolveActorReportScope` and `checkPermission` ever drift apart. ⛔ **OR would be a genuine escalation**: one qualifying node would authorize a query that reads N. ⛔ **The Open/Closed invariant survives** (10.7 AC1, inherited from 10.6 Decision 5): the loop iterates **scope values**, never `reportType`. Both proofs stay green **unmodified** — the **source-text** check (`tests/reports/assemble.test.ts:52`, which `readFileSync`s `assemble.ts` itself) and the **two-divergent-fixture behavioural** proof (`:34`). ⚠ The source-text test reads the file it guards, so a careless refactor can break it in a way `typecheck` will not.
3. ⭐ **D3 — ARM B: the `state` narrowing arm STAYS `deny`, labelled *"Closed by [edit]"*, and ⛔ NO SUCCESSOR IS MINTED.** ⛔ **REJECTED — building `state` → `WHERE district IN (<descendants>)`:** it needs a district **enumeration** API that does not exist (`GeoTreeResolver` is `contains`-only **by interface** — architectural freeze row 9; `LoadedGeoTree.parents` is child→parent **only**, `geo-tree/resolver.ts:100-105`), it needs a widened `ReportScopeCtx` seam, and it is **unreachable by every real role**. **The evidence, not a preference:** no role in `roles.ts` holds a district-narrowable report key at a `state` ceiling — `state_trustee` (`:361-369`, ceiling `state`) holds `member.view_validity` but **not** `member.export_roster`; `member.export_roster` is held only by `pariwar_admin` (`:341`, ceiling `pariwar`) and `district_admin` (`:401`, ceiling `district`); `reconciliation.review` is held only at `pariwar` ceilings (`:319` under `pariwar_admin`, `:452` under `finance_officer`). ⇒ **zero live consumers, zero backlog consumers, no FR.** That is the exact evidentiary shape on which Story 1.18's **D4-R** closed `self` **by edit with no successor**, rather than manufacturing an un-gated re-commitment ([[feedback_record_unattested_no_backfill]]).
   ⭐ **THE REASON MUST BE DURABLE THIS TIME — it has already been wrong twice.** *"No geo-tree resolver until Story 1.18"* expired when 1.18 shipped; *"`DistrictNarrowing` / `ResolvedReportScope` are SINGLE-VALUED"* expires the moment D1 lands. The **third** reason is the one that does not decay:
   > `state` denies because **no role holds a district-narrowable report key at a `state` ceiling** — there is no actor to serve, and no enumeration API exists because none was ever needed. If a state-ceiling role ever gains such a key, **that** story raises it with a live requirement attached.

   ⛔ **Do NOT mint a story, an epic pointer, or a "re-trigger: someday" line.** *A deferral naming an epic expires unowned* ([[project_r7_fact_producer_unbuilt]]), and minting an owner for work nobody has asked for is the un-gated re-commitment [[feedback_record_unattested_no_backfill]] warns decays. ⛔ `block` (rank order — a resolver can only ever narrow) and `self` (not a tree node, `GeoNode` excludes it **by type**) are **untouched**; their reasons remain correct and are re-stated verbatim.
   ⚠ **AC4's own verb is independent evidence for this arm.** The minted text says the pin is *"re-pinned **as a query deny-deeper pin**"* — which presupposes it remains a **deny** pin.
4. **D4 — audit actor-role attribution matches on the SET, takes the FIRST, and its residual ambiguity is RECORDED rather than solved.** Both sites (`handlers.ts:347-353`, `reports-export.ts:175-178`) resolve the acting role by matching a grant against the resolved scope; the match becomes `dimension === resolvedScope.dimension && resolvedScope.values.includes(g.scopeValue)`, first hit — **deterministic because D1(ii) sorts the set**. ⚠ **Stated openly rather than solved:** an actor holding *different roles* at *different nodes* has **one** of those roles recorded. That is **not a regression** — today's code has the same ambiguity with **less** determinism — and the 10.7 review already chose *"the grant that actually authorized"* over *"the first grant in the tenant"*. ⛔ **No audit column, no array field, no second audit line.**
5. **D5 — `DistrictNarrowing`'s kind is RENAMED `district` → `districts`, and an EMPTY set DENIES AT THE SOURCE.** The ruled shape is `{ kind: 'all' } | { kind: 'districts'; districts: readonly string[] } | { kind: 'deny' }`, where `districts` is **NON-EMPTY by construction**. The rename is deliberate: **every call site becomes a compile error**, and a silently-still-compiling call site is how the trap below ships. ⛔ **`districts.length === 0` ⇒ return `{ kind: 'deny' }` inside `resolveDistrictNarrowing`**, so no consumer can ever hold an empty array — the invariant is enforced at the **one** narrowing authority, not by convention at two query sites. Under D1 this is defence-in-depth (a non-global scope with an empty set is already unreachable), and defence-in-depth is exactly what this trap warrants.
   ⭐ **WHY THIS IS THE DANGEROUS ONE — it is a privilege escalation with a `.length === 0` on it.** `WHERE district IN ()` is a Postgres **syntax error**, so it arrives as a runtime failure in a test, and the smallest edit that makes the error go away is to **drop the predicate** — converting a fail-closed deny into a **full-tenant export** without touching a single line that mentions authorization. It is therefore pinned by a **polarity pair**, both halves mandatory: (a) a two-district scope ⇒ **both** districts in the `IN` list; (b) an empty set ⇒ **zero rows against a seeded tenant that HAS rows** — never the tenant.
   **SQL:** `sql.join(districts.map((d) => sql`${d}`), sql`, `)`. ⛔ **Never string-concatenate** — `templates.test.ts:54-60` already proves a district value can be hostile (`=SUM(A1:A9)`). Both templates use raw `client.execute(sql…)`, not the query builder, so drizzle's `inArray` does not apply.
6. **D6 — `resolveActorReportScope`'s SIGNATURE does not change.** Same parameters, same `null`-when-unheld contract, same purity. Only the return **shape** widens, keeping the three call sites (`handlers.ts:103`, `:345`, `reports-export.ts:165`) structurally unchanged and the diff legible. ⛔ **Do not add a `geoResolver` parameter** — that is D3 arm A by the back door.
7. **D7 — de-duplication is a `Set`, pinned by a test that CAN ACTUALLY FAIL.** Dedupe happens at the producer (D1(ii)). The pin is **not** hypothetical: an actor holding `district_admin` **and** a second key-bearing role at the *same* district produces the same node twice **today**. A unit test asserting `values` contains `'Patna'` exactly once satisfies AC5's *"must not double-count"* with a genuinely falsifiable test ([[feedback_gate_scope_semantic_coverage]]) — unlike an ancestry-based double-count test, which under D3 could never run.
8. **Escalation 1 — `contribution_rate_by_district`'s district branch is DEAD for every real holder; edit it anyway, and record the unreachability AT THE SITE.** `CONTRIBUTION_RATE_PERMISSION_KEY = 'reconciliation.review'` is held only by pariwar-ceiling roles, so `resolveDistrictNarrowing` can **never** return a districts-narrowing for it (already recorded at `deferred-work.md:3447`). ✅ **Ruled: accept the recommendation** — edit for **shape consistency** (it shares `_shared.ts`) and record the unreachability at the site. ⛔ **Do NOT invent a district-capable grant** to make AC2's second template demonstrable — that is manufacturing a consumer to satisfy a test. **`member-roster` carries AC3's proof**, as it already carries 10.7's district-narrowing proof.
9. **Escalation 2 — REVERT-SANITY IS MANDATORY, and both observations are recorded with their REAL output.** Ruled in BigDev's own words: *"The AC3 test must demonstrably go RED when the old strict-`<` implementation is restored, then GREEN after restoration."* A two-grant test written against the new code passes trivially; the probe is the only thing that proves it discriminates **both districts** rather than **some rows**. ⛔ A described probe is not a run ([[feedback_record_unattested_no_backfill]]); a probe never made to fail proves nothing ([[feedback_gate_scope_semantic_coverage]]). ⛔ The restoration is verified **byte-for-byte**, not assumed — the Story 1.18 AC8 discipline, whose revert probes proved the tests discriminated *ancestry* rather than *presence*.
10. **Escalation 3 — D4's residual attribution ambiguity is RECORDED, with NO successor and NO additional audit mechanism.** It lands in `deferred-work.md` as a **recorded limitation with no owner** — deliberately, on the same discipline as D3. The entry states the ambiguity, states that today's code already has it with **less** determinism, and stops. ⛔ No audit column, no array field, no second audit line, no *"re-trigger: someday"*.
11. **Escalation 4 — request→build scope drift is already correct; ⛔ do NOT "fix" it.** The idempotency key is **actor-scoped** and excludes scope (`paramsDigest` hashes `params` only; `findActiveReportExport(tx, actorId, …)`), and the build worker **re-resolves** grants and tree at build time (`reports-export.ts:163-192`), so a grant added between request and build **widens** the export and one revoked **narrows** it — deliberate, and documented at `:183-190`. ✅ **Ruled: leave unchanged.** ⛔ Freezing scope into the pending row would reintroduce the resolved-scope columns Story 10.7 deliberately removed.

**Open follow-ups:**

- ⛔ **D3's `state` narrowing arm is *"Closed by [edit]"* with NO OWNER, and this is deliberate.** It is **not** a deferral and must never be re-read as one ([[feedback_closure_language_precision]] — the three labels are never collapsed). Should a state-ceiling role ever gain a district-narrowable report key, that story raises the enumeration question **with a live requirement attached**.
- ⛔ **Escalation 3's audit-attribution ambiguity is RECORDED WITH NO OWNER**, on the same discipline. Recorded in `deferred-work.md`; no successor minted.
- **`contribution_rate_by_district`'s district branch remains unreachable** for every real holder (`reconciliation.review` is pariwar-ceiling only) — recorded at the site and at `deferred-work.md:3447`. Its real numerator and lifecycle-state denominator filter remain an **Epic 8/9 follow-up**, unchanged by this entry.
- **Story 10.28 is the LAST of the three mints** made by Decision `2026-08-12-102` (Epic 3 → Story 1.18 → {1.19, 6.17, 10.28}). With it, the third generation of the Epic-3 geo deferral closes.
- ADR-0038 remains `drafted`, **not ratified** — unchanged by this entry.

### Decision 2026-08-13-104: Story 6.17's nine rulings — the block-dimension ground-inspection gate is a **per-ROW** gate over a **nullable `block` column** (AC1 arm A, on evidence that arm B is structurally impossible), **absence of a geo tree DENIES and never widens**, the catalog version moves while the key count does not, and the inherited governance-boundary obligation is recorded as **EVALUATED AND NON-FIRING**

**Decision type:** Engineering-substrate decision **+** the governance dispositions that accompany it. **Not** a Trustee-Panel matter — no policy, eligibility or cadence commitment moves here ([[feedback_architecture_vs_prd_boundary]]); this rules the mechanism behind Story 1.18's already-committed successor and disposes of one inherited obligation and one deferral.
**Status:** **Author-committed** (BigDev, Solo Builder, 2026-08-13), ruled at Story 6.17 authoring across four passes (D1–D9 and Escalations 1–4 on 2026-08-13). Recorded here **before** implementation ([[feedback_governance_commits_precede_implementation]]).
**Author:** BigDev (Solo Builder)
**Ratifying trustees:** n/a — not a Trustee-Panel matter (see decision type)
**Supersedes:** nothing. Decisions `2026-08-12-102` (which **minted** Story 6.17) and `2026-08-13-103` (whose D1 left the standing obligation this entry disposes of) are **not** edited, re-read or superseded ([[feedback_supersede_never_reinterpret]]); this entry rules the questions Story 6.17's authoring raised.

**Context:**

PRD **FR-40** names *"a block-/district-level admin"* as the ground-inspection actor. Story 6.7 shipped the surface gated at `dimension: 'district'` and granted `claim.conduct_ground_inspection` to `district_admin` **only**; `block_admin` was recorded DEFERRED pending "the Epic-3 geo-tree resolver". Story 1.18 proved that deferral was **misclassified**: both denial paths are resolver-free.

```
GEO_RANK: state 2 < district 3 < block 4        (lower = BROADER)
grant {block,'Block-1'} → gRank 4;  target {district,'Patna'} → tRank 3
scopeContains:  `if (tRank < gRank) return false`  →  3 < 4  →  DENIED, before any resolver runs.
scopeWithinCeiling('district','block')  →  CEILING_RANK 3 >= 4  →  false, no resolver parameter at all.
```

The honest path is therefore a **different GATE**, not a resolver: re-gating at `dimension: 'block'` authorizes **both** actors — `block_admin` by **exact-node** match, and `district_admin` by **district→block ancestry** through the resolver Story 1.18 shipped. A resolver can only ever **narrow**; this is the direction it points.

**Decision:**

1. **D1 — AC1's either/or resolves to ARM A (a `block` column). Arm B is structurally IMPOSSIBLE, not merely undesirable.** `claim_ground_inspections` gains `block text` (migration `0102`); the block value is **supplied at schedule time**, never derived. Three independent lines of evidence, any one sufficient:
   - **The 1.19 primitive cannot supply it, permanently.** `liftDistrictThroughTree` returns `block: geoAbsent('no-member-attribute')` **unconditionally**, with the reason chosen precisely so no future edit can make it look tree-dependent. Decision `2026-08-13-103` D5: *"A posting supplies a **district**; ancestry walks **UP**; `block` sits **BELOW** district. No tree, however complete, can ever populate a member's block."*
   - **No member block attribute exists at any layer.** `member_postings` carries `district text NOT NULL` and nothing else geographic, verified across all migrations through `0101`.
   - ⭐ **Even if it existed it would be the WRONG VALUE.** The assignment's jurisdiction is the **SITE's**, not the member's — `GROUND_INSPECTION_SITE_TYPES` includes `workplace`, `school_or_office` and `incident_location`, and `district` is already the schedule-time D6 **authorization anchor**. Deriving jurisdiction from the deceased member's posting would silently re-anchor a shipped authorization boundary.
   ⛔ **AC1's text is NOT edited** — it asks that the choice be *"recorded, not assumed"*, and this clause is that record.
2. **D2 — `block` is NULLABLE, and the gate dimension is a property of the ROW, not of the route.** `block != null` → the conduct key is checked at `dimension: 'block'` against the row's block. `block == null` → checked at `dimension: 'district'` against the row's district, **byte-identically to today**. Two problems, one answer:
   - **(a) Pre-6.17 rows cannot be backfilled.** A `NOT NULL` add needs a value for every existing row and no honest value exists; inventing one is exactly the reconstruction [[feedback_record_unattested_no_backfill]] forbids. ⇒ nullable, no default, no backfill.
   - **(b) An UNCONDITIONAL block gate REVOKES `district_admin` in every treeless Pariwar.** With `dimension: 'block'` and a `{district,'Patna'}` grant, `scopeContains` falls through to `resolver.contains(...)`; with no published tree the middleware passes **no** resolver and `denyDeeperGeoResolver` denies. ⭐ **There is NO code default geography (ADR-0038)**, so "no tree" is the state every Pariwar starts in and — with no writer surface anywhere in the repo — the state every Pariwar is permanently in today. An unconditional block gate would ship as a **total outage of the 6.7 surface**, which no AC asks for.
   ⛔ **REJECTED — an OR of two checks** (`district`-dim OR `block`-dim). It is strictly wider than either gate, introduces a compound authorization primitive the codebase does not have, and would let a `district_admin` act on a block-tagged row **without** the ancestry AC3 names — delivering AC3's *outcome* while deleting its *mechanism*.
   ⚠ AC2 is **REFINED, not contradicted**: it does not say *"every assignment"*.
3. **D3 — `block` is supplied at schedule time and is IMMUTABLE on reschedule; it mirrors `district` exactly.** ⛔ **No normalization** — no trimming, no case-folding — because `geo-tree/resolver.ts` made that exact commitment for that exact reason, and a module that case-folded while the tree did not would resolve `Bihar ⊇ patna` but not `Patna ⊇ patna` **within one request**. A reschedule must not move the assignment to a different block for the identical reason it must not move districts: the gate resolves from the target row, so allowing it to change would mint a replacement in a node the actor was never checked against (cross-node authz escalation). ⇒ a **SIBLING** `GroundInspectionBlockImmutableError` → `ground_inspection.block_immutable`. ⛔ The existing `ground_inspection.district_immutable` code literal is **not** generalized; its contract stays byte-identical. The idempotency-mismatch discriminator is extended to cover `block`, or a retry with a *different* block silently returns the first assignment.
4. **D4 — the AC5 read route gains an optional `?block=`, with EXACTLY-ONE-OF enforced.** `GET …/ground-inspection` requires `?district=` today and gates on it, which a `block_admin` can never satisfy. ⇒ the read query becomes `{ district?, block? }` with **exactly one** required; the gate resolves dimension from whichever is present and the handler filters on the same field. ⛔ Supplying **both** is a 400, never a silent precedence rule. ⛔ Supplying **neither** is a 400 — it does not degrade into "return everything".
5. **D5 — `PERMISSION_CATALOG_VERSION` 31 → 32; `PERMISSION_CATALOG.keys` stays 41.** This story mints **no key**. It applies the rule Story 10.18 already wrote down: *"the catalog version is the version of the CAPABILITY MODEL, not a count of keys. A consumer caching authorization decisions keyed on `catalogVersion` must see the model move **when a new role can hold an existing key**"* — which is exactly `block_admin` + `claim.conduct_ground_inspection`. ⛔ If the key-count assertion ever moves in this story, a key was minted and the story has exceeded its scope.
6. **D6 — ⛔ NO fallback to the district gate when a tree is absent. Absence must DENY, never widen.** Ruled in BigDev's own words: *"No district fallback when the geo tree is absent. Absence must deny rather than widen authorization."* It will be tempting to write *"if this Pariwar has no tree, gate this block row at district instead"*. ⛔ **That inverts ADR-0038's whole posture** — *a wrong tree silently GRANTS authority; an absent one merely denies*. A grant-on-absence rule makes **the absence of data widen authorization** and makes **publishing a tree narrow it**: a config-shaped privilege switch with no config file. The correct behaviour is that a `district_admin` in a treeless Pariwar **cannot act on a block-tagged assignment**, gets a clean structured 403, and simply omits `block` (D2's null path) until their Pariwar publishes a tree.
   ⛔ **THE POLARITY SHIPS AS TWO PAIRED, NEVER-SKIPPABLE INTEGRATION ASSERTIONS — the D6 polarity pair — not as a comment.** A comment is not the control. **(a)** *block assignment + NO resolvable tree → access DENIED* (non-NULL block, **no tree at all**, actor = a `district_admin` who **would** pass under the legacy path so the tree's absence is the sole cause; a clean structured 403 with an `authz.denied` emission — ⛔ not a 404, a 500 or a validation error, because a crash that happens to block access is not a deny). **(b)** *district assignment + existing district path → behaviour UNCHANGED* (`block = NULL`, no tree, right district → 200, wrong district → 403). ⭐ The pair is non-negotiable **as a pair**: (a) alone passes on a system that denies everything; (b) alone passes on a system that has silently re-widened. A **fallback probe** (temporarily insert the forbidden fallback; (a) must go RED while (b) stays GREEN) is the evidence the polarity is *enforced* rather than *described* ([[feedback_gate_scope_semantic_coverage]]).
7. **D7 — the stale Family-B line in `rbac/scope.ts` is CORRECTED, comment-only.** §RANK-ORDER's Family-B list still reads *"…(`state`→`district`, `block`→`district` ancestry). Those are real deferrals."* A `block` grant reaching a `district` target is the target being **BROADER** — the exact inverted premise the twelve lines above it exist to correct, and which Decision `2026-08-12-102` §5 ruled on and rewrote at `permissions.ts` and `roles.ts` but which survived here. Corrected to `district`→`block` (the ancestry **this story** uses), citing Story 6.17. ⛔ **Comment-only**: the `scope.ts` diff must contain **zero non-comment lines** (AC5 / architectural freeze row 9), proven with `git diff` and recorded in the Dev Agent Record. ⛔ The alternative — leave it byte-unchanged and record it in `deferred-work.md` — is **NOT taken**: a stale line that is neither corrected nor recorded is the failure mode this marker discipline exists to prevent.
8. **D8 — `claim.override_ground_inspection` is NOT granted to `block_admin`.** The D6 supervisor override is a **pariwar-ceiling** authority *above* the inspector. Re-gating the **conduct** key says nothing about who may act on an assignment they are not the inspector of. A block admin therefore acts **only** as the assigned inspector, and the in-handler inspector-identity guard is unchanged. Recorded so a later reader does not read the omission as an oversight.
9. **D9 — the inherited governance-boundary obligation (Decision `2026-08-13-103`, D1) is EVALUATED AND DOES NOT FIRE, and the NON-FIRING is recorded in both directions.** D1 takes arm A ⇒ **no authorization or routing consumer of `resolveMemberGeoNode` is created by this story** ⇒ `packages/domain/src/member-geo` is **NOT** admitted to `governance_boundary.yaml`'s `prohibited` list. ⛔ **The yaml is not edited** — no root added, no `count` touched.
   ⛔ **"Does not bind" is NOT "nothing to do".** The re-trigger comment in `member-geo/index.ts` names **Story 6.17 by number**; left standing after this story ships it reads as **pending forever** — precisely the failure mode [[project_r7_fact_producer_unbuilt]] names. The bullet is therefore **rewritten to record that the trigger evaluated and did not fire**, with the reason (arm A), leaving **Story 10.4** as the **sole standing trigger**. ⛔ The bullet is **not deleted**: a deleted trigger is indistinguishable from a forgotten one.
   ⚠ **A passing `governance-boundary` run proves NOTHING here.** The root is unlisted, so the scan was always going to be green (`governance_boundary.yaml`'s own README caveat; [[feedback_gate_scope_semantic_coverage]]).
10. **Escalation 1 — ABSORBED (the admin UI ships inside this story).** `apps/admin/src/modules/ground-inspection/GroundInspectionPage.tsx` is the only human path to this surface and hardcodes district in three places. Shipping the gate without the UI would leave `block_admin` holding a capability with **no way to exercise it** — the exact inert-grant failure this story exists to end, moved one layer up. Absorbed **minimally**: an optional `Block` input on both forms, `block` on the client's assignment + schedule shapes, `block` surfaced on the assignment row, and the read call passing whichever locator the operator supplied. ⛔ **No redesign.** ⚠ `apps/admin/src/**` is inside the microcopy gate's `code_globs`, so new strings go through the module-local `i18n-en.ts` and any colour comes from `@twt/tokens`, never a hex literal.
11. **Escalation 2 — ABSORBED: AC3's ancestry path is DECLARED, NOT PRODUCTION-ACTIVE, and that is recorded rather than papered over.** `geo_tree_versions` has a domain writer (`geoTree.createGeoTreeVersion`) and **no route** — Decision `2026-08-12-102` §7: *"No writer surface ships with Story 1.18."* Verified at `ba52ba0`: no `apps/api` route references `geoTree` beyond the per-request loader. ⇒ AC3 is **provable by test** and **unreachable by a real operator**, so this story ships `district_admin`-on-block-rows as a **capability, not a live behaviour** — the `wa_cost_optimization` / `kyc_provider_selection` *"DECLARED, NOT PRODUCTION-ACTIVE"* posture. ⛔ Do not claim AC3 is "live". **Re-trigger: the first surface that publishes a geo tree.** ⛔ No story is minted from inside this one — mints are governance acts taken at their owning story.
12. **Escalation 3 — ABSORBED: `block_admin` gains NO other surface access, and the boundary is recorded.** `block_admin`'s bundle is `[MEMBER_SUSPEND, MEMBER_VIEW_VALIDITY]`. It holds neither `claim.verify` (the 6.10 console) nor `claim.approve`. After this story it can schedule and complete an inspection **it was given the id of**, and cannot browse claims to find one. FR-40 asks for the *inspection* actor, not a claim-reading one, and widening a role's read surface is a separate capability decision. ⛔ Do not opportunistically add `claim.verify` to `block_admin` — a different gate, a different dimension (district), and denied anyway.
13. **Escalation 4 — ⏸ DEFERRED with a re-trigger, recorded verbatim as ruled:**
    > **The verifier-console contract remains district-only for this story. The first block-tagged assignment reaching the verifier console re-triggers Story 6.10's surface for a reviewed contract widening.**

    `packages/contracts/src/claims/verifier-console.ts` types each ground-inspection entry as `{ groundInspectionId, district, … }`. Adding `block` to the row breaks nothing — but after this story a verifier reading the console sees a **district-only** description of an assignment whose actual jurisdiction is a block. That is an incomplete signal on the surface FR-40 exists to feed. ⛔ **NOT absorbed**: widening a `@twt/contracts` read shape is Story 6.10's surface and a separate reviewed change; taking it here would grow this story from *"re-gate one action"* into *"re-shape the verifier console"*. ⛔ Do **not** quietly add the field "while we are here". ⛔ The deferral is **recorded, not merely intended** — it is written into `deferred-work.md` **and** into this entry, because a deferral that lives only in a story file decays ([[feedback_mechanization_split_commitment]]). ⛔ The re-trigger names a **STORY** (6.10), never an epic ([[project_r7_fact_producer_unbuilt]]).

**Open follow-ups:**

- ⭐ **STANDING (inherited from Decision `2026-08-13-103` D1), now with ONE trigger instead of two:** the first authorization or routing consumer of `resolveMemberGeoNode` must re-assess `packages/domain/src/member-geo` for admission to `governance_boundary.yaml`'s `prohibited` list. **Story 6.17's half is DISCHARGED — evaluated, did not fire (D9).** **Story 10.4** (`helpdesk_tickets.member_scope_context` geo enrichment) remains the **sole standing trigger**, mechanized in its own `epics.md` section and in `member-geo/index.ts`.
- **AC3's ancestry path is DECLARED, NOT PRODUCTION-ACTIVE.** Re-trigger: *the first surface that publishes a geo tree* (no writer surface exists anywhere in the repo). ⛔ Do not mint that story from here.
- **Escalation 4 — the verifier-console contract stays district-only.** Re-trigger: *the first block-tagged assignment reaching the verifier console* → **Story 6.10**. Also recorded in `deferred-work.md`.
- **`field_worker` ground inspection** remains deferred to **Epic 12** (its `self` scopeCeiling + the dispatch/assignment substrate) — unchanged by this entry and not asserted otherwise.
- ADR-0038 remains `drafted`, **not ratified** — unchanged by this entry.

**References:**
- `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/6-17-block-dimension-ground-inspection-gate.md` — the story file (D1–D9, Escalations 1–4, the Three Traps)
- `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/epics.md:2707-2765` — Story 6.17's five minted ACs + the inherited obligation
- `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/prds/prd-TWT-2026-05-22/prd.md:685-687` — FR-40
- `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/epics.md:527` — architectural freeze row 9 (RBAC key + scope-dimension model)

- Decision `2026-08-13-103` — D1's non-admission + MECHANIZED re-trigger; D5's permanent `block` absence (not edited by this entry)

- `packages/domain/src/member-geo/{index,resolve}.ts` — the re-trigger comment + the permanent `block` absence
