### Decision 2026-08-13-103: Story 1.19's eight rulings — member→geo attribution ships as its OWN `member-geo` module **deliberately NOT admitted** to the prohibited roots (with a MECHANIZED re-trigger), the quiet-turn-on hazard is discharged **by evidence** rather than by migration, and `role`/`cohort` are recorded **"Not addressed"** with **no successor minted**

**Decision type:** Engineering-substrate decision **+** the governance dispositions that accompany it. **Not** a Trustee-Panel matter — no policy, eligibility or cadence commitment moves here ([[feedback_architecture_vs_prd_boundary]]); this rules the mechanism behind Story 1.18's already-committed successor and assigns owners to what remains.
**Status:** **Author-committed** (BigDev, Solo Builder, 2026-08-13), ruled at Story 1.19 authoring across four passes (D1–D7 on 2026-08-13, D8 on 2026-08-13 after a readiness check). Recorded here **before** implementation ([[feedback_governance_commits_precede_implementation]]).
**Author:** BigDev (Solo Builder)
**Ratifying trustees:** n/a — not a Trustee-Panel matter (see decision type)
**Supersedes:** nothing. Decision `2026-08-12-102` **minted** Story 1.19 and is **not** edited, re-read or superseded ([[feedback_supersede_never_reinterpret]]); this entry rules the questions that story's authoring raised.

**Context:**

Story 1.18 shipped the geo-tree scope resolver and deliberately lit up **none** of the six geo-audience markers it re-pointed (D6, D7, D8, D14, D15, D16). The reason is a capability distinction, not an oversight: *a tree answers "is Patna in Bihar"; it never answers "which members are in Patna."* Audience **selection** and authorization **containment** merely share the word "geo". Story 1.19 supplies the missing member→district read and wires one consumer family end-to-end.

Authoring raised eight decisions and three escalations. All eight are ruled below; Escalations 1 and 2 are **absorbed** into the story; Escalation 3 is informational (use re-derived current-head anchors; ⛔ minted AC text is **not** edited to fix a drifted line number).

**Decision:**

1. **D1 — the module's home, and its DELIBERATE non-admission to the prohibited roots.** `resolveMemberGeoNode` ships in a **new** `packages/domain/src/member-geo/`, exported as the `memberGeo` namespace. It is **deliberately NOT added** to `governance_boundary.yaml`'s `prohibited` list.
   - It **cannot** live in `packages/domain/src/geo-tree/`: that root **is** prohibited (`:302-305`, prohibition (d) — *"the resolver's answers ARE authorization decisions"*). A member-attribution read is **not** an authorization decision today, and folding it under a prohibition it does not earn makes the prohibition **mean less, not more**.
   - It **cannot** live in `rbac/` (`:238`), which deliberately touches no DB.
   - ⚠ **The non-admission is RECORDED, not left to a passing scan.** *A green scan over an UNLISTED root proves the root is unlisted, not that the behaviour is admissible* — the gate's own README (`:169-174`), and the exact lesson Story 1.18's AC7 paid for.
   - **Re-trigger, retained verbatim:** *the first authorization or routing consumer of `resolveMemberGeoNode` requires reassessment* — specifically Story 10.4's `member_scope_context` geo enrichment or Story 6.17's block gate. At that moment a flag that could weaken the read becomes a config-shaped privilege switch, and the root must be admitted.
2. **D1-M — the re-trigger is MECHANIZED, not merely stated.** Unlike D2 (a one-time act discharged at Task 0), D1 leaves a **standing** obligation that fires in a story nobody has scheduled. An obligation recorded only here and in the story file decays exactly like an epic-shaped deferral ([[project_r7_fact_producer_unbuilt]]; [[feedback_mechanization_split_commitment]] — *decay concentrates in the un-mechanized half*). So the obligation is written into **both successors' OWN `epics.md` sections** — the fix Story 1.18's D9-R applied to Story 10.13. ⚠ The two are **not symmetric**: Story 6.17's AC1 already names 1.19's primitive but carries no governance-boundary half, and its AC1 offers a **genuine either/or**, so the obligation binds **only** on the derive-via-1.19 arm; Story 10.4 mentions the geo obligation **nowhere**, existing solely as a code comment. ⛔ Neither successor's **acceptance criteria** are edited — minting scope into another story's ACs from outside is not this story's to do.
3. **D2 — the quiet-turn-on hazard is discharged BY EVIDENCE.** Existing `state`-scoped banners **retain their authored status** (option (a)) and become targetable when the arm ships — **but only after a mandatory pre-flight disposition query is run and its result recorded**, per environment. ⛔ Non-zero live/scheduled rows ⇒ **STOP and escalate**. ⛔ The state machine is **never** bypassed by a migration: `published → draft` is not a legal transition (`banners/status.ts:18-19`) and `retracted` is terminal (`schema/banners.ts:91`), so options (b) and (c) are **unauthorized** without a fresh ruling. *Record what is actually true; do not manufacture a mechanism for an empty population* ([[feedback_record_unattested_no_backfill]]). ⛔ "Probably zero" is not a disposition.
4. **D3 — `created_at DESC, posting_id DESC`.** The repo's two newest-posting readers **disagree**: `member/posting.ts:117-129` orders by `created_at` alone (ties nondeterministic); `claim/peer-mesh-read.ts:83-88`/`:122-128` adds the `posting_id` tie-break. Adopt the **peer-mesh** form — two rows can share `created_at` (same-transaction inserts, `defaultNow()` resolution), and a nondeterministic audience is a nondeterministic *test*. This **refines** AC1's prose rather than contradicting it. ⛔ `getMemberPostingLatest` is **not** "fixed" here — it serves Story 3.9's panel summary and Epic 4's retirement anchor, a different blast radius. The divergence is **commented at both sites** so the next reader sees it as deliberate.
5. **D4 — resolved-geo injection; the predicate stays SYNCHRONOUS and PURE.** `isMemberInBannerAudience` is a pure sync predicate called inside a `.filter()` (`banners/read.ts:188`). AC3's *"signature grows a member argument"* must **not** be read as *"grows a `Db` argument"*: that would make the filter async and issue **one query per candidate banner** — the very N+1 AC7 forbids in the *other* consumer. `listMemberBannerCandidates` resolves the member's geo **ONCE**, before filtering, and passes the resolved value in. ⛔ Never load geo inside the filter; ⛔ never make the predicate async.
6. **D5 — `block` is PERMANENTLY typed-absent, with its own `reason`.** A posting supplies a **district**; ancestry walks **UP**; `block` sits **BELOW** district. **No tree, however complete, can ever populate a member's block** — the resolver only walks `descendant → ancestor` (`geo-tree/resolver.ts:152-157`). So `block` ships unconditionally `{available: false, reason: 'no-member-attribute'}`, with a reason **distinct** from the tree-shaped absences: collapsing it into `'not-in-tree'` would tell a future reader that a richer tree lights it up. It does not. Only a new member attribute would.
7. **D6 — a CLOSED five-value `reason` union.** Exactly `no-posting-row`, `no-tree-published`, `node-not-in-tree`, `no-ancestor-at-dimension`, `no-member-attribute` — no additions without a fresh ruling, so consumers can branch and logs stay greppable. ⛔ A free-text `reason` is rejected: the signal lives in a **closed vocabulary**, not prose ([[project_anonymous_diagnostic_log_convention]]).
8. **D7 — the peer-mesh correlated-scalar-subquery with LITERAL outer qualifiers.** ⛔ **Not** a freshly-invented `DISTINCT ON`. The peer-mesh form is **proven against the exact bug class**: interpolating a Drizzle `Column` into a subquery over a table with a same-named column collapses the correlation into a tautology (`peer-mesh-read.ts:60-73`) — a live ~30–40% wrong-district bug, invisible to DB-free tests ([[project_epic6_drizzle_correlated_subquery_bug]]). `DISTINCT ON` additionally carries the **42P10** trap. The district set is computed **in memory** from the in-force tree document (bounded by `MAX_NODES = 5000`) and passed as one `IN`-list — ⭐ the tree is a **JSONB document**, not a table; there is nothing to join *to* in SQL.
9. **D8 — `role`/`cohort` are recorded "Not addressed", and ⛔ NO successor is minted.** AC4 requires the seam prose to *"name a **story** and never an epic"*. **No such story exists**, and this was **verified** at `9fa4e31`, not assumed: `members` carries lifecycle `state` + `pariwar_id` only. ⛔ **Story 10.8 is not the owner** — it is `done`, and its "cohort" is a *flag-targeting* tag (`epics.md:115`, FR-58C), so pointing a re-deferral at it would be **worse than pointing at an epic**: it reads as **already-delivered**. ⛔ **Story 12.2 is not the owner** either — it *consumes* a role/cohort filter but does not **own** the attribute. So the third label of [[feedback_closure_language_precision]] is used **honestly**, with a concrete re-trigger: *the first surface that must target members by `role` or `cohort`*. No FR sits behind the attribute and there is no live or backlog consumer, so an owner minted here would be precisely the un-gated re-commitment [[feedback_record_unattested_no_backfill]] warns decays — the same disposition Story 1.18 gave `deferred-work.md:1091`.
   ⚠ **AC4 OVER-SPECIFIED**: it assumed a story existed to name. Every **other** obligation in AC4 is delivered in full (resolve `false`/`[]`, log the seam note, state the *"no attribute exists at all"* distinction explicitly). ⛔ **AC4's text is NOT edited** ([[feedback_supersede_never_reinterpret]]); the disposition is recorded beside it and the AC stands as minted.
10. **Escalation 1 — ABSORBED (recorded as AC6-E, a ruled ADDITION, never an in-place re-reading).** News-blog dispatch is one-shot at publish, so already-`published` `state` posts are safe **by evidence**. But `scheduled` is a **stored status** (`schema/news_posts.ts:56`) and `runNewsPublish` transitions `scheduled → published` and *then* fans out — so a post scheduled today with `audience_scope='state'` **will dispatch for real** after this story lands. D2's pre-flight therefore **extends** to `news_posts` in `draft`/`submitted`/`approved`/`scheduled`, under the identical stop-and-escalate gate. ⛔ No migration, no status rewrite.
11. **Escalation 2 — ABSORBED.** `BANNER_TARGETABLE_AUDIENCE_SCOPES` has **three** consumers, not two: the domain authority (`banners/audience.ts:91`), the browser mirror (`contracts/src/banners/enums.ts:63`, pinned by an **order-sensitive** `toEqual` sync-guard) and — the one that is not an indicator — the **AC5 visibility verdict** at `apps/admin/src/modules/banners/derive.ts:171`. Adding `'state'` therefore changes what the console tells an author about whether their draft would be **seen**. That is correct behaviour, **but it must be asserted, not discovered**. ⛔ Both lists gain `'state'` in the **same position** or the sync-guard fails on ordering.

**Discharge record (2026-08-13, this story's Task 0 — recorded because a zero result discharges nothing unless it is written down):**

Both mandatory pre-flight queries returned **zero rows**:

```
-- D2, over `banners`                      → (0 rows)
-- AC6-E / Escalation 1, over `news_posts` → (0 rows)
```

⚠ **Environment scope, stated honestly.** The **local dev/test database is the only environment that exists**: `infra/gcp/` authors dev/staging/prod Cloud SQL, but the Terraform has **never been applied** — no `.tfstate`, **no remote backend block**, no real `terraform.tfvars`, and no `gcloud` on the workstation. This was **verified live**, not assumed ([[feedback_verify_before_committing_governance_claims]]). There is therefore no other environment against which the query could return a different answer. **The D2 / AC6-E hazard is discharged by evidence, and option (a) applies: existing rows retain their authored status.**

**Open follow-ups:**

- ⭐ **STANDING (D1):** the **first authorization or routing consumer** of `resolveMemberGeoNode` must re-assess `packages/domain/src/member-geo` for admission to `governance_boundary.yaml`'s `prohibited` list. Mechanized into **Story 6.17** and **Story 10.4**'s own `epics.md` sections — ⛔ do not rely on this entry alone.
- **`role`/`cohort` member attribute — "Not addressed", no owner.** Re-trigger: *the first surface that must target members by `role` or `cohort`*. ⛔ Do not mint a successor before a live requirement attaches.
- **Story 12.2** (`epics.md:4569`) inherits this primitive as a downstream consumer of its scope filter. Recorded in 12.2's own section; **no code is written for it by Story 1.19**.
- ADR-0038 remains `drafted`, **not ratified** — unchanged by this entry and not asserted otherwise.

**References:**
- `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/1-19-member-geo-attribution-geo-audience-consumer.md` — the story file (D1–D8, Escalations 1–3, AC6-E)
- Decision `2026-08-12-102` — Story 1.18's mint of Story 1.19 (not edited by this entry)
- `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/epics.md:1435-1491` — Story 1.19's eight minted ACs
- `docs/adr/ADR-0038-geo-tree-scope-resolver.md` — no code default geography (`drafted`)
- `governance_boundary.yaml:232-305` — the prohibited roots + the gate's own README caveat (`:169-174`)

### Decision 2026-08-12-102: The geo-tree scope resolver's source of truth — a versioned per-Pariwar registry table holding a JSONB tree document, with **NO code-resident default** (ADR-0038); plus the disposition of every Family-B re-deferral, including **three named-story mints** and **two deliberate non-mints**

**Decision type:** Engineering-substrate decision (ADR-0038 authoring) **+** the governance dispositions that accompany it. **Not** a Trustee-Panel matter — the RBAC containment property was ratified at ADR-0008; this selects the mechanism behind an already-committed seam ([[feedback_architecture_vs_adr_boundary]]) and assigns owners to what remains.
**Status:** **Author-committed** (BigDev, Solo Builder, 2026-08-12), at Story 1.18 authoring. ⚠ ADR-0038 ships `drafted`, **NOT ratified** — trustee ratification is a named forward obligation this story may not assert ([[feedback_verify_before_committing_governance_claims]]).
**Supersedes:** nothing. ⛔ **ADR-0008 is `ratified` and is NOT edited, re-read, or superseded** ([[feedback_supersede_never_reinterpret]]). ADR-0038 **discharges** ADR-0008 Decision 4's deferred geo-tree seam and cross-references it; the ratified text stands exactly as ratified.

#### 1. The decision itself

**The organizational tree is a versioned per-Pariwar registry table (`geo_tree_versions`) holding one JSONB tree document per version row, with NO code-resident default.** A Pariwar with no published row has **no tree**: the loader returns `null`, the caller passes no resolver, and `denyDeeperGeoResolver` applies. **Today's behaviour, byte-identical, by construction.**

The posture is `helpdesk_routing_policy_versions`' (`packages/domain/src/helpdesk/registry.ts:1-19`), itself the `clause_versions` posture: append a version row, never mutate a prior one except its forward pointer; `(pariwar_id, version)` unambiguous; one in-force version; a typed conflict error. ⭐ **The one deliberate divergence from that precedent is the absence of a code default** — routing policy ships `DEFAULT_ROUTING_POLICY`; geography ships nothing.

#### 2. Why the two rejected options were rejected — on evidence, which AC1 required

- **A projection over `member_postings` is IMPOSSIBLE, not merely undesirable.** The table carries `district text NOT NULL` and nothing else geographic (`schema/member_postings.ts:51`). **There is no `state` column and no `block` column anywhere in the schema** (verified across all migrations applied through `0100`). `helpdesk_tickets`' `MemberScopeContextSnapshot` (`:83-89`) *declares* `state`/`district`/`block` and its only producer leaves **all three null** (`apps/api/src/modules/helpdesk/handlers.ts:10-15`) — a declared shape with no data behind it. A projection can enumerate the **set** of district values in use; it can supply **zero edges**. ⭐ **Ancestry cannot be projected from data that records no parent.**
- **A code-resident seeded geography is REJECTED on the asymmetry of failure modes.** There is no universally-correct Indian state→district→block tree, and the tenant model puts each Pariwar *above* its geography. **A wrong tree silently GRANTS authority — no error, no log, no denial to notice. An absent tree merely denies, visibly.** Fail-closed must remain the resting state, so the one option that can make the system *less* safe than shipping nothing is the one that gets rejected.
- A row-per-node `geo_nodes` table was rejected on **cost, not correctness**: it forces an N-query walk or a recursive CTE per request, against the purity constraint below. Revisit trigger recorded in ADR-0038.

#### 3. Purity is the binding constraint, and it is inherited from ADR-0008

`hasPermission` is a **PURE, side-effect-free predicate** (ADR-0008 Decision 8; `check.ts:9-11`) and `GeoTreeResolver.contains` is **synchronous by interface** (`scope.ts:161-163`). The resolver therefore **cannot query**. The tree loads **once per request**, beside `request.scopeGrants` in the scope-resolution middleware, and is closed over. ⛔ Making `contains` async would change the seam interface — **architectural freeze row 9** — and is out of bounds.

#### 4. ⛔ What this decision explicitly does NOT do

- It does **not** touch `GEO_RANK`, `CEILING_RANK`, `scopeContains`'s ordering, the `GeoTreeResolver` interface, or the permission-key / scope-dimension model (**freeze row 9**), and does **not** re-rank `pariwar` vs `state` — which would silently re-authorize every grant in the system.
- It does **not** discharge the **rank-order** (Family A) denials. A resolver answers *"is X beneath Y"* and can only ever narrow; a narrower grant authorizing a broader target is the ordering working correctly. See `scope.ts` §RANK-ORDER.
- It does **not** place `self` in the geo tree (`scope.ts:50-55`; `GeoNode` excludes it **by type**, `:149`).
- It does **not** supply member→geo attribution ("which members are in Patna"). That is **Story 1.19**.

#### 5. ⭐ A correction to Story 10.18's own Family-B table, raised rather than silently applied

Story 10.18 placed **P1** (`permissions.ts:122`) and **R11** (`roles.ts:414`) in Family B, on the reasoning that *"`block`→parent-`district` is same-tree ancestry with the target strictly narrower."* **The target is not narrower — it is the parent, hence broader.** Traced at `63c844e`: grant `{block,'Block-1'}` → `gRank 4`; target `{district,'Patna'}` → `tRank 3`; `scope.ts:232` (`tRank < gRank`) denies at `3 < 4`, **before the resolver is reached**. The alternative — a district-scoped grant for `block_admin` — fails the other line: `scopeWithinCeiling('district','block')` → `3 >= 4` → false. **Both denial paths are resolver-free.**

This is **Family A wearing a Family-B label**, and re-pointing it to Story 1.18 preserved the very error Story 10.18 existed to remove, one epic later under a fresher date. **RULED (BigDev, 2026-08-12):** P1 and R11 are **rewritten in place** as rank-order, citing §RANK-ORDER — the same treatment Story 10.18 gave the other 24. The `check.test.ts:184` assertion **stands unchanged**; its *comment* is corrected, because it repeats the misdiagnosis. The ACCEPTANCE CONDITION prose in both files is corrected too: it promises `block_admin` support *"when the authorization layer can resolve a block grant through verified block→district ancestry"*, and **that is not a thing this model can do.**

The honest path for FR-40's block-level actor is **a different gate, not a resolver** — re-gating at `dimension: 'block'`, which authorizes `block_admin` by exact-node **and** `district_admin` by district→block ancestry through this story's resolver. That is a schema + gate-design change, and it is **Story 6.17**.

#### 6. The disposition of every re-deferral — three mints, two deliberate non-mints

Committed governance-first, before any marker points at them ([[feedback_governance_commits_precede_implementation]]), each existing in `sprint-status.yaml` with acceptance criteria in `epics.md`. ⛔ **Every successor is a STORY. Never an epic** — [[project_r7_fact_producer_unbuilt]]: *a deferral naming an EPIC expires unowned*, which is precisely what D1-1.8 did for seven epics.

| Re-deferral | Model it extends | Owner | Action |
|---|---|---|---|
| Geo **audience** selection (6 markers) | member→geo attribution over this tree | **Story 1.19** (Epic 1, 8 ACs) | **MINTED** |
| Block-dimension ground-inspection gate | Story 6.7's gate (FR-40) | **Story 6.17** (Epic 6, 5 ACs) | **MINTED** |
| Multi-node report scope | Story 10.7's `ResolvedReportScope` | **Story 10.28** (Epic 10, 5 ACs) | **MINTED — unconditional** |
| Trustee-**directory** half of `pool/fixed-amount.ts:78` | Story 7.5's attesting panel | **Story 10.13** *(exists)* | **RE-POINTED, no mint** |
| `self` targets (`scope.ts:218`) | *(nothing — no consumer exists)* | **none** | **CLOSED BY EDIT** |

**Three and not one, deliberately.** The re-deferrals share **no owning model**; bundling four unrelated capabilities into a single Epic-1 story would have recreated the epic-shaped bucket this correct-course exists to abolish, in story clothing.

**S4 is *"Closed by [edit]"*, and the label matters** ([[feedback_closure_language_precision]] — never collapsed). The repo's **only** live `dimension: 'self'` check (`apps/api/src/modules/member-validity/handlers.ts:132`) passes **`grants: []` with `isSelf: true`** and bypasses the grant path entirely, so the deferred branch has **zero live consumers, zero backlog consumers, and no FR**. The comment was a **misdescription of a deliberate design choice**, not a deferral. ⛔ The deny does not change. Minting an owner for work nobody has asked for manufactures the un-gated re-commitment [[feedback_record_unattested_no_backfill]] warns decays.

**Story 10.28's existence is UNCONDITIONAL.** Multi-node scope (grants at *several* districts) is orthogonal to ancestry (reaching districts *beneath* a state). A permanent owner born already-discharged is a contradiction, so **Story 1.18 dispositions it and never builds it**, even if it looks cheap after the ancestry work. What Story 1.18 *does* owe: `reports/templates/_shared.ts:40`'s `deny` branch is genuinely reached differently once ancestry is live, so it is re-examined and re-pinned here; **cardinality is 10.28's alone.**

#### 7. Recorded, not resolved

- **Third generation.** Epic 3 → Story 1.18 → {1.19, 6.17, 10.28}. The difference meant to break the cycle is that all three carry acceptance criteria and a named owner. **Three owners is also three chances to repeat the failure.**
- **Publishing a tree widens authorization.** A Pariwar publishing `Patna ∈ Bihar` thereby lets every `state=Bihar` grant reach Patna-scoped targets. That is the intended capability, and the reason the tree is *declared* per tenant rather than *assumed*. **No writer surface ships with Story 1.18** — the substrate, the loader, and the resolver do.
- **Validation cannot catch a factually wrong edge.** Cycles, rank inversions and duplicate siblings are rejected; `Patna ∈ Kerala` is structurally valid and would be accepted. Accepted risk, mitigated only by publication being explicit, versioned and append-only.

**Disposition:** ADR-0038 authored `drafted`; `adr-index.md` Section A row added; the three successors minted in the preceding `governance:` commit; the marker sweep and the code follow in `story(1.18):` commits. **Trustee ratification of ADR-0038 is NOT asserted here.**

### Decision 2026-08-12-101: Two record corrections to Decision `2026-08-12-099` — clause 8.1's "CONFIRMED by the Trustee Panel" claim is CORRECTED (the brief-principle-3 reading was always author-committed reasoning), and the missing `APPENDIX A — RULE INDEX` disposition is recorded

**Decision type:** Two record corrections to Decision `2026-08-12-099`, both caught by the same code-review pass (`bmad-code-review` chunk 5): a status-claim correction (main body below) and a missing disposition, filled by the Addendum.
**Status:** **Author-committed** (record correction). **Amends no Trustee-ratified text** — Decision `099`'s Trustee-ratified clauses 1–7 stand exactly as ratified; this corrects a status CLAIM inside clause 8, which Decision `099`'s own Status line already places under "Author-committed... neither is presented as ratified," and separately fills a disposition Decision `099` omitted (the Addendum) — neither touches ratified content.
**Supersedes:** the sentence *"✅ CONFIRMED by the Trustee Panel, 2026-08-12: brief principle 3 (Proportionality) STANDS as §8.6 clause 3. The reading above is ratified, not merely author-committed, and this sub-clause is closed — no superseding decision is owed on it"* in Decision `2026-08-12-099`, Clause 8, sub-item 1. The Addendum supersedes nothing — it fills a gap Decision `099` left unaddressed.
⛔ **Decision `099`'s text is NOT edited** — it remains the record exactly as committed ([[feedback_supersede_never_reinterpret]]: a status correction is not a backfill, and the historical payload stays legible under the status in force when it was written). This entry is the correction of record going forward.

**What was wrong.** Clause 8.1's reconciliation of §8.6 to seven principles — mapping the ruling's seven summary bullets onto brief principles 1–7 and establishing that brief principle 3 (*Proportionality*) is bullet-absent but still one of the ratified seven — is sound reasoning, and the commit that introduced it (`f92699c`) frames it correctly: *"NO PRINCIPLE WAS ADDED, REMOVED OR REWORDED. This needed no new Panel ruling because it changes no substance."* But the decision-log prose drafted for it went further, adding a claim of Trustee Panel confirmation — *"✅ CONFIRMED by the Trustee Panel, 2026-08-12... ratified, not merely author-committed... closed"* — with no corresponding Panel session anywhere in the routing note (`_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/trustee-panel-routing-note-2026-08-11-story-10-20.md`) or elsewhere in the record. This directly contradicted Decision `099`'s own Status line. Caught by `bmad-code-review`'s governance-chunk pass (two independent review layers), verified against the commit history and the routing note before this correction was filed.

**Correction.** Clause 8.1's REASONING stands, unchanged in substance: brief principle 3 IS §8.6 clause 3, and the presentational fix (renumbering the appeal gap out of the seven, correcting the "eight clauses vs. seven adopted" mismatch) needed no new Panel ruling — the Panel was never asked to re-decide anything, and nothing in §8.6's ratified text changed. What is corrected is the STATUS CLAIM: this is author-committed reasoning, exactly as Decision `099`'s Status line always said clause 8 was. No Panel session ratified it on 2026-08-12 or any other date. **⛔ The one thing this story must not do: manufacture a ratification** — the corrected reading of Decision `099` clause 8.1 is: the mapping and its four-part justification (a)–(d) are author-committed; there is no Panel confirmation to cite, and none is needed for a reading that changes no substance.

**Disposition:** CLOSED as a record correction. No code change — nothing in the shipped §8.6 text changes; it was already correct. If the Panel later wishes to formally ratify this specific reading (rather than have it stand as author-committed reasoning), that requires an actual routed question and a fresh ruling, not a retroactive relabeling.

#### Addendum — Decision `2026-08-12-099`'s missing `APPENDIX A — RULE INDEX` disposition, recorded here `[Author-committed]`

Also caught by the same review pass: Decision `2026-08-12-099` never states whether an `APPENDIX A — RULE INDEX` entry is added for §8.5/§8.6/§8.9, unlike every comparable precedent in this log (Decision `2026-08-10-097` clause 10, Decision `2026-08-10-096`, Decision `2026-08-06-080`), each of which explicitly records the disposition so a later reader does not read the absence as an omission. The disposition, by the same precedent: **no `APPENDIX A — RULE INDEX` entry is added, in either locale.** §8.5 is a body of grounds and §8.6 a body of principles — neither introduces an indexable `R`-rule (the `R7(A)–(G)` contribution-discipline family is a distinct vocabulary). §8.9's future-governance test is likewise a procedural clause, not an `R`-rule. Part 8's existing rule references (`R56 / R6`) are unchanged. Recorded here, under the same "decisions are never edited in place" discipline, rather than inserted into Decision `099`'s original text.

### Decision 2026-08-12-100: §8.4a's mechanization disclosure is UPDATED — the escalation-justification row flips to *mechanized*, and the notice row to *mechanized to the extent of the dwell only*; both locales reproduced verbatim, because `docs/legal/` is untracked and this entry is its only durable copy

**Decision type:** Disclosure update to Niyamavali §8.4a, made under the standing rule §8.4a itself states and Decision `2026-08-12-099` clause 10 records — *a row is moved out of the stated-but-unmechanized list ONLY once the enforcing mechanism and its test are in place.*
**Status:** **Author-committed.** ⛔ **This is NOT a new Trustee ruling and does not present itself as one.** The Panel ratified the RULES in Decision `2026-08-12-099`; what this entry records is the factual observation that two of them are now enforced, plus the resulting edit to a disclosure whose whole purpose is to state what the system actually does. No principle, ground or sanction changes.
**Supersedes:** nothing. ⛔ Decision `2026-08-12-099`'s reproduction of §8.4a is **NOT edited** — it remains the correct record of the block as it stood when that decision was taken, under the mechanization state then in force ([[feedback_supersede_never_reinterpret]]: a policy change is never a backfill, and a historical payload stays correct under the policy in force when it was written). This entry records the block as it stands NOW.

**Why this entry exists at all.** `docs/legal/` is gitignored — the amendment leaves no diff and no blame. The verbatim reproduction below **is** the record.

#### Clause 1 — What changed, and on what evidence [Author-committed]

Two rows moved out of the stated-but-unmechanized list. Each rides its own evidence:

- **Escalation justification → mechanized.** A termination cannot be recorded without both parts of
  the two-part justification. Enforced in three layers: the record's own shape (a separate column per
  part, plus a database constraint making presence structural on every write path including raw SQL),
  a typed refusal naming which part failed and why, and the authoring surface, which renders two
  controls with no copy-across affordance. A restatement of part (b) offered as part (a) is refused.
- **Notice + opportunity to respond → mechanized TO THE EXTENT OF THE DWELL ONLY.** The interval
  between suspension and termination is enforced and its duration is a ratified registry rule, not a
  property of the software. ⛔ The richer requirement is **not** met and is **not** claimed: no
  response and no waiver is recorded or required, because there is as yet no route by which a member
  may respond. §8.8 is reserved for it and remains unlanded (Story 10.22).

#### Clause 2 — What did NOT change [Author-committed]

- **Prior sanction required** — the *correction* landed with Decision `2026-08-12-099`; what this
  entry adds is that the **dwell** it named as missing is now enforced. ⚠ The dwell governs the
  **ordinary** path only — the immediate-termination exception the Panel preserved (Q4.1) remains
  available where the actor records the reason, which the record now requires and retains.
- **Portal access** — ⛔ **UNTOUCHED.** It remains gated on a control that is built but not enabled,
  which is Story 10.21's to flip and not this story's. ⛔ Nothing here may be read as authorising it.
- **No count sentence was reintroduced.** The four dispositions still differ in kind, and a single
  number would misdescribe them — ⛔ a correction is not a mechanization.

#### Clause 3 — §8.4a mechanization block, VERBATIM (`en`) [Author-committed]

> > **Mechanization status.** This table states the constitutional distinction in full. Some of its rows are not yet enforced by any system workflow, and are recorded here as stated-but-unmechanized rather than omitted. **Each row is dispositioned individually, and no single count is given, because the dispositions differ in kind.**
> >
> > · **Escalation justification** — **mechanized.** A termination cannot be recorded without both parts of the two-part justification. The requirement is structural: the record has a separate field for each part, a termination lacking either is refused, and part (a) is refused where it merely restates part (b). *(The refusal of a mere restatement is a check on form, not a judgement of the reasoning; the reasoning remains the Trustee Panel's.)*
> > · **Prior sanction required** — ⚠ **corrected, and the dwell is now mechanized.** This disclosure previously stated that *"no precondition requires a prior suspension."* **That was wrong, and it understated this instrument's own mechanization.** Termination cannot be reached except from a suspended state: a transition directly from an unmoderated state to termination is not a legal transition and is refused. The **dwell** that was missing is now enforced — an interval must separate a suspension from the termination that ordinarily follows it, and its duration is a ratified rule of the instrument rather than a property of the software. ⚠ The dwell governs the **ordinary** path only: an immediate termination remains available where this instrument permits the exception **and the authorised actor records the reason for invoking it**, which the record now requires and retains.
> > · **Notice + opportunity to respond** — **mechanized to the extent of the dwell only.** The interval between suspension and termination is enforced, and elapsed dwell is what presently satisfies *"an opportunity to respond"*. ⛔ **The richer requirement is not met and is not claimed:** no response, and no waiver of the opportunity, is recorded or required, because there is as yet **no route by which a member may respond** — §8.8 is reserved for it and remains unlanded. This row is **deliberately scoped to the dwell by ruling**, and is not to be read as partially satisfied by accident.
> > · **Portal access — "Ended"** — authenticated access is ended by a control that is **built but not enabled**; until it is enabled, a terminated member retains portal access.
> >
> > A row's presence in this table states what the Trust holds to be right, not what the system currently does. **The distinction is deliberate and is not to be read as an assertion of compliance.** ⚠ A row is moved out of this list **only once the enforcing mechanism and its test are in place** — a disclosure that runs ahead of its mechanism is the same defect as one that trails it.

#### Clause 4 — §8.4a mechanization block, VERBATIM (`hi`) [Author-committed]

> > **यंत्रीकरण-स्थिति (Mechanization status)।** यह तालिका संवैधानिक भेद को पूर्णतः कहती है। इसकी कुछ पंक्तियाँ अभी किसी सिस्टम-कार्यप्रवाह द्वारा प्रवर्तित नहीं हैं, और उन्हें छोड़ने के बजाय कहा-गया-किंतु-अयंत्रीकृत के रूप में यहाँ अभिलिखित किया गया है। **प्रत्येक पंक्ति का निपटान पृथक्-पृथक् किया गया है, और कोई एकल गणना नहीं दी गई, क्योंकि निपटान प्रकार में भिन्न हैं।**
> >
> > · **वृद्धि-औचित्य** — **यंत्रीकृत।** दो-भागीय औचित्य के दोनों भागों के बिना समाप्ति अभिलिखित नहीं की जा सकती। यह अपेक्षा संरचनात्मक है: अभिलेख में प्रत्येक भाग हेतु पृथक् क्षेत्र है, किसी भी भाग के अभाव में समाप्ति अस्वीकृत होती है, और भाग (क) तब भी अस्वीकृत होता है जब वह केवल भाग (ख) को दोहराता हो। *(मात्र पुनरुक्ति की अस्वीकृति स्वरूप की जाँच है, तर्क का मूल्यांकन नहीं; तर्क का मूल्यांकन ट्रस्टी पैनल का ही रहता है।)*
> > · **पूर्व प्रतिबंध अपेक्षित** — ⚠ **संशोधित, तथा प्रतीक्षा-अवधि अब यंत्रीकृत।** इस प्रकटन में पहले कहा गया था कि *"किसी पूर्व निलंबन की कोई पूर्वशर्त लागू नहीं है।"* **वह कथन गलत था, और उसने स्वयं इस नियमावली के यंत्रीकरण को कम करके कहा।** समाप्ति निलंबित अवस्था के अतिरिक्त किसी अन्य अवस्था से प्राप्य नहीं है: अ-मॉडरेटेड अवस्था से सीधे समाप्ति का संक्रमण वैध संक्रमण नहीं है और अस्वीकृत होता है। जो **प्रतीक्षा-अवधि (dwell)** अनुपस्थित थी, वह अब प्रवर्तित है — निलंबन एवं सामान्य क्रम में उसके पश्चात् आने वाली समाप्ति के बीच अंतराल अपेक्षित है, और उसकी अवधि सॉफ़्टवेयर का गुण नहीं, अपितु इस नियमावली का अनुसमर्थित नियम है। ⚠ प्रतीक्षा-अवधि केवल **सामान्य** मार्ग को शासित करती है: तत्काल समाप्ति वहाँ उपलब्ध रहती है जहाँ यह नियमावली अपवाद की अनुमति देती है **और अधिकृत कर्ता उस अपवाद को प्रयुक्त करने का कारण अभिलिखित करता है** — जिसे अभिलेख अब अपेक्षित करता है और धारण करता है।
> > · **सूचना एवं उत्तर का अवसर** — **केवल प्रतीक्षा-अवधि की सीमा तक यंत्रीकृत।** निलंबन एवं समाप्ति के बीच का अंतराल प्रवर्तित है, और व्यतीत प्रतीक्षा-अवधि ही वर्तमान में *"उत्तर का अवसर"* को संतुष्ट करती है। ⛔ **व्यापक अपेक्षा पूर्ण नहीं है और उसका दावा नहीं किया जाता:** न कोई उत्तर, न उस अवसर का कोई अधित्याग अभिलिखित होता है या अपेक्षित है, क्योंकि अभी **ऐसा कोई मार्ग नहीं है जिससे सदस्य उत्तर दे सके** — §8.8 उसी हेतु आरक्षित है और अभी अनवतरित है। यह पंक्ति **निर्णय द्वारा जानबूझकर प्रतीक्षा-अवधि तक सीमित** है, और इसे संयोगवश आंशिक रूप से संतुष्ट नहीं पढ़ा जाना है।
> > · **पोर्टल-पहुँच — "समाप्त"** — अधिप्रमाणित पहुँच एक ऐसे नियंत्रण से समाप्त होती है जो **निर्मित है किंतु सक्रिय नहीं**; जब तक वह सक्रिय नहीं होता, समाप्त सदस्य की पोर्टल-पहुँच बनी रहती है।
> >
> > इस तालिका में किसी पंक्ति की उपस्थिति यह कहती है कि ट्रस्ट क्या उचित मानता है, यह नहीं कि सिस्टम वर्तमान में क्या करता है। **यह भेद जानबूझकर है और इसे अनुपालन के दावे के रूप में नहीं पढ़ा जाना है।** ⚠ किसी पंक्ति को इस सूची से **तभी** हटाया जाता है जब प्रवर्तक तंत्र एवं उसका परीक्षण विद्यमान हों — ऐसा प्रकटन जो अपने तंत्र से आगे चले, उसी दोष का है जो पीछे रहने वाले प्रकटन का है।

### Decision 2026-08-12-099: Trustee Panel rules Story 10.20's seven questions — Niyamavali §8.5 (grounds for termination), §8.6 (the principles) and §8.9 (the future-governance test) are authored, §8.2 is amended to authorise two previously unanchored grounds, and §8.4a's mechanization disclosure is CORRECTED; all new and amended text is reproduced verbatim in both locales, because `docs/legal/` is untracked and this entry is its only durable copy

**Decision type:** Governance amendment to Niyamavali Part 8, on the Story 10.18 / 10.19 shape — routing note committed alone, Panel ruling, instrument authored, then implementation.
**Status:** **Trustee-ratified** as to clauses 1–7 (the seven questions and their consequences). **Author-committed** as to clause 8 (drafting choices the ruling did not fix), clause 9 (the §8.9 placement deviation) and clause 11 (mechanical records). **Author-finding** as to clause 10 (the §8.4a correction) and clause 12 (the rejoin drift) — neither was ruled on, and neither is presented as ratified.
**Author:** BigDev (Solo Builder)
**Ratifying trustees:** Trustee Panel
**Date:** 2026-08-12
**Routing note:** `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/trustee-panel-routing-note-2026-08-11-story-10-20.md` (raised 2026-08-11, committed **alone** at `5ea5213` with no `packages/` or `apps/` file; ruled 2026-08-12).

---

#### Context

Story 10.20 proposes to make the system **enforce** a two-part escalation test, a dwell precondition and a restoration-exhaustion assertion before a member may be terminated. Every one of those rules rested on principles **no governing instrument stated**.

Niyamavali §8.2 enumerated five grounds for **suspension**. **Nothing enumerated grounds for termination** — which made `prd.md:871`'s testable consequence (*"Grounds for termination are enumerated separately from grounds for suspension; the two sets are not interchangeable"*) untestable, because one of the two sets did not exist. §8.4 was one sentence about reinstatement and the rejoin lock. The principles existed only in `moderation-model-decision-brief.md:531-583` and in PRD FR-56 — neither a governing instrument.

`deferred-work.md:3865-3869` routed §8.5/§8.6/§8.8/§8.9 to this story by name; `niyamavali.md:230` held the numbers open and directed that they *"are not to be closed up"*. Seven questions were routed rather than authored. **The Panel ruled all seven, and added two dimensions the note did not offer.**

---

#### Clause 1 — Q1 ratified: option (a). §8.5 carries BOTH a general test AND a six-item enumeration. `[Trustee-ratified]`

Both govern. The enumeration does not exhaust the test; the test does not authorise a ground outside the enumeration. ⛔ The six grounds are **not** a licence to invent further termination grounds in application code — that prohibition is stated in the instrument itself (§8.5, §8.9).

#### Clause 2 — Q2 ratified: all seven substantive principles are adopted, and the appeal gap is stated as clause 8. `[Trustee-ratified]`

§8.6 carries the constitutional sentence **verbatim and leading**, the **seven** principles, and the two-part escalation justification.

**The seven adopted principles are the brief's 1–7** (`moderation-model-decision-brief.md:531-583`), which is what Q2 put to the Panel. ⚠ **Brief principle 3 — *Proportionality, termination is the final measure* — is among them**, notwithstanding that the ruling's summary bullets do not restate it; see clause 8.1 for the mapping and the reasoning.

⛔ **The appeal gap is stated in §8.6 as an unnumbered *Recorded gap*, not as an eighth principle.** It is ratified content — the ruling states it and directs that §8.8 not be built here — but it is **not one of the seven**. No §8.8 appeal functionality is implemented by Story 10.20; that is Story 10.22's.

⚠ **Principles 6 and 7 each preserve an express immediate-termination exception**, and the word *normally* in each is **load-bearing** — clause 4's mechanization depends on it.

#### Clause 3 — Q3 ratified: option (a). §8.2 authorises `regulator-action` and `voluntary-pending-review`. `[Trustee-ratified]`

Both codes shipped with **no §8.2 anchor**, so a member could be suspended on a ground the Niyamavali did not authorise. §8.2 is amended to state them. ⛔ **No retirement, no vocabulary-removal migration, no enum change** — option (b) was put and not taken, and the routing note recorded that (b) was not implementable by this story in any event.

#### Clause 4 — Q4 ratified in four parts, one of which ADDS a dimension the routing note did not offer. `[Trustee-ratified]`

⚠ **The Panel's Q4 sub-numbering is not the routing note's.** The mapping is recorded in the note and must be consulted before any clause is read against a question number.

1. **Q4.1 — an ordinary 7-day dwell PLUS an express immediate-termination exception.** ⭐ **This is an addition, not a selection.** The routing note framed dwell as an **absolute** precondition on `terminate`; the Panel ruled it governs the **ordinary path only**. The immediate path remains available where the governing rules permit the exception **and the authorised actor records the reason for invoking it**. ⛔ **Immediate termination is not eliminated merely because a dwell exists**, and invoking it does **not** forfeit the member's future right of appeal.
   ⚠ The note's framing was wrong on the ruling's own terms: **principles 6 and 7 as adopted say termination *normally* follows suspension and notice *normally* precedes it.** An absolute gate would have contradicted the very principles it was built to mechanize.
2. **Q4.2 — the Terminate control remains enabled, gated by explicit re-confirmation.** A third shape; the note offered only *disabled-with-reason* or *enabled-and-409s*. The confirmation must state that the seven-day dwell is still open and that the actor is invoking the immediate-termination route. **The server remains authoritative.** ⛔ **The UI confirmation does not grant authority.** ⛔ Do not disable the button until day 7. The Panel affirmed by name that `legal_actions` must not be silently rewritten merely because a dwell exists.
3. **Q4.3 — elapsed dwell satisfies v1 opportunity-to-respond.** ⛔ **No response-or-waiver record may be invented as a precondition** — it would block ordinary termination on a surface this story does not build. The richer response/appeal capability is **Story 10.22's**.
4. **Q4.4 — seven days, held as a versioned Registry policy value.** ⛔ **`7` is never hard-coded in the moderation service.** The Trust runs versioned per-Pariwar rules (FR-7), and the moderation record must preserve enough policy-version information to explain **which dwell policy governed the decision**.

#### Clause 5 — Q5 ratified: option (a). Evaluate, snapshot, and require justification — never a hard block. `[Trustee-ratified]`

⛔ **Restoration exhaustion is NOT a hard technical gate and may not veto a termination.** The applicable restoration-use fact is evaluated, **snapshotted onto the moderation record**, and justified against where termination proceeds despite an available restoration path. ⛔ **An unresolved projection must not refuse an otherwise-authorised Trustee decision.**

**The Panel additionally clarified what restoration MEANS.** This was volunteered, not asked, and it governs:

- **Two distinct cases, never merged.** *Suspension restoration* — the ordinary lifecycle `active → suspended → restored`. *Termination restoration* — the exceptional `terminated → restored`, requiring the dedicated Trustee Panel authority established by Decision `2026-08-10-097` clause 1.
- The three restoration grounds (`rule-clearance`, `trustee-discretion`, `moderation-error`) are **reasons for a restoration decision, not permissions**.
- ⭐ **Restoration is PROSPECTIVE, not retroactive.** It restores membership **from the restoration point forward** and **does not erase the terminated period**. Therefore: **no retroactive contribution credit**; missed contribution obligations are **not** treated as fulfilled; R7 continues to determine the member's resulting obligations and standing; and renewal / lapse / rejoin rules are evaluated on the member's **actual state and dates**. If restoration occurs months later and the ordinary rules mean the member has lapsed, **the ordinary lapse rules apply**.
- ⛔ **No special "restored terminated member" state may be created** that bypasses R7 or any other ordinary membership rule. **Restoration is not amnesty.**

#### Clause 6 — Q6 ratified: the recommendation in full. `[Trustee-ratified]`

`ordinarilyResultsIn` is **guidance**, not automatic sanction selection.

- **All seven moderation grounds** — `r7-contribution-discipline`, `r14-forgery`, `r10a-parallel-org-office`, `concealment-confirmed`, `helpdesk-escalated-abuse`, `regulator-action`, `voluntary-pending-review` — ratified as **`'suspend'`**.
- **All three restore grounds** — `rule-clearance`, `trustee-discretion`, `moderation-error` — ratified as **`null`**. ⛔ **No `ordinarilyResultsIn` value may be invented for a restore ground.**

*A reason code does not itself terminate a member.* The Trustee Panel decides whether the actual case warrants suspension or termination (§8.6, principle 2).

#### Clause 7 — Q7 ratified: §8.9 lands here, and the severity gradient is intentional. `[Trustee-ratified]`

1. **Q7.1 — yes.** §8.9 establishes that any future moderation ground or sanction is evaluated **against the principles**, never justified by analogy to an existing reason code. ⛔ Future moderation policy cannot be created by casually adding a reason code.
2. **Q7.2 — the severity distinction is intentional, and the Panel stated a MORE PRECISE model than §2.5 currently expresses:**

   > Termination remains **uncleared** → **no ordinary rejoin eligibility**.
   > Authorised restoration / clearance → membership restored **prospectively** → ordinary membership / R7 / lapse / **rejoin** rules are **evaluated from the member's actual state and dates**.

   ⛔ **The model is NOT "12 months after restoration."** Restoration supplies **neither a new start instant nor a new duration**. ⛔ **No restoration-relative 12-month clock may be introduced** unless a future governance amendment says so **expressly**.
   ⛔ The old wording must **not** be implemented mechanically as *"termination and lapse both get a 12-month rejoin lock running from the date of termination/lapse."*

   ⚠ **The severity distinction is structural, not numeric.** Suspension → temporary disciplinary restriction, ordinary restoration possible. Termination → exceptional disciplinary removal; the termination must be **cleared** by authorised restoration; no retroactive erasure of the terminated period; ordinary membership / R7 rules apply after restoration; rejoin eligibility then follows the applicable rejoin rules.

   ⚠ **This clause states a governing position that §2.5, §8.4 and §8.4a do not yet express.** It is **not** a re-reading of those sections. See clause 12.

---

#### Clause 8 — Drafting choices the ruling did not fix `[Author-committed]`

Recorded so they are visible and correctable rather than buried in prose:

1. **Principle numbering — RECONCILED 2026-08-12, before Task 4.** §8.6 was first authored with **eight** numbered clauses, the appeal gap as clause 8. That made the instrument assert **eight principles where the Panel adopted seven**. The reconciliation established that the mismatch was **presentational, not substantive**, and §8.6 was corrected in both locales:

   **The mapping.** The ruling's seven summary bullets map onto the brief's principles as **{1, 2, 4, 5, 6, 7, 8}** — bullet 3 is brief 4, bullet 4 is brief 5, and so on. **Brief principle 3 (*"Proportionality — termination is the final measure"*) appears in NO bullet.** So the bullet list cannot be read as the enumeration of *"all seven"*, because that reading would silently drop brief 3.

   **Why brief 1–7 is the ratified set.** (a) Q2 as put asked the Panel to *"land principles 1–7"* and required that *"any principle the Panel declines must be named"* — none was named. (b) Brief 3 is the governing basis for the **two-part escalation justification**, which the same ruling ratified in substance and which AC6 mechanizes; ratifying the mechanization while dropping its principle is incoherent. (c) The ruling's own wording is *"All seven principles are Trustee-ratified. **In particular**, implementation must preserve these distinctions"* — *in particular* introduces emphasis, not an enumeration. (d) The ruling cites *"principles 6 and 7"* on the brief's numbering, confirming the frame.

   ⇒ **§8.6 clauses 1–7 = brief principles 1–7 = the seven adopted principles** `[Trustee-ratified]`. **The appeal-gap statement is ratified content** (the ruling states it, and directs that §8.8 not be implemented here) **but it is NOT one of the seven principles** — so it is no longer numbered as one. It now appears as an unnumbered closing clause, ***Recorded gap — the moderation appeal***, which says in terms that it is *"not one of the seven principles above"*. §8.6's opening line now reads *"The following **seven** principles govern"*, so the instrument's arithmetic matches the ruling's words.

   ✅ **CONFIRMED by the Trustee Panel, 2026-08-12: brief principle 3 (*Proportionality*) STANDS as §8.6 clause 3.** The reading above is ratified, not merely author-committed, and this sub-clause is **closed** — no superseding decision is owed on it.

   ⛔ **No principle was added, removed or reworded by this reconciliation** — clauses 1–7 are byte-identical to what was authored. Cross-references repointed from *"§8.6, principle 8"* to *"§8.6, Recorded gap"* in both locales' reserved-numbers note, in the story (AC13.1, Task 3) and in the routing note.
2. **The §8.2 wording of the two newly-authorised grounds.** The ruling authorised the codes; the sentence describing each is the author's. `regulator-action` is stated as *"an order or direction of a competent authority"*; `voluntary-pending-review` as *"entered at the member's own request while a matter concerning them is under consideration"*.
3. **The §8.5 gloss on each of the six grounds.** The six ground **names** are the Panel's; the one-line definition after each is the author's, drawn from the brief's usage.
4. **The closing sentence of §8.2** stating that neither new ground is a ground for termination. Not ruled; it follows from Q1 and prevents the §8.2/§8.5 sets being read as one list.

#### Clause 9 — §8.9 is placed AFTER §8.7, not before it `[Author-committed]`

Story 10.20's AC2 and Task 3 both said to insert §8.5, §8.6 and §8.9 *"between §8.4a and §8.7"*. Followed literally, that yields a document reading **§8.6 → §8.9 → §8.7**, which is numerically wrong.

⇒ §8.5 and §8.6 are placed **between §8.4a and §8.7**, as instructed; **§8.9 is placed after §8.7**, following the amended reserved-numbers note. Part 8 now reads in full numeric order: **8.1 · 8.2 · 8.3 · 8.4 · 8.4a · 8.5 · 8.6 · 8.7 · [8.8 reserved] · 8.9**, identically in both locales. ⛔ **§8.7 is not renumbered and its text is unchanged.**

#### Clause 10 — §8.4a's mechanization disclosure was FACTUALLY WRONG and is CORRECTED `[Author-finding]`

⛔ **Not a Panel ruling. Not a mechanization.** The disclosure landed by Story 10.19 on 2026-08-10 stated: *"**Prior sanction required** — no precondition requires a prior suspension."*

**That was false at HEAD.** `nextModerationStatus('none', 'terminate')` returns `null` (`packages/domain/src/member/moderation/status.ts:41-42`), so termination structurally **cannot** be reached except from `suspended` — Story 10.10's Decision 2, true since it shipped. What is actually missing is **dwell**.

⇒ The row is **corrected**, in both locales: the precondition **exists** and is **nominal**. The instrument was **understating its own mechanization**, which is the same copy-truth defect as overstating it. ⛔ **A correction is not a mechanization**, and this entry claims no row as newly mechanized.

⚠ **The other three rows are unchanged and remain stated-but-unmechanized.** Escalation justification and notice flip **only when their enforcing code and tests are green** (Story 10.20 Tasks 5 and 6); portal access remains gated on the `termination_access_block` flip, which is **Story 10.21's**. The block's *"Four of its rows"* count sentence is replaced by **per-row dispositions**, because the four dispositions now differ in kind and a single count would misdescribe them.

---

#### The amended and new text, VERBATIM — ENGLISH

⚠ `docs/legal/` is gitignored. **This reproduction is the only durable copy.**

**§8.2 as amended** (new material begins at *"helpline-escalated abuse; **regulator or statutory direction**"*):

> ### 8.2 Grounds for suspension
> An R7 sub-clause violation; **R14** forgery; **R10(A)** parallel-org office-bearing; a concealment flag **confirmed by a State Trustee** (§5.6); helpline-escalated abuse; **regulator or statutory direction**, where an order or direction of a competent authority requires that the member's participation be suspended; or a **voluntary suspension pending review**, entered at the member's own request while a matter concerning them is under consideration.
>
> *The last two grounds are stated here so that every ground on which the Trust may in fact suspend a member is authorised by this Niyamavali. Neither is a ground for termination: §8.5 states those separately, and they are not interchangeable.*

**§8.4a's mechanization-status block as corrected** (replacing the block landed by Decision `2026-08-10-097`):

> **Mechanization status.** This table states the constitutional distinction in full. Some of its rows are not yet enforced by any system workflow, and are recorded here as stated-but-unmechanized rather than omitted. **Each row is dispositioned individually, and no single count is given, because the dispositions differ in kind.**
>
> · **Escalation justification** — **not yet mechanized.** No two-part justification is captured at the point of termination.
> · **Prior sanction required** — ⚠ **corrected.** This disclosure previously stated that *"no precondition requires a prior suspension."* **That was wrong, and it understated this instrument's own mechanization.** Termination cannot be reached except from a suspended state: a transition directly from an unmoderated state to termination is not a legal transition and is refused. What is **not** enforced is any **dwell** — a required interval between a suspension and the termination that follows it — so a suspension and a termination may today be recorded moments apart.
> · **Notice + opportunity to respond** — **not yet mechanized.** Notice is sent; **no opportunity to respond precedes the act**.
> · **Portal access — "Ended"** — authenticated access is ended by a control that is **built but not enabled**; until it is enabled, a terminated member retains portal access.
>
> A row's presence in this table states what the Trust holds to be right, not what the system currently does. **The distinction is deliberate and is not to be read as an assertion of compliance.** ⚠ A row is moved out of this list **only once the enforcing mechanism and its test are in place** — a disclosure that runs ahead of its mechanism is the same defect as one that trails it.

**§8.5 — new:**

> ### 8.5 Grounds for termination
>
> **The test.** Termination requires conduct evidencing a **fundamental failure of the trust on which membership rests**. Seriousness alone is not the test: grave conduct that leaves the trust relationship intact and curable is a ground for suspension under §8.2, not a ground for termination.
>
> **The grounds.** The grounds for termination are:
>
> 1. **Forged documents** — the fabrication or material alteration of a document submitted to the Trust.
> 2. **Identity fraud** — the assumption of another person's identity, or the material falsification of one's own.
> 3. **Financial fraud** — obtaining, or attempting to obtain, Trust funds or benefits by deception.
> 4. **Deliberate concealment** — the intentional withholding of a fact the member was obliged to disclose.
> 5. **Repeated malicious abuse** — sustained abusive conduct toward members, volunteers, staff or office-bearers, persisted in after warning.
> 6. **Persistent conduct materially threatening the Trust after due process** — conduct that continues to place the Trust or its members at material risk after the member has been given notice and an opportunity to respond.
>
> **Both the test and the enumeration govern.** The enumeration does not exhaust the test, and the test does not authorise a ground outside the enumeration. A ground not stated above requires an amendment under **Part 11** before it may be acted upon, and shall be evaluated under **§8.9**. The enumeration is **not** a warrant to derive further grounds by analogy, nor to create them in the configuration or operation of any system.
>
> **These grounds are stated separately from the grounds for suspension, and the two sets are not interchangeable.** §8.2 states the grounds on which a member may be suspended. That a ground appears in §8.2 does not make it a ground for termination; that a ground appears in this section does not compel termination in any given case. **The Trustee Panel determines the sanction** (§8.6, principle 2).

**§8.6 — new:**

> ### 8.6 Principles governing termination
>
> **Termination is an exceptional governance act, not a stronger suspension.** It carries its own threshold, its own reasoning and its own record.
>
> The following **seven** principles govern every termination under this Part.
>
> **1. Constitutional distinction.** Suspension and termination are distinct sanctions with distinct thresholds — not two intensities of one act.
>
> **2. Reason codes establish the ground; the Trustee Panel determines the sanction.** Reason codes establish the factual ground requiring moderation. They do not, by themselves, determine whether the appropriate outcome is suspension or termination. The Trustee Panel shall determine the proportionate sanction after considering the seriousness of the conduct, any previous disciplinary history, the member's response, the possibility of restoration, and the Trust's duty to protect the Pariwar.
>
> **3. Proportionality — termination is the final measure.** Termination is the Trust's final disciplinary measure and shall be used only where suspension or restoration is inadequate to protect the Trust or to enforce this Niyamavali.
>
> **4. Restoration is ordinarily exhausted first.** Where a Niyamavali-defined restoration path exists, termination shall ordinarily occur only after that restoration opportunity has been exhausted, unless the Trustee Panel records specific reasons why immediate termination is necessary to protect the Trust.
>
> **5. The test is a failure of trust, not merely seriousness.** Termination is ordinarily reserved for conduct that irreparably destroys the relationship of trust between the member and the Trust, or presents a continuing material risk to the Trust or its members (§8.5).
>
> **6. Termination normally follows a prior suspension**, except where this Niyamavali expressly creates an immediate-termination exception.
>
> **7. Documented notice and an opportunity to respond** must normally precede termination, unless an express exception applies.
>
> **The escalation justification.** Where termination is imposed, the decision-maker shall record **both**: **(a) why suspension is inadequate** to the case — what suspension would fail to protect, what risk would persist through it, or why the restoration path it preserves is unavailable or futile; **and (b) why termination is proportionate** to the conduct. The two parts are **separately answerable**, and neither may be derived or pre-filled from the other. Part (a) is **not** satisfied by asserting the seriousness of the conduct, by citing the reason code, or by restating part (b).
>
> **Recorded gap — the moderation appeal.** The absence of a moderation-specific appeal mechanism is a separate governance gap. It is **stated** here; it is **not closed** here; and it is **not one of the seven principles above**. Its closure requires its own amendment (§8.8, reserved).

**The reserved-numbers note as amended:**

> *§8.8 is reserved. §8.7 is deliberately numbered ahead of it; the intervening number is held for the remaining Part 8 amendment — the moderation appeal (§8.6, *Recorded gap*) — and is not to be closed up.*

**§8.9 — new:**

> ### 8.9 Evaluation of future grounds and sanctions
>
> **Any future moderation ground or sanction shall be evaluated against the principles stated in §8.6, rather than by analogy to an existing reason code.**
>
> **A reason code is not a route to new policy.** The moderation vocabulary is governed data. The addition of a code to it does not create a ground, does not create a sanction, and does not enlarge the grounds stated in §8.5. A new ground or a new sanction requires an amendment under **Part 11**, evaluated against §8.6, and may not be introduced by the configuration or operation of any system.

---

#### The amended and new text, VERBATIM — HINDI (हिन्दी)

⚠ The Hindi is a **co-equal governing instrument**, not a translation artifact.

**§8.2 as amended:**

> ### 8.2 निलंबन के आधार
> R7 उप-खंड उल्लंघन; **R14** जालसाज़ी; **R10(A)** समानांतर-संगठन पदाधिकार; **State Trustee द्वारा पुष्ट** छिपाव-चिह्न (§5.6); helpline-escalated दुरुपयोग; **नियामक अथवा वैधानिक निर्देश**, जहाँ किसी सक्षम प्राधिकारी का आदेश या निर्देश यह अपेक्षा करता हो कि सदस्य की भागीदारी निलंबित की जाए; अथवा **समीक्षा-लंबित स्वैच्छिक निलंबन**, जो सदस्य के अपने अनुरोध पर, उससे संबंधित किसी विषय के विचाराधीन रहते हुए, लागू किया गया हो।
>
> *अंतिम दो आधार यहाँ इसलिए कहे गए हैं ताकि प्रत्येक वह आधार, जिस पर ट्रस्ट वस्तुतः किसी सदस्य को निलंबित कर सकता है, इस नियमावली द्वारा प्राधिकृत हो। इनमें से कोई भी समाप्ति का आधार नहीं है: §8.5 उन्हें पृथक् रूप से कहता है, और दोनों परस्पर विनिमेय नहीं हैं।*

**§8.4a की यंत्रीकरण-स्थिति, संशोधित:**

> **यंत्रीकरण-स्थिति (Mechanization status)।** यह तालिका संवैधानिक भेद को पूर्णतः कहती है। इसकी कुछ पंक्तियाँ अभी किसी सिस्टम-कार्यप्रवाह द्वारा प्रवर्तित नहीं हैं, और उन्हें छोड़ने के बजाय कहा-गया-किंतु-अयंत्रीकृत के रूप में यहाँ अभिलिखित किया गया है। **प्रत्येक पंक्ति का निपटान पृथक्-पृथक् किया गया है, और कोई एकल गणना नहीं दी गई, क्योंकि निपटान प्रकार में भिन्न हैं।**
>
> · **वृद्धि-औचित्य** — **अभी अयंत्रीकृत।** समाप्ति के समय कोई दो-भागीय औचित्य अभिलिखित नहीं होता।
> · **पूर्व प्रतिबंध अपेक्षित** — ⚠ **संशोधित।** इस प्रकटन में पहले कहा गया था कि *"किसी पूर्व निलंबन की कोई पूर्वशर्त लागू नहीं है।"* **वह कथन गलत था, और उसने स्वयं इस नियमावली के यंत्रीकरण को कम करके कहा।** समाप्ति निलंबित अवस्था के अतिरिक्त किसी अन्य अवस्था से प्राप्य नहीं है: अ-मॉडरेटेड अवस्था से सीधे समाप्ति का संक्रमण वैध संक्रमण नहीं है और अस्वीकृत होता है। जो प्रवर्तित **नहीं** है, वह है **प्रतीक्षा-अवधि (dwell)** — निलंबन एवं उसके पश्चात् आने वाली समाप्ति के बीच कोई अपेक्षित अंतराल — अतः आज निलंबन एवं समाप्ति क्षणों के अंतर पर अभिलिखित हो सकते हैं।
> · **सूचना एवं उत्तर का अवसर** — **अभी अयंत्रीकृत।** सूचना भेजी जाती है; **कार्य से पूर्व उत्तर का कोई अवसर नहीं** है।
> · **पोर्टल-पहुँच — "समाप्त"** — अधिप्रमाणित पहुँच एक ऐसे नियंत्रण से समाप्त होती है जो **निर्मित है किंतु सक्रिय नहीं**; जब तक वह सक्रिय नहीं होता, समाप्त सदस्य की पोर्टल-पहुँच बनी रहती है।
>
> इस तालिका में किसी पंक्ति की उपस्थिति यह कहती है कि ट्रस्ट क्या उचित मानता है, यह नहीं कि सिस्टम वर्तमान में क्या करता है। **यह भेद जानबूझकर है और इसे अनुपालन के दावे के रूप में नहीं पढ़ा जाना है।** ⚠ किसी पंक्ति को इस सूची से **तभी** हटाया जाता है जब प्रवर्तक तंत्र एवं उसका परीक्षण विद्यमान हों — ऐसा प्रकटन जो अपने तंत्र से आगे चले, उसी दोष का है जो पीछे रहने वाले प्रकटन का है।

**§8.5 — नया:**

> ### 8.5 समाप्ति के आधार
>
> **कसौटी।** समाप्ति हेतु ऐसा आचरण अपेक्षित है जो **उस विश्वास के मूलभूत भंग** को प्रमाणित करे जिस पर सदस्यता टिकी है। केवल गंभीरता कसौटी नहीं है: ऐसा गंभीर आचरण जो विश्वास-संबंध को अक्षुण्ण एवं सुधार्य छोड़ता हो, §8.2 के अंतर्गत निलंबन का आधार है, समाप्ति का नहीं।
>
> **आधार।** समाप्ति के आधार निम्नलिखित हैं:
>
> 1. **जाली दस्तावेज़** — ट्रस्ट को प्रस्तुत किसी दस्तावेज़ का गढ़ना अथवा भौतिक परिवर्तन।
> 2. **पहचान-छल** — किसी अन्य व्यक्ति की पहचान धारण करना, अथवा अपनी पहचान का भौतिक मिथ्याकरण।
> 3. **वित्तीय छल** — छल द्वारा ट्रस्ट की निधि अथवा लाभ प्राप्त करना, या प्राप्त करने का प्रयास।
> 4. **जानबूझकर छिपाव** — ऐसे तथ्य को जानबूझकर छिपाना जिसे प्रकट करने हेतु सदस्य बाध्य था।
> 5. **बारंबार दुर्भावनापूर्ण दुरुपयोग** — सदस्यों, स्वयंसेवकों, कर्मियों अथवा पदाधिकारियों के प्रति निरंतर अपमानजनक आचरण, जो चेतावनी के पश्चात् भी जारी रहे।
> 6. **उचित प्रक्रिया के पश्चात् भी ट्रस्ट हेतु भौतिक रूप से संकटकारी सतत आचरण** — ऐसा आचरण जो सदस्य को सूचना एवं उत्तर का अवसर दिए जाने के पश्चात् भी ट्रस्ट अथवा उसके सदस्यों को भौतिक जोखिम में डालता रहे।
>
> **कसौटी एवं गणना, दोनों शासित करती हैं।** गणना कसौटी को निःशेष नहीं करती, और कसौटी गणना से बाहर किसी आधार को प्राधिकृत नहीं करती। ऊपर न कहा गया कोई आधार, उस पर कार्य किए जाने से पूर्व, **भाग 11** के अंतर्गत संशोधन की अपेक्षा करता है और **§8.9** के अंतर्गत मूल्यांकित होगा। यह गणना सादृश्य से अन्य आधार निकालने का, अथवा किसी प्रणाली के विन्यास या संचालन में उन्हें गढ़ने का, अधिकार-पत्र **नहीं** है।
>
> **ये आधार निलंबन के आधारों से पृथक् कहे गए हैं, और दोनों समुच्चय परस्पर विनिमेय नहीं हैं।** §8.2 उन आधारों को कहता है जिन पर सदस्य निलंबित किया जा सकता है। किसी आधार का §8.2 में होना उसे समाप्ति का आधार नहीं बनाता; किसी आधार का इस धारा में होना किसी भी प्रकरण में समाप्ति को बाध्यकर नहीं बनाता। **प्रतिबंध का निर्धारण ट्रस्टी पैनल करता है** (§8.6, सिद्धांत 2)।

**§8.6 — नया:**

> ### 8.6 समाप्ति को शासित करने वाले सिद्धांत
>
> **समाप्ति एक असाधारण शासन-कार्य है, कोई प्रबलतर निलंबन नहीं।** इसकी अपनी सीमा-रेखा, अपना तर्क, एवं अपना अभिलेख है।
>
> इस भाग के अंतर्गत प्रत्येक समाप्ति निम्नलिखित **सात** सिद्धांतों से शासित होती है।
>
> **1. संवैधानिक भेद।** निलंबन एवं समाप्ति भिन्न सीमा-रेखाओं वाले भिन्न प्रतिबंध हैं — एक ही कार्य की दो तीव्रताएँ नहीं।
>
> **2. कारण-कोड आधार स्थापित करते हैं; प्रतिबंध ट्रस्टी पैनल निर्धारित करता है।** कारण-कोड मॉडरेशन की अपेक्षा करने वाला तथ्यात्मक आधार स्थापित करते हैं। वे स्वयं यह निर्धारित नहीं करते कि उपयुक्त परिणाम निलंबन है अथवा समाप्ति। ट्रस्टी पैनल आचरण की गंभीरता, किसी पूर्व अनुशासनिक इतिहास, सदस्य के उत्तर, पुनर्स्थापन की संभावना, तथा परिवार की रक्षा के ट्रस्ट के कर्तव्य पर विचार करने के पश्चात् समानुपातिक प्रतिबंध निर्धारित करेगा।
>
> **3. समानुपातिकता — समाप्ति अंतिम उपाय है।** समाप्ति ट्रस्ट का अंतिम अनुशासनिक उपाय है और केवल वहीं प्रयुक्त होगी जहाँ ट्रस्ट की रक्षा अथवा इस नियमावली के प्रवर्तन हेतु निलंबन या पुनर्स्थापन अपर्याप्त हो।
>
> **4. पुनर्स्थापन सामान्यतः पहले निःशेष किया जाता है।** जहाँ नियमावली-परिभाषित पुनर्स्थापन-पथ विद्यमान हो, वहाँ समाप्ति सामान्यतः उस पुनर्स्थापन-अवसर के निःशेष हो जाने के पश्चात् ही होगी, जब तक ट्रस्टी पैनल यह विशिष्ट कारण अभिलिखित न करे कि ट्रस्ट की रक्षा हेतु तत्काल समाप्ति क्यों आवश्यक है।
>
> **5. कसौटी विश्वास का भंग है, केवल गंभीरता नहीं।** समाप्ति सामान्यतः उस आचरण हेतु आरक्षित है जो सदस्य एवं ट्रस्ट के बीच विश्वास-संबंध को अपूरणीय रूप से नष्ट कर दे, अथवा ट्रस्ट या उसके सदस्यों हेतु सतत भौतिक जोखिम प्रस्तुत करे (§8.5)।
>
> **6. समाप्ति सामान्यतः किसी पूर्व निलंबन के पश्चात् होती है**, सिवाय वहाँ जहाँ यह नियमावली स्पष्टतः तत्काल-समाप्ति का अपवाद सृजित करती हो।
>
> **7. प्रलेखित सूचना एवं उत्तर का अवसर** सामान्यतः समाप्ति से पूर्व आवश्यक है, जब तक कोई स्पष्ट अपवाद लागू न हो।
>
> **वृद्धि-औचित्य।** जहाँ समाप्ति अधिरोपित की जाती है, वहाँ निर्णयकर्ता **दोनों** अभिलिखित करेगा: **(क) निलंबन क्यों अपर्याप्त है** — निलंबन किसकी रक्षा करने में विफल रहता, उससे कौन-सा जोखिम बना रहता, अथवा वह जिस पुनर्स्थापन-पथ को सुरक्षित रखता है वह क्यों अनुपलब्ध या निष्फल है; **और (ख) समाप्ति क्यों समानुपातिक है** उस आचरण हेतु। दोनों भाग **पृथक्-पृथक् उत्तरणीय** हैं, और किसी एक से दूसरा व्युत्पन्न अथवा पूर्व-भरित नहीं किया जा सकता। भाग (क) आचरण की गंभीरता कहने से, कारण-कोड उद्धृत करने से, अथवा भाग (ख) को दोहराने से **संतुष्ट नहीं होता**।
>
> **अभिलिखित अंतराल — मॉडरेशन अपील।** मॉडरेशन-विशिष्ट अपील-तंत्र का अभाव एक पृथक् शासन-अंतराल है। यह यहाँ **कहा गया** है; यहाँ **बंद नहीं किया गया** है; और यह **ऊपर कहे गए सात सिद्धांतों में से एक नहीं** है। इसका समापन अपने स्वयं के संशोधन की अपेक्षा करता है (§8.8, आरक्षित)।

**आरक्षित-क्रमांक टिप्पणी, संशोधित:**

> *§8.8 आरक्षित है। §8.7 जानबूझकर उससे पहले क्रमांकित है; बीच का क्रमांक भाग 8 के शेष संशोधन — मॉडरेशन अपील (§8.6, *अभिलिखित अंतराल*) — हेतु सुरक्षित है और उसे भरा नहीं जाना है।*

**§8.9 — नया:**

> ### 8.9 भावी आधारों एवं प्रतिबंधों का मूल्यांकन
>
> **कोई भी भावी मॉडरेशन-आधार अथवा प्रतिबंध §8.6 में कहे गए सिद्धांतों के विरुद्ध मूल्यांकित होगा, न कि किसी विद्यमान कारण-कोड की सादृश्यता से।**
>
> **कारण-कोड नई नीति का मार्ग नहीं है।** मॉडरेशन-शब्दावली शासित आँकड़ा है। उसमें किसी कोड का जुड़ना न कोई आधार सृजित करता है, न कोई प्रतिबंध, और न §8.5 में कहे गए आधारों का विस्तार करता है। कोई नया आधार अथवा नया प्रतिबंध **भाग 11** के अंतर्गत संशोधन की अपेक्षा करता है, §8.6 के विरुद्ध मूल्यांकित होगा, और किसी प्रणाली के विन्यास (configuration) या संचालन से प्रवर्तित नहीं किया जा सकता।

Per `[[feedback_closure_language_precision]]`: **authored and Panel-ratified**; **counsel review remains outstanding**. Never "approved", never "final".

#### Clause 12 — GOVERNANCE DRIFT on the rejoin model: surfaced, NOT closed `[Author-finding]`

⛔ **Nothing in this clause is ratified, and nothing in it was edited by this decision.** Recorded at the Panel's direction: *"if an already-ratified text conflicts, surface it as governance drift rather than editing it."*

Clause 7.2's model conflicts with **four texts and one live code path**:

| Text | Status |
|---|---|
| `niyamavali.md:64` / hi `:68` — **§2.5** (*"terminated **or lapses** … only after a [[12-month]] rejoin lock"*) | Base instrument; no numbered ratification |
| `niyamavali.md:182` / hi `:180` — **§8.4** | ⛔ **RATIFIED** by Decision `2026-08-10-097`; reproduced verbatim in that entry |
| `niyamavali.md:207` / hi `:205` — **§8.4a**, *Rejoin* row | ⛔ **RATIFIED** by the same decision |
| `prd.md:858` — FR-56's rejoin consequence | PRD; not amended by this story |
| `signup.handlers.ts:118-123` + `handlers.ts:269` | **Shipped code** — see below |

⭐ **The sharp one.** The signup guard blocks a returning identity only while `moderationStatus === 'terminated' && now < rejoin_permitted_at`, and `rejoin_permitted_at` is fixed at termination as `addTwelveMonths(now)`. ⇒ **At month 13, an identity whose termination was never cleared passes the guard.** **Time alone currently cures an uncleared termination** — precisely the mechanical reading clause 7.2 directs against.

⛔ **The guard is NOT changed by this decision.** Changing it first would make the code enforce a rejoin model **no governing instrument states** — the inversion this entire arc exists to end. **The instrument moves first.** Until a superseding amendment lands, **§2.5 governs as written.**

**Disposition:** OWED, owner unassigned. Reconciling §2.5 / §8.4 / §8.4a is a **Part 11 amendment against ratified text** requiring its own routing note, its own ruling and its own **superseding** decision (`[[feedback_supersede_never_reinterpret]]`). Recorded in full at `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/deferred-work.md`.

⚠ **`prd.md:300` (FR-6, voluntary withdrawal) is expressly OUT of scope** of that amendment — a second, independent lock arising from a **voluntary** act. ⛔ An amendment that "fixes the 12-month lock" without distinguishing the two would silently change a withdrawal consequence for disciplinary reasons.

✅ **Not drift:** the prospective-restoration half of clause 5 is **consistent with the code as shipped**. No special *"restored terminated member"* state exists — the signup guard maps the **latest** action, so a `restore` clears the block (`member-auth.repo.ts:72-76`). Recorded as **verified-consistent, not fixed**.
