### Decision 2026-08-10-098: The two conditions Decision `2026-08-10-097` left open are DISCHARGED — the no-session implementation is adopted, the Q6(b) annex affirmations are ratified-as-written and the capability-bar entry is authorised, and the domain vocabulary is corrected: "login succeeds but returns 403" is the wrong model

**Decision type:** Other — a short confirming entry that discharges two conditions its own predecessor created, plus one Panel-issued vocabulary constraint on the implementation. ⛔ **It introduces no new ruling on any of Story 10.19's six questions.**
**Status:** **Trustee-ratified** as to clauses 1–3. **Author-committed** as to clause 4 (the story edits that follow mechanically) and clause 5 (mechanical records).
**Author:** BigDev, on the Panel's confirmation of Decision `2026-08-10-097` clauses 7, 11 and 12
**Ratifying trustees:** Trustee Panel
**Date:** 2026-08-10

---

#### Context

Decision `2026-08-10-097` deliberately left **two conditions** open rather than resolving them by assumption, and both are discharged here:

- **Clause 11** adopted the no-session reading of the Panel's member-facing direction but recorded that the direction admitted a second reading — a restricted notice-only session — and that *"if the Panel intended the restricted-session reading, it must say so **before** implementation begins."*
- **Clause 7** affirmed the three Q6(b) mechanism-annex properties but noted they had been posed as **prose, not lettered options**, entered *"A/A/A"* as affirmation-as-written, and held that *"the entry is not written until"* the Panel confirmed nothing narrower was meant.

**This entry does not edit `097`** (`[[feedback_supersede_never_reinterpret]]`). `097` was correct when written and remains the governing record of the six rulings; this entry resolves the conditions `097` itself set, and adds the vocabulary constraint at clause 3.

---

1. **⭐ The NO-SESSION implementation is ADOPTED.** Decision `097` clause 11's adopted reading stands, and the alternative it foreclosed is now foreclosed **by ruling, not by author judgement**: ⛔ **no restricted, notice-only, or reduced-scope session may be minted on the termination path.** `issueFullSession` is not reached. Notice access is distinguished from ordinary member access **structurally** — one path issues a session and the other does not — **never** by a flag, scope, audience or claim on a session object.

2. **⭐ The Q6(b) mechanism-annex affirmations are RATIFIED AS WRITTEN. No narrower interpretation was intended.** All three properties stand as the annex states them: (i) the capability bar is **extended into authentication**, and that extension is the ruling rather than a side effect of a passing source scan; (ii) the block **fails open**, `fallbackDefault: false`, and the `allow` entry's `rationale` must **trace** that polarity rather than assert it; (iii) the flag is named for the **overlay**, not the lifecycle, with kind `member_flow`. ⇒ **The capability-bar entry is AUTHORISED and may now be written** — the condition at `097` clause 7 is discharged. ⚠ Authorisation to **admit** the flag is not authorisation to **flip** it: the flip remains gated on Story 10.21 per `097` clause 6, and requires its own `.decision-log.md` entry.

3. **⭐ The domain vocabulary is CORRECTED, and the correction is binding on the implementation.** Panel direction, reproduced verbatim:

   > "Login succeeds but returns 403."
   >
   > That's technically confusing.
   >
   > The better conceptual model is:
   >
   > OTP verification succeeds → termination status is established → session issuance is denied → structured termination response returned → client renders termination surface.
   >
   > The HTTP representation can still use whatever error/status convention the existing auth flow requires, but the domain semantics should be clear: identity verification succeeded; authorization to establish a member session did not.

   ⛔ **"Login succeeds but returns 403" must not appear in code, comments, test titles or copy.** It collapses two distinct things — identity verification, which **succeeds**, and authorization to establish a member session, which is **denied**. The HTTP status code is a **transport detail** and may remain whatever the existing auth flow requires; **the domain semantics are not a transport detail.** Symbols, doc-comments and test names are written for *session issuance denied after successful identity verification*, never for a failed login.
   ⚠ This is why `097` clause 12 required a **structured** response rather than a bare error code: a bare code is precisely the shape of a generic authentication failure, and would encode the wrong model in the wire contract.

#### 4. [Author-committed] The story edits that follow, which are consequences and NOT rulings

Per the Panel's instruction — *"treat these as implementation consequences of Decision 097, not as a new Trustee ruling"* — the following are recorded as story edits made under `097` clause 12 and this entry, and are **not** re-ratifications:

- **AC4** gains the structured-response requirement: the payload carries the elements required to render the termination notice (Decision, Ground **label**, Effective date, Further communication), with **Summary structurally absent** per Q2(a) — ⛔ never an empty string. It also gains the explicit no-session clause and the prohibition on failed-login vocabulary. ⚠ **The API returns values, never sentences** — rendered prose stays in the i18n catalog under the en/hi parity gate.
- **AC10** gains the express **preservation** of unauthenticated access to public Trust content, the requirement that the surface render **from the AC4 payload** rather than hardcoded strings, and the constraint that the surface must **not** sit inside an authenticated navigator or shell.
- **AC12 is added**: a test proving the path establishes **no session and no member privileges**, written to defeat the specific future mistake the Panel named — *"a normal session created merely because OTP authentication succeeded."* It asserts positively (no token, no refresh/trusted-device row, `issueFullSession` not invoked, payload present, and anything returned is unusable on a subsequent call), verifies a **correct** OTP so it cannot pass for the wrong reason, and carries a **revert-sanity pair**. A second test pins the flag-OFF default, which is the shipped truth until the flip.
- **A ruling block is added at the head of the Acceptance Criteria** pinning all six outcomes plus the annex, so every conditional in the story reads against known values. Branches not taken are retained as **the reasoning that motivated the ruling**, not as live options — the Story 10.18 note's posture.

#### 5. [Author-committed] Mechanical records

**No Niyamavali text changes by this entry** — §8.4 and §8.4a stand exactly as ratified at `097` clauses 9–10, and no further amendment is authored or implied. **No permission grant, no `TERMINAL_STATES` change, no `architecture.md` amendment** (Decision `2026-08-04-072` clause 3). **No standing Trustee Panel obligation is discharged**, and none is added: the queue stands at **seven** as recorded at `097` clause 13. This entry **unblocks** the capability-bar admission and the implementation half; it closes nothing.

### Decision 2026-08-10-097: Trustee Panel rules Story 10.19's six questions and the Q6(b) mechanism annex — D5 is ratified into Part 8, restoration from termination becomes a Panel-exclusive act, the termination notice stays in-app-only, and the login block ships behind a default-OFF flag whose flip is gated on Story 10.21; Niyamavali §8.4 is amended and §8.4a authored in both locales and reproduced verbatim here, because `docs/legal/` is untracked and this entry is its only durable copy

**Decision type:** Other — six pre-implementation Trustee-Panel rulings on Story 10.19, plus an affirmation of the Q6(b) mechanism annex and a Panel-authored direction on the member-facing termination experience; includes the text of a Niyamavali amendment that leaves no diff
**Status:** **Trustee-ratified** as to clauses 1–8 (the six rulings, the annex affirmation, and the member-facing direction) and as to the §8.4 / §8.4a texts at clauses 9–10. **Author-committed** as to clauses 11–14 (readings, findings, non-discharges and mechanical records). See the per-clause provenance table below — this labelling is mandatory under Decision `2026-08-09-095`.
**Author:** BigDev, via `trustee-panel-routing-note-2026-08-10-story-10-19.md`, on Story 10.19's AC1, AC2 and AC3
**Ratifying trustees:** Trustee Panel
**Date:** 2026-08-10

---

#### Context

Story 10.19's epic acceptance criterion opens *"Given D5's **ratified** principles"*. **D5 was not ratified.** Decision `2026-08-04-072` ratified only the architecture-recording scope of the moderation-model correct-course and is explicit that it is a process and documentation-convention decision. Niyamavali §8.4 consisted of two clauses — reinstatement-only recovery and the 12-month rejoin lock — and said nothing about membership privileges, historical record, or statutory rights. **This entry supplies the missing ratification**, on the Story 10.18 shape: governance first, implementation second, with the routing note committed alone before any `packages/` or `apps/` change.

**First, this entry cannot and does not make Part 8 legally settled.** The Niyamavali remains an unadopted draft — `niyamavali.md:5` carries `[[v1.0]]`, `[[date]]` and `[[date]]`, none filled — and counsel is not engaged: every return field in `docs/legal-counsel-engagement/` is a placeholder, and Story 0.13 has not closed. **No version bump, effective date, or `[LEGAL]` acceptance line is written by this entry, and none may be inferred from it.** The amendment is *authored and Trustee-ratified*; counsel review *remains outstanding* (clause 5).

**Second, the routing note was amended twice before this ruling, and the Panel ruled on the amended version.** The first amendment corrected an overstated benefit in Q6: the note originally credited option (a) with *"closes ESCALATION 3 immediately"*, which was too strong — the login block ends **one account's** authenticated access and does not prevent the same person re-registering on a different mobile number. The second addition was the Q6(b) mechanism annex, at the Panel's request. Both are recorded in the note's status header. **This entry ratifies the corrected question set.**

**Third, one ruling went against the author's recommendation, and is recorded as such.** Q3 was recommended (a) — a mandatory off-portal channel — and the Panel ruled **(b)**, in-app-only. That is a Panel ruling, not a default taken, and clause 3 records it as a ruling with its consequence stated in full rather than softened.

#### Per-clause provenance (mandatory under Decision `2026-08-09-095`)

| Clause | Provenance |
|---|---|
| 1–6 | **Trustee-ratified** — the six question rulings, each at a named option |
| 7 | **Trustee-ratified** — affirmation of the three Q6(b) annex properties |
| 8 | **Trustee-ratified** — Panel-authored direction, reproduced verbatim; **not** selected from any option the note offered |
| 9–10 | **Trustee-ratified** — the amended §8.4 and the new §8.4a, both locales |
| 11 | **[Author-committed]** — the reading adopted for clause 8, and the alternative reading it forecloses |
| 12 | **[Author-committed]** — consequences that follow mechanically from clauses 1–8 |
| 13 | **[Author-committed]** — what this entry does NOT do |
| 14 | **[Author-committed]** — no existing Decision body is edited |

---

#### The rulings

1. **Q1 — Option (a) ratified: restoration from a TERMINATED member requires a formal act of the Trustee Panel.** §8.4 states it **expressly** (clause 9), because Decision `2026-08-10-096` clause 3 ruled the Panel's Part 8 authority **concurrent, not exclusive** — so silence would have left reinstatement available to any authority Part 8 names. This is the **first stated exclusivity** in Part 8. Implementation adds a **precondition on the caller** at `performAction`'s legality path (`apps/api/src/modules/member-moderation/handlers.ts:174-258`); ⛔ the `terminated --restore--> none` arm at `status.ts:47-48` is **not removed**.
   ⚠ **This question's second deposit is hereby discharged.** Decision `2026-08-10-096` clause 8 deferred it to Story 10.19 as an acceptance criterion; it was carried as **AC3** and is answered here. `deferred-work.md:3593` closes with **Resolved via explicit ruling** — not *Closed by edit*, per `[[feedback_closure_language_precision]]`. **It is not carried forward.**

2. **Q2 — Option (a) ratified: the termination notice's *Summary* line is a first-class ABSENT element** until Story 10.20's structured `decision_note` exists. It is `{ available: false }`-shaped (the `[[project_nominee_vpa_deferred_seam]]` precedent), ⛔ **never an empty string rendered as prose**. The notice carries Decision, Ground (a resolved label), Effective date and Further communication meanwhile. No member-facing field is minted now and restructured by 10.20's WS-B.

3. **Q3 — Option (b) ratified: NO off-portal channel guarantee. The termination notice is in-app-only.** ⚠ **Ruled against the author's recommendation**, and recorded as a ruling rather than a default. **Consequence, stated in full and not softened:** the one communication a terminated member receives is delivered to a surface they can no longer authenticate into. Once the Q6 flag is enabled, an in-app-only notice reaches a member who cannot open the app in an authenticated state. **AC11 must record this as a ruled-upon reach limitation**, with the new `CostOptimizationInput` per-alert exemption field named as the **re-trigger** — never left implicit. ⛔ No `packages/channels` contract change is authorised by this ruling; `time_critical` stays `false`; no 10th `AlertCategory` is minted; the shared `alert_published` `windowMsByCategory` lever is **not** touched.
   ⚠ **This ruling interacts with clause 8.** The Panel's direction requires a terminated member to be able to *see* a dedicated termination notice. Clause 3 governs **push/SMS/WhatsApp fan-out**; clause 8 governs **what the member sees on the surface itself**. They are not in conflict, and clause 8 is not a route around clause 3.

4. **Q4 — Option (a) ratified: §8.4a lands COMPLETE, with an explicit mechanization-status disclosure.** All twelve rows are stated (clause 10). The table is a **constitutional statement, not a status report on the codebase**; a partial table would make the Niyamavali describe the implementation rather than govern it. The disclosure names **four** unmechanized rows with their owners — escalation justification (**10.20**), prior sanction required (**10.20**), notice-and-opportunity-to-respond (**10.20 / 10.22**), and portal access (**built, not enabled** — clause 6). ⚠ Per `[[feedback_mechanization_split_commitment]]`, decay concentrates in the un-mechanized half; the owners are named in the instrument itself, not left to a general "a later story".
   **And no `APPENDIX A — RULE INDEX` entry is added in either locale** — §8.4a is a comparison, not an indexable `R`-rule (the Decision `2026-08-10-096` clause 10 precedent). **The absence is recorded here so a later reader does not read it as an omission.** The `niyamavali.md:196` reserved-numbers note is **unchanged**: it reserves §8.5, §8.6, §8.8 and §8.9, and §8.4a is not among them.

5. **Q5 — Option (a) ratified: Decision `2026-08-06-080`'s Panel-attestation-only precedent extends to §8.4 and §8.4a.** The amendment proceeds on Panel ratification alone, and **counsel review of §8.4/§8.4a is recorded as OWED and un-attested**, per `[[feedback_record_unattested_no_backfill]]`. ⚠ **This is the third amendment landing on attestation alone** (`080` → `096` §8.7 → this entry). The Panel notes the count rather than letting it accumulate silently: Part 11 requires counsel review before publication, but Story 0.13 has not closed, no counsel is selected, and waiting has no termination condition. **Under no reading does this clause constitute counsel acceptance.**

6. **Q6 — Option (b) ratified with sub-choice (b-i): the login block ships behind a DEFAULT-OFF flag, and the flip is authorised once Story 10.21 lands.** The mechanism lands built, tested and revert-proven; the flip is a **single auditable act**, gated on the off-portal DPDPA route existing. The `sprint-change-proposal-2026-08-04.md:156` release gate is **honoured, not withdrawn**.
   ⛔ **The flip is NOT gated on the identity-collision control** (sub-choice (b-ii), not ratified). The Panel rules (b-i) **knowingly**: under it, the Niyamavali states that termination *"ends all membership privileges and authenticated member access"* while the system, once enabled, enforces that **against one account and not one person**. **AC11 must record that as a ruled-upon limitation.** The identity-collision gap is recorded in `deferred-work.md` with an unassigned owner and its own re-trigger, and **nothing in this entry discharges it**.

7. **Q6(b) mechanism annex — all three properties AFFIRMED as stated.**
   (i) ⭐ **The capability bar is extended into AUTHENTICATION, and that extension is this ruling, not a side effect of it.** `apps/api/src/modules/auth/` is not among `governance_boundary.yaml`'s prohibited roots — not because it was cleared, but because no story had proposed a flag there. A passing source scan proves the root is unlisted, **not** that the behaviour is admissible. The Panel authorises the admission expressly.
   (ii) **The block FAILS OPEN, and that is accepted.** `fallbackDefault: false` is required by the default-OFF intent; every degraded path — no version in force, malformed cohort rule, lookup error — resolves to *do not block*. ⛔ The `allow` entry's `rationale` must **trace** that polarity, not assert it: the `kyc_manual_fallback` attestation stated the opposite of its own code through three review passes.
   (iii) **The flag is named for the OVERLAY, not the lifecycle.** `parseCapabilityBar` rejects any artifact containing `member_lifecycle` (freeze row 2), and the block is deliberately an overlay read with `members.state` untouched. Kind is `member_flow` — the closest fit of three declared kinds, and **that classification is itself a small governance act**, named here rather than left for a later auditor.
   ⚠ **Recorded honestly:** the three annex properties were posed as prose, not as lettered option tables. The Panel answered *"A/A/A"*, which is recorded as **affirmation of each property as written in the annex**. If the Panel intended anything narrower, it should say so before the capability-bar entry is authored; the entry is not written until then.

8. **⭐ Member-facing termination experience — Panel direction, reproduced verbatim.** This clause was **authored by the Panel** and was not selected from any option the routing note offered:

   > The default-OFF termination-access flag is intended to produce a controlled termination state, not a generic authentication failure. Once enabled, a terminated member must be prevented from entering authenticated member surfaces while still being able to see a dedicated termination notice and public trust content. The notice-only path must not establish normal member privileges.
   >
   > Existing access-token residual remains governed by the documented TTL limitation; refresh tokens and trusted devices are revoked at termination.
   >
   > The implementation must distinguish "termination notice access" from ordinary authenticated member access.

---

#### 9. The ratified text of Niyamavali §8.4, reproduced verbatim in both locales

Amended in `docs/legal/niyamavali.md` and `docs/legal/niyamavali.hi.md`. **Both files are untracked; this reproduction is the record.** The Hindi text is a co-equal governing instrument, not a translation artifact (`counsel-roster.md:32`: *"The Niyamavali is Hindi-primary"*). The existing two clauses are **retained**; three paragraphs are added, the third being the Q1(a) ruling.

**English —**

> ### 8.4 Termination
> Termination is recoverable **only by explicit Trustee reinstatement**, and is subject to the **[[12-month]] rejoin lock** (§2.5, FR-6).
>
> **Termination ends membership, not history.** The Trust shall preserve the historical record of the member's participation, moderation decisions and financial transactions in accordance with its retention obligations, while ending all membership privileges and authenticated member access.
>
> **Statutory rights survive termination.** Termination ends authenticated member access. Any statutory rights of access, correction, portability or erasure shall be exercised through an **identity-verified administrative process designated by the Trust**.
>
> **Restoration from termination is an act of the Trustee Panel.** The *explicit Trustee reinstatement* named above is an act of the Trustee Panel sitting as such under §8.7 — not an exercise of the individual **Trustee discretion** by which a *suspended* member is restored under §8.3. This requirement is stated **expressly**, because the Panel's authority under this Part is otherwise **concurrent** and not exclusive (§8.7): absent this clause, silence would leave reinstatement available to any authority this Part names. No other authority named in this Part may reinstate a terminated member.

**Hindi —**

> ### 8.4 समाप्ति
> समाप्ति केवल **स्पष्ट ट्रस्टी पुनर्नियुक्ति** से ही उलट सकती है, और **[[12-माह]] पुनः-सम्मिलन रोक** के अधीन है (§2.5, FR-6)।
>
> **समाप्ति सदस्यता समाप्त करती है, इतिहास नहीं।** ट्रस्ट सदस्य की भागीदारी, मॉडरेशन निर्णयों एवं वित्तीय लेन-देनों का ऐतिहासिक अभिलेख अपने प्रतिधारण (retention) दायित्वों के अनुरूप सुरक्षित रखेगा, जबकि समस्त सदस्यता-विशेषाधिकार एवं अधिप्रमाणित (authenticated) सदस्य-पहुँच समाप्त हो जाएँगे।
>
> **वैधानिक अधिकार समाप्ति के उपरांत भी बने रहते हैं।** समाप्ति अधिप्रमाणित सदस्य-पहुँच समाप्त करती है। पहुँच, सुधार, सुवाह्यता (portability) अथवा विलोपन (erasure) के किसी भी वैधानिक अधिकार का प्रयोग **ट्रस्ट द्वारा निर्दिष्ट एक पहचान-सत्यापित प्रशासनिक प्रक्रिया** के माध्यम से किया जाएगा।
>
> **समाप्ति से पुनर्स्थापन ट्रस्टी पैनल का कार्य है।** ऊपर उल्लिखित *स्पष्ट ट्रस्टी पुनर्नियुक्ति* §8.7 के अंतर्गत ट्रस्टी पैनल का, उसी रूप में बैठकर किया गया, कार्य है — न कि वह व्यक्तिगत **ट्रस्टी विवेकाधिकार** जिससे §8.3 के अंतर्गत किसी *निलंबित* सदस्य को पुनर्स्थापित किया जाता है। यह अपेक्षा **स्पष्टतः** कही गई है, क्योंकि इस भाग के अंतर्गत पैनल का प्राधिकार अन्यथा **समवर्ती (concurrent)** है, अनन्य नहीं (§8.7): इस खंड के अभाव में मौन रहने पर पुनर्नियुक्ति इस भाग में नामित किसी भी प्राधिकारी के लिए उपलब्ध रह जाती। इस भाग में नामित कोई अन्य प्राधिकारी किसी समाप्त सदस्य को पुनर्स्थापित नहीं कर सकता।

#### 10. The ratified text of Niyamavali §8.4a, reproduced verbatim in both locales

Inserted after §8.4 in both files, ahead of §8.7. Twelve rows per Q4(a), with the mechanization-status disclosure.

**English —**

> ### 8.4a Suspension and termination compared
>
> Suspension and termination differ **in kind at every dimension below, not in degree**. This section is a comparison, not a rule; it does not itself confer or remove an authority, and nothing in it displaces §8.2, §8.3 or §8.4.
>
> | Dimension | **Suspension** | **Termination** |
> |---|---|---|
> | **Nature** | A corrective sanction | **An exceptional governance act** |
> | **Test** | Restoration is realistically possible | The trust relationship is **fundamentally and irreparably broken**, or a continuing material risk persists |
> | **Curable by the member?** | **Yes** — rule-clearance or trustee discretion | **No** — recoverable only by explicit Trustee reinstatement |
> | **Donor roster** | **Remains on it** — the obligation to contribute survives, because it is the mechanism of cure (§3.3) | **Removed** |
> | **Beneficiary entitlement** | Withdrawn during suspension | Ended |
> | **Portal access** | **Retained** — the member is curing and needs the contribution surface | **Ended** — all membership privileges and authenticated access cease |
> | **Membership** | Continues | **Ends.** History does not — the record is preserved per retention obligations |
> | **Statutory rights (DPDPA)** | Exercised through the portal | **Survive**, exercised through an identity-verified administrative process |
> | **Escalation justification** | Not required | **Mandatory** — two-part: why suspension is inadequate, *and* why termination is proportionate |
> | **Prior sanction required?** | No | **Yes** — follows a prior suspension, absent an express Niyamavali exception |
> | **Notice + opportunity to respond** | Notice required | **Notice and an opportunity to respond** must precede the act; the notice is **self-contained** |
> | **Rejoin** | Not applicable — membership continues | **12-month rejoin lock** (§2.5, FR-6) |
>
> > **Mechanization status.** This table states the constitutional distinction in full. **Four of its rows are not yet enforced by any system workflow**, and are recorded here as stated-but-unmechanized rather than omitted:
> >
> > · **Escalation justification** — no two-part justification is captured at the point of termination.
> > · **Prior sanction required** — no precondition requires a prior suspension.
> > · **Notice + opportunity to respond** — notice is sent; **no opportunity to respond precedes the act**.
> > · **Portal access — "Ended"** — authenticated access is ended by a control that is **built but not enabled**; until it is enabled, a terminated member retains portal access.
> >
> > A row's presence in this table states what the Trust holds to be right, not what the system currently does. **The distinction is deliberate and is not to be read as an assertion of compliance.**

**Hindi —**

> ### 8.4a निलंबन एवं समाप्ति की तुलना
>
> निलंबन एवं समाप्ति नीचे दिए गए **प्रत्येक आयाम पर प्रकार में भिन्न हैं, मात्रा में नहीं**। यह धारा एक तुलना है, नियम नहीं; यह स्वयं कोई प्राधिकार न प्रदान करती है न हटाती है, और इसमें कुछ भी §8.2, §8.3 अथवा §8.4 का स्थान नहीं लेता।
>
> | आयाम | **निलंबन** | **समाप्ति** |
> |---|---|---|
> | **स्वरूप** | एक सुधारात्मक प्रतिबंध | **एक असाधारण शासन-कार्य** |
> | **कसौटी** | पुनर्स्थापन वास्तविक रूप से संभव है | विश्वास-संबंध **मूलभूत एवं अपूरणीय रूप से भंग** है, अथवा सतत भौतिक जोखिम बना हुआ है |
> | **क्या सदस्य द्वारा सुधार्य?** | **हाँ** — नियम-अनुपालन अथवा ट्रस्टी विवेकाधिकार | **नहीं** — केवल स्पष्ट ट्रस्टी पुनर्नियुक्ति से उलट सकती है |
> | **दाता सूची (Donor roster)** | **बनी रहती है** — सहयोग का दायित्व शेष रहता है, क्योंकि वही सुधार का साधन है (§3.3) | **हटा दी जाती है** |
> | **लाभार्थी पात्रता** | निलंबन-अवधि में वापस ले ली जाती है | समाप्त |
> | **पोर्टल-पहुँच** | **बनी रहती है** — सदस्य सुधार-पथ पर है और उसे सहयोग-सतह चाहिए | **समाप्त** — समस्त सदस्यता-विशेषाधिकार एवं अधिप्रमाणित पहुँच समाप्त |
> | **सदस्यता** | जारी रहती है | **समाप्त होती है।** इतिहास नहीं — अभिलेख प्रतिधारण दायित्वों के अनुरूप सुरक्षित रहता है |
> | **वैधानिक अधिकार (DPDPA)** | पोर्टल के माध्यम से प्रयुक्त | **बने रहते हैं**, एक पहचान-सत्यापित प्रशासनिक प्रक्रिया के माध्यम से प्रयुक्त |
> | **वृद्धि-औचित्य (Escalation justification)** | अपेक्षित नहीं | **अनिवार्य** — दो-भागीय: निलंबन क्यों अपर्याप्त है, *और* समाप्ति क्यों समानुपातिक है |
> | **पूर्व प्रतिबंध अपेक्षित?** | नहीं | **हाँ** — किसी पूर्व निलंबन के पश्चात्, जब तक नियमावली में स्पष्ट अपवाद न हो |
> | **सूचना एवं उत्तर का अवसर** | सूचना अपेक्षित | कार्य से **पूर्व सूचना एवं उत्तर का अवसर** आवश्यक; सूचना **स्वतःपूर्ण** हो |
> | **पुनः-सम्मिलन** | लागू नहीं — सदस्यता जारी है | **12-माह पुनः-सम्मिलन रोक** (§2.5, FR-6) |
>
> > **यंत्रीकरण-स्थिति (Mechanization status)।** यह तालिका संवैधानिक भेद को पूर्णतः कहती है। **इसकी चार पंक्तियाँ अभी किसी सिस्टम-कार्यप्रवाह द्वारा प्रवर्तित नहीं हैं**, और उन्हें छोड़ने के बजाय कहा-गया-किंतु-अयंत्रीकृत के रूप में यहाँ अभिलिखित किया गया है:
> >
> > · **वृद्धि-औचित्य** — समाप्ति के समय कोई दो-भागीय औचित्य अभिलिखित नहीं होता।
> > · **पूर्व प्रतिबंध अपेक्षित** — किसी पूर्व निलंबन की कोई पूर्वशर्त लागू नहीं है।
> > · **सूचना एवं उत्तर का अवसर** — सूचना भेजी जाती है; **कार्य से पूर्व उत्तर का कोई अवसर नहीं** है।
> > · **पोर्टल-पहुँच — "समाप्त"** — अधिप्रमाणित पहुँच एक ऐसे नियंत्रण से समाप्त होती है जो **निर्मित है किंतु सक्रिय नहीं**; जब तक वह सक्रिय नहीं होता, समाप्त सदस्य की पोर्टल-पहुँच बनी रहती है।
> >
> > इस तालिका में किसी पंक्ति की उपस्थिति यह कहती है कि ट्रस्ट क्या उचित मानता है, यह नहीं कि सिस्टम वर्तमान में क्या करता है। **यह भेद जानबूझकर है और इसे अनुपालन के दावे के रूप में नहीं पढ़ा जाना है।**

#### 11. [Author-committed] The reading adopted for clause 8, and the alternative it forecloses

⚠ **Clause 8's direction admits two implementations, and the story adopts one. This is recorded rather than decided silently, because the two differ by an entire auth primitive.**

- **⭐ ADOPTED — no session is minted.** Authentication succeeds (the caller proved possession of the OTP for that mobile, so they *are* the member); **issuance of a member session is refused**. The `403 auth.member_terminated` response carries the notice elements, and the client renders a dedicated termination surface from that payload — the technique of the existing `auth.rejoin_locked` precedent, at the outer catch (`apps/mobile/app/(auth)/otp.tsx:73-79`). Public trust content is already unauthenticated and needs nothing. This satisfies every element of clause 8: the member cannot enter authenticated member surfaces (no session exists), sees a dedicated notice, reaches public content, and **"the notice-only path must not establish normal member privileges"** is satisfied by establishing **none**.
- **⛔ FORECLOSED — a restricted notice-only session.** A second session class with its own token audience, refresh semantics and RLS posture would be a **new authentication primitive**, materially larger than this story owns and itself deserving capability-bar-grade scrutiny. It is also strictly worse against clause 8's own words: a restricted session *does* establish privileges, and would then need a mechanism proving they are not "normal" ones.

⛔ **"Distinguish termination notice access from ordinary authenticated member access" is therefore satisfied structurally, not by a flag on a session** — one path issues a session and the other does not. If the Panel intended the restricted-session reading, it must say so **before** implementation begins, because that is a different story.

#### 12. [Author-committed] Consequences that follow mechanically from clauses 1–8

- **The story's ACs change.** Clause 8 is not covered by any existing acceptance criterion. AC4 must produce a response payload sufficient to render the notice (not merely an error code); AC10 must render the dedicated surface and a route to public trust content; and a new criterion must pin, as a **testable property**, that the notice path establishes no member privileges. These are recorded as story edits, not as re-ratifications.
- **Under (b-i) the flag ships default-OFF, so AC7's test posture is the doubled one**: `moderation-auth-effects.spec.ts:242` asserts `403` + `auth.member_terminated` **with the flag forced ON**, *and* a second test pins that with the flag **OFF** the member still logs in (`200`). ⛔ A test asserting `403` while the shipped flag defaults OFF is a **false green**.
- **AC8's and AC9's copy corrections are flag-gated.** Until the flip, *"You can sign in as usual…"* remains **true** of the shipped default. The copy edit and the five-site sweep must describe the flag's default as the shipped truth, or defer to the flip and be recorded in AC11 as flag-gated. ⛔ Neither is a licence to sweep first and reconcile later.
- **Clause 6 does not authorise the flip.** It authorises the flag's **admission and default-OFF shipping**. The flip requires Story 10.21 to have landed, and remains a separate act.

#### 13. [Author-committed] What this entry does NOT do

It does not version the Niyamavali, set an effective date, record a Board resolution, or constitute counsel acceptance (see Context). It does not amend §8.1, §8.2, §8.3, §8.7 or Part 9, and does not land §8.5, §8.6, §8.8 or §8.9 — those remain reserved and owned by **10.20** and **10.22**. It does not remove or add any permission grant. It does not touch the five `TERMINAL_STATES` Sets. **It does not close the DPDPA gap** — Story 10.21 remains `backlog` and is now the named gate on the Q6 flip. **It does not unblock Story 10.22** — the appeal CTA still has no moderation destination; this entry removes Decision 6's *justification*, not the *need* for an appeal route. **And it discharges none of the standing Trustee Panel obligations**, which now number **five**: Story 10.23's Escalation 6 (`2026-08-07-089`), the copy-truth defect (still unassigned), R7(A)'s unpublished Part 11 amendment (`2026-08-06-077`), the R7(C)/R7(F) lock-in asymmetry, and counsel review of §8.7 (`2026-08-10-096` clause 5). **This entry adds two more** — counsel review of §8.4/§8.4a (clause 5), and the Q6 flip authorisation itself (clause 6) — bringing the queue to **seven**. Stated as a count rather than as progress.

#### 14. [Author-committed] No existing Decision body is edited by this entry

Per `[[feedback_supersede_never_reinterpret]]`. Decision `2026-08-10-096` is **followed**, not amended: clause 3's concurrency ruling is what makes clause 1's express exclusivity necessary, and clause 8's deferral to Story 10.19 is **discharged** by clause 1 rather than reinterpreted. Decision `2026-08-04-072` clause 3 stands — `architecture.md` is **not** amended; the record is an `AI-10-2` doc block at the point of use. **Decision 6 is superseded, not edited**: a new record supersedes it, and the five live assertion sites are corrected to state the new truth rather than deleted.

#### Follow-ups, each with a named owner or a concrete re-trigger

- **Counsel review of §8.4 and §8.4a remains OWED and un-attested** (clause 5). Recorded in `deferred-work.md`. **Re-trigger:** the first story that receives an actual counsel-review outcome on a Niyamavali amendment; no owner exists today because Story 0.13 has not closed.
- **The Q6 flip is UNAUTHORISED until Story 10.21 lands** (clause 6). **Owner:** Trustee Panel, through a further `.decision-log.md` entry. ⛔ Flipping it without that entry is a governance violation, not a configuration change — the `2026-08-07-089` posture, applied to a second flag.
- **The in-app-only reach limitation** (clause 3). **Re-trigger:** a story that adds the per-alert `CostOptimizationInput` exemption field. **Owner:** unassigned.
- **The identity-collision gap is NOT discharged** and is not this entry's to close. Recorded in `deferred-work.md` with an unassigned owner; its re-trigger fires on the Q6 flip or on any change to the DigiLocker mapper.
- **The access-token residual stands** (clause 8, second paragraph): `revokeAllMemberSessions` revokes refresh tokens and trusted devices but cannot invalidate a live access JWT, whose TTL is `MEMBER_ACCESS_TTL_MS` (default 15 min, `apps/api/src/config.ts:377`). **Governed by the documented TTL limitation, per the Panel's direction.** ⛔ Not closed by shortening the TTL, by touching the `TERMINAL_STATES` Sets, or by inventing a token denylist.
- **The four unmechanized §8.4a rows** (clause 4). **Owners named in the instrument:** 10.20 (escalation justification, prior sanction), 10.20/10.22 (notice and opportunity to respond), and the Q6 flip (portal access).

### Decision 2026-08-10-096: Trustee Panel rules on its own constitution as a Part 8 sanctioning authority — the Panel is the Board in a moderation capacity, adopts and extends the existing §1.3 body, and holds authority CONCURRENT with the authorities Part 8 already names; Niyamavali §8.7 is authored in both locales and reproduced verbatim here, because `docs/legal/` is untracked and this entry is its only durable copy

**Decision type:** Other — eight pre-implementation Trustee-Panel rulings constituting the Panel itself, ratified together because each settles a governance fact that the implementation would otherwise settle by default; includes the text of a Niyamavali amendment that leaves no diff
**Status:** Trustee-ratified as to clauses 1–8 (the eight rulings) and as to the §8.7 text at clause 9. **Author-committed** as to clauses 10–12 (findings, non-discharges, and mechanical records). See the per-clause provenance table below — this labelling is mandatory under Decision `2026-08-09-095`.
**Author:** BigDev, via `trustee-panel-routing-note-2026-08-10-story-10-18.md`, on Story 10.18's AC1 and AC2
**Ratifying trustees:** Trustee Panel

**Context:**
The Trustee Panel has exercised authority this system cannot recognise. Decision `2026-08-07-089` states that the Panel *"exclusively owns authorization to activate Story 10.23's imposition flag. No other role holds it, and it is not delegable by the holder of any adjacent responsibility."* Eight ratified Decisions in this log are signed *"Ratifying trustees: Trustee Panel."* Yet there is no `trustee_panel` among the twelve seeded role bundles, no permission a Panel member could hold **as** a Panel member, and therefore no way for the system to distinguish a Panel act from a `pariwar_admin` act. **Every exclusivity this log asserts has been enforced by convention alone.**

Story 10.18 exists to close that gap, and `epics.md:3721` fixes the order: *"this is not an RBAC task. Its subject is who holds the authority to sanction a member; the permission key is the last and smallest step in expressing that … A role bundle preceding that definition would be a capability without a constituted holder."* The story is `ready-for-dev` and **no code has been written**; it halts before implementation until this entry exists.

Three facts shape what this entry is and what it is not.

**First, `docs/legal/` is gitignored** (`.gitignore:67-68`; `git ls-files docs/legal/` returns nothing). The Niyamavali has no git history. Amending it produces **no commit, no diff, and no reviewable trail**. This entry is therefore not a summary of the amendment — **it is the only durable copy of it**, and §8.7 is reproduced verbatim below in both locales for that reason. The precedent is exact: Decision `2026-08-06-080` amended §3.1/Appendix A the same way and for the same reason.

**Second, the premise the epic carried was wrong, and correcting it changed the work.** A prior planning document (`sprint-change-proposal-2026-08-04.md:67`, "V3") stated that *"the Niyamavali does not define a 'Trustee Panel.'"* It does. `niyamavali.md:39` (§1.3) defines *"**District Admin / State Trustee / Trustee Panel (Core Team)** — the three escalation tiers of decision authority (Part 9)"*, mirrored in Hindi at `:43`, with §5.3 (`:126`) adding that the Panel *"retains full discretion"*. So the term exists, is equated with "Core Team", sits at the **top** of three tiers above State Trustee, and is scoped to **Part 9 only**. The work was therefore **extension and reconciliation, not invention** — and the risk was that a §8.7 silent about §1.3 would leave the instrument carrying two incompatible Trustee Panels. That risk is what Q2 exists to close. *(The epic's own wording, `epics.md:3725`, is Part-8-scoped and correct; the error is V3's, and this entry does not attribute it to the epic.)*

**Third, this entry cannot and does not make Part 8 legally settled.** The Niyamavali is an unadopted draft — `niyamavali.md:5` carries `[[v1.0]]`, `[[date]]` and `[[date]]`, none filled — and counsel is not engaged: every return field in `docs/legal-counsel-engagement/` is a placeholder. Part 11 requires a Board-resolution reference and counsel review before publication. **No version bump, effective date, or `[LEGAL]` acceptance line is written by this entry, and none may be inferred from it.** The amendment is *authored and Trustee-ratified*; counsel review *remains outstanding*.

**Decision:**

1. **Q1 — Option (a) ratified: the Trustee Panel is the Board of Trustees acting in a moderation capacity, not a Clause-20(h) delegate committee.** Its authority to suspend, terminate, and restore membership is the Board's own power under **Deed Clause 20(b)**; its composition, appointment and tenure are governed by **Deed Clause 18**, and its meetings, quorum and resolutions by **Deed Clause 19**. Those clauses govern **directly**, and §8.7 **cites** them rather than restating their terms — a restated quorum would create a second source of truth that drifts from the Deed. **Consequence the Panel accepts:** moderation is not delegable to a subset of the Board without a further governance act, because option (b) — the shape that would have permitted a smaller sitting — was considered and not taken.

2. **Q2 — Option (a) ratified: §8.7 adopts the existing §1.3 body and extends its scope from Part 9 to Part 8.** It does **not** constitute a new body, and §1.3 is **not** amended. There is one Trustee Panel in this Niyamavali. The extension is stated expressly in §8.7, because silent adoption would read as a coincidence of naming rather than an identity.

3. **Q3 — Option (a) ratified: the Panel's Part 8 sanctioning authority is CONCURRENT, not exclusive.** The Panel is *a* sanctioning authority under Part 8, not the only one. **The authorities Part 8 already names are unaffected and continue to ground the transitions they ground** — a concealment flag confirmed by a **State Trustee** under §8.2, and **Trustee discretion** under §8.3. §8.7 states the concurrency expressly, because a body constituted as *"the body that determines sanctions"* would otherwise be read as exclusive later. **Consequence:** `pariwar_admin`'s live `member.moderate` grant (`roles.ts:238`) **stands and is not removed by Story 10.18**. Exclusivity remains available as a deliberate future act; it is not taken here, and it is not to be inferred from §8.7's existence.

4. **Q4 — Option (a) ratified: the thirteenth seeded role requires no separate OQ-3 confirmation.** `trustee_panel` ships **provisional on the same footing as the other twelve**, which `roles.ts:1-9` already declares for the whole set; OQ-3 will confirm the set including this role, pre-launch. **The provisional status must be stated in the bundle comment and the story's Completion Notes, not assumed** — a role that is the Panel's own must not acquire a firmer status than the twelve by accident.

5. **Q5 — Option (a) ratified: Decision `2026-08-06-080`'s Panel-attestation-only precedent extends to §8.7.** The amendment proceeds on Panel ratification alone, and **counsel review of §8.7 is recorded as OWED and un-attested**, per `[[feedback_record_unattested_no_backfill]]`. The Panel notes the tension explicitly rather than resolving it silently: `sprint-change-proposal-2026-08-04.md:618` names §8.7 as legal counsel's critical-path deliverable, and Part 11 (`niyamavali.md:211`) requires counsel review before publication — but Story 0.13 has not closed, no counsel is selected, and waiting has no termination condition. **Under no reading does this clause constitute counsel acceptance.**

6. **Q6 — Option (a) ratified: §1.3's escalation-tier ranking may be cited as an independent corroborating reason** that the Panel is a trust-wide governing body rather than a geographic office. ⚠ **This changes no code and licenses no code comment.** The `scopeCeiling: 'pariwar'` choice rests on the rank ordering alone — `scopeWithinCeiling` (`packages/domain/src/rbac/scope.ts:74-79`) is a pure numeric compare in which a `state`, `district` or `block` ceiling can never satisfy a `pariwar`-dimension check — and **the corroborating argument does not enter `roles.ts` under this or any ruling**, because a code comment cannot display ratification status. The citation is permitted in the story and the routing note only.

7. **Q7 — Option (a) ratified: `verifier` retains its `member.moderate` grant, recorded as a deliberate deferral with an acceptance condition.** The grant is **inert today**: `verifier` holds the key at `scopeCeiling: 'district'` (`roles.ts:436-437`) while the only route gating it checks `{ dimension: 'pariwar' }` (`member-moderation/routes.ts:112`), so `scopeWithinCeiling('pariwar','district')` is `1 >= 3` → false. It is not removed, because removal changes who may moderate and is the less reversible direction. **The deferral must be pinned by an assertion that can actually observe the grant** — the shipped `check.test.ts` deferral-pin form builds a synthetic bundle so the proof is catalog-independent, and therefore passes identically whether or not the grant exists; a second, catalog-dependent assertion is required.

8. **Q8 — Option (a) ratified: whether restoring a TERMINATED member requires a formal Panel ceremony is deferred to Story 10.19**, which must carry it **as an acceptance criterion, not as a note**. Today's single-actor `trustee-discretion` path stands meanwhile. ⚠ **This is the question's second deposit.** Story 10.17's review deposited it at `deferred-work.md:3260` with the re-trigger *"Story 10.18 lands and defines what 'sanctioning authority' actually gates"* — that trigger has now fired. A deferral to a story that does not carry it as an AC is how it lapsed the first time.

9. **⭐ The ratified text of Niyamavali §8.7, reproduced verbatim in both locales.** Authored into `docs/legal/niyamavali.md` after §8.4 and into `docs/legal/niyamavali.hi.md` at the structurally identical position. **Both files are untracked; this reproduction is the record.** The Hindi text is a co-equal governing instrument, not a translation artifact (`counsel-roster.md:32`: *"The Niyamavali is Hindi-primary"*).

   **English —**

   > ### 8.7 The Trustee Panel
   >
   > **The body.** The Trustee Panel is the body defined at **§1.3** — the *Core Team*, the highest of the three escalation tiers of decision authority. This section does **not** constitute a new body. It **extends the scope of the §1.3 Trustee Panel from Part 9 to this Part**, so that the same body which sits as the final internal tier in grievance and appeal is also the body that determines moderation sanctions under this Part. There is one Trustee Panel in this Niyamavali, and this is it.
   >
   > **Its constitution.** The Trustee Panel is **the Board of Trustees acting in a moderation capacity**, and not a delegate committee. Its authority to suspend, terminate, and restore membership is the Board's own power under **Deed Clause 20(b)**. Its composition, appointment, and tenure are governed by **Deed Clause 18**; its meetings, quorum, and manner of resolution by **Deed Clause 19**. Those clauses govern directly. This Niyamavali does not restate their terms, and where this document and the Deed differ, the Deed prevails (§1.2).
   >
   > **Its authority under this Part is concurrent, not exclusive.** The Panel is *a* sanctioning authority under this Part; it is not the only one. The authorities named elsewhere in this Part are unaffected — a concealment flag **confirmed by a State Trustee** under §8.2, and **Trustee discretion** under §8.3 — and each continues to ground the transition it grounds. Nothing in this section removes an authority conferred elsewhere in this Part, and nothing in it requires a Panel act for a transition that this Part permits another authority to make.
   >
   > **The Trustee Panel is not the "State Trustee panel" of Part 9.** Part 9, Stage 2 names a *State Trustee panel* — a geographic body voting by majority under **R9**. The Trustee Panel constituted here is a **trust-wide governing body, not a geographic office**, and the two differ in composition, in scope, and in the manner in which they act. In particular, the quorum and voting rule stated for the Part 9 State Trustee panel **do not apply** to the Trustee Panel, whose quorum is that of Deed Clause 19.
   >
   > **Record.** Every exercise of the Panel's authority under this Part is audit-logged with rationale under §8.1, and is attributable to the Panel as such.
   >
   > > *§8.5, §8.6, §8.8 and §8.9 are reserved. §8.7 is deliberately numbered ahead of them; the intervening numbers are held for the remaining Part 8 amendments and are not to be closed up.*

   **Hindi —**

   > ### 8.7 ट्रस्टी पैनल (Trustee Panel)
   >
   > **निकाय।** ट्रस्टी पैनल वही निकाय है जो **§1.3** में परिभाषित है — *Core Team*, निर्णय-प्राधिकार के तीन उत्तरोत्तर स्तरों में सर्वोच्च। यह धारा कोई **नया** निकाय गठित नहीं करती। यह **§1.3 के ट्रस्टी पैनल के क्षेत्र को भाग 9 से बढ़ाकर इस भाग तक** विस्तारित करती है, जिससे वही निकाय जो शिकायत एवं अपील में अंतिम आंतरिक स्तर के रूप में बैठता है, इस भाग के अंतर्गत मॉडरेशन प्रतिबंध भी निर्धारित करता है। इस नियमावली में एक ही ट्रस्टी पैनल है, और वह यही है।
   >
   > **इसका गठन।** ट्रस्टी पैनल **मॉडरेशन-क्षमता में कार्यरत न्यासी मंडल (Board of Trustees)** है, न कि कोई प्रत्यायोजित समिति। सदस्यता को निलंबित, समाप्त एवं पुनर्स्थापित करने का इसका प्राधिकार **डीड खंड 20(b)** के अंतर्गत मंडल की अपनी शक्ति है। इसकी संरचना, नियुक्ति एवं कार्यकाल **डीड खंड 18** से, तथा इसकी बैठकें, गणपूर्ति (quorum) एवं संकल्प-प्रक्रिया **डीड खंड 19** से शासित होती हैं। वे खंड प्रत्यक्षतः लागू होते हैं। यह नियमावली उनकी शर्तों को दोहराती नहीं, और जहाँ यह दस्तावेज़ एवं डीड भिन्न हों, वहाँ डीड प्रभावी होगा (§1.2)।
   >
   > **इस भाग के अंतर्गत इसका प्राधिकार समवर्ती (concurrent) है, अनन्य (exclusive) नहीं।** पैनल इस भाग के अंतर्गत *एक* प्रतिबंध-प्राधिकारी है; एकमात्र नहीं। इस भाग में अन्यत्र नामित प्राधिकारी अप्रभावित रहते हैं — §8.2 के अंतर्गत **State Trustee द्वारा पुष्ट** छिपाव-चिह्न, तथा §8.3 के अंतर्गत **ट्रस्टी विवेकाधिकार** — और प्रत्येक उसी संक्रमण का आधार बना रहता है जिसका वह आधार है। इस धारा से इस भाग द्वारा अन्यत्र प्रदत्त कोई प्राधिकार समाप्त नहीं होता, और न ही यह ऐसे किसी संक्रमण हेतु पैनल-कार्य की अनिवार्यता बनाती है जिसे यह भाग किसी अन्य प्राधिकारी को करने की अनुमति देता है।
   >
   > **ट्रस्टी पैनल, भाग 9 का "State Trustee पैनल" नहीं है।** भाग 9 का चरण 2 एक *State Trustee पैनल* नामित करता है — एक भौगोलिक निकाय, जो **R9** के अंतर्गत बहुमत से मत देता है। यहाँ गठित ट्रस्टी पैनल एक **ट्रस्ट-व्यापी शासी निकाय है, कोई भौगोलिक कार्यालय नहीं**, और दोनों संरचना, क्षेत्र एवं कार्य-प्रणाली में भिन्न हैं। विशेष रूप से, भाग 9 के State Trustee पैनल हेतु कथित गणपूर्ति एवं मत-नियम ट्रस्टी पैनल पर **लागू नहीं होते**; उसकी गणपूर्ति डीड खंड 19 की है।
   >
   > **अभिलेख।** इस भाग के अंतर्गत पैनल के प्राधिकार का प्रत्येक प्रयोग §8.1 के अंतर्गत **कारण सहित audit-logged** होता है, और पैनल को उसी रूप में अभिलिखित (attributable) होता है।
   >
   > > *§8.5, §8.6, §8.8 एवं §8.9 आरक्षित हैं। §8.7 जानबूझकर उनसे पहले क्रमांकित है; बीच के क्रमांक भाग 8 के शेष संशोधनों हेतु सुरक्षित हैं और उन्हें भरा नहीं जाना है।*

10. **[Author-committed] No `APPENDIX A — RULE INDEX` entry is added, in either locale.** §8.7 constitutes a body; it does not introduce an indexable `R`-rule. Part 8's existing rule references (`R56 / R6`) are unchanged. Recorded so a later reader does not treat the absence as an omission.

11. **[Author-committed] What this entry does NOT do.** It does not version the Niyamavali, set an effective date, record a Board resolution, or constitute counsel acceptance — see Context. It does not amend §1.3, §8.2, §8.3 or Part 9. It does not remove or add any permission grant. It does not discharge any of the four Trustee Panel obligations already open — Story 10.23's Escalation 6 (`2026-08-07-089`), the copy-truth defect, R7(A)'s unpublished Part 11 amendment (`2026-08-06-077`), or the R7(C)/R7(F) lock-in asymmetry — and this story opens a fifth.

12. **[Author-committed] No existing Decision body is edited by this entry**, per `[[feedback_supersede_never_reinterpret]]`. No prior Decision is superseded: `2026-08-06-080` is followed as precedent, not amended, and `2026-08-07-089`'s Panel-exclusive authority over the Story 10.23 flag is untouched — that exclusivity concerns a different instrument and is unaffected by clause 3's concurrency ruling on Part 8 moderation.

### Decision 2026-08-09-095: Decision `2026-08-09-093` is labelled flatly "Trustee-ratified" but contains AUTHOR-WRITTEN clauses, and `2026-08-09-094` compounded it by asserting "clauses 1–5 stand exactly as ratified" — the ratified scope is clauses 1–3 ONLY, and per-clause provenance is now mandatory on any entry mixing ratification with author analysis

**Decision type:** Other — record correction of provenance labelling. **Ratifies nothing new, reopens nothing, and changes no substantive clause.** Every commitment in `093` and `094` stands exactly as written; only the attribution of WHO committed each one is corrected.
**Status:** Author-committed (record correction). **Amends no Trustee-ratified text.** Corrects the Status lines of `2026-08-09-093` and `2026-08-09-094`.
**Author:** BigDev
**Ratifying trustees:** n/a — this resolves an attribution error against the ratified source. It is not a governance question and was not routed as one.

**Context:**
Found during a review of the 10.23 governance and implementation commits. Decision `2026-08-09-093`'s Status line reads, in full, **"Trustee-ratified."** — with no per-clause qualification. But the entry mixes two kinds of content:

| Clause | What it actually is |
|---|---|
| **1** — Q1 ratified at Option (a) | ✅ Trustee-ratified |
| **2** — Q2 ratified at Option (a) | ✅ Trustee-ratified |
| **3** — the population direction, verbatim | ✅ Trustee-ratified (Panel's own words) |
| **4** — "clause 3 mandates no code change" | ⛔ **AUTHOR analysis.** Code findings with file:line citations (`imposesRestorationObligation`, `RESTORATION_OBLIGATION_KEYS`, R7(G)'s `never_excuses`). The Panel ratified no such thing. |
| **5** — the non-discharge statement | ⛔ **AUTHOR restatement** of constraints ratified elsewhere (`088`, `089`, `092`). Correct in substance; not itself a ratification. |
| **6** — the enabling-Decision form requirement | ⛔ **AUTHOR-written**, and already acknowledged as such by `094` clause 3. It derives from `cohort_empty` behaviour, not from any Panel answer. |
| Open follow-ups | ⛔ **AUTHOR findings.** |

**`2026-08-09-094` then compounded the error.** Its Status line asserts that "`2026-08-07-088`, `089`, and **clauses 1–5** of `2026-08-09-093` stand exactly as ratified" — which sweeps clauses 4 and 5 into the ratified set. Worse, that same entry's own **Decision type** line refers to *"the record correction it forces in the **author-written half** of Decision `2026-08-09-093`"*. One entry therefore says both that `093` has an author-written half and that clauses 1–5 are ratified. Both cannot be true.

⚠ **This is the failure class Decision `2026-08-08-092` exists to correct**, arriving one layer up: there, a single added word ("alone") spread a ratified reading over text the Panel had not written; here, a Status line spreads ratified authority over analysis the Panel never saw. The mechanism is identical — authored content inheriting ratified authority by adjacency — and it is exactly what makes a later reader unable to tell which commitments the Panel actually made.

**Decision:**

1. **The Trustee-ratified scope of Decision `2026-08-09-093` is clauses 1, 2 and 3 ONLY.** Clauses 4, 5, 6 and every open follow-up are **author-committed**. This changes no clause's content and withdraws no commitment — clause 4's technical finding and clause 6's form requirement remain correct and remain binding on the implementer; they are simply not Panel acts and must not be cited as such.

2. **Decision `2026-08-09-094`'s "clauses 1–5 … stand exactly as ratified" is CORRECTED to "clauses 1–3 … stand exactly as ratified; clauses 4–6 stand as author-committed."** `094`'s own clauses are unaffected: its clause 1 remains Trustee-ratified, its clauses 2–4 remain author-committed record corrections, exactly as its Status line already says.

3. ⛔ **Neither `093` nor `094` is edited in place** ([[feedback_supersede_never_reinterpret]]). Both are corrected by this entry, which is how a ratified record is amended. Readers of `093` must read this entry alongside it, in the same way `092` governs the reading of the story file's Escalation 6 block.

4. **Per-clause provenance is now MANDATORY on any decision-log entry that mixes ratification with author analysis.** A flat "Trustee-ratified" is permitted only on an entry that is ratified in its entirety. Where an entry carries both, each clause states which it is — the pattern `094` used correctly and `093` did not. ⚠ The routing-note → Decision pipeline makes this mixing the NORM rather than the exception: the note's author supplies the analysis, the Panel supplies the answers, and both land in one entry. Without per-clause marking, every future entry in this pipeline inherits the same defect.

5. **Recorded because the drift is in the direction that is hardest to detect.** Over-attributing to the Panel does not look like an error — it reads as a stronger record. It surfaces only when someone asks the Panel to stand behind a clause the Panel never saw, by which time the entry has been cited for months. That is precisely what happened here in under 48 hours.

**Open follow-ups:**
- ⛔ **Nothing about Story 10.23's substantive posture changes.** Escalation 6 remains UNDISCHARGED, `restoration_discipline_imposition` remains default-OFF with Trustee-Panel-exclusive enablement (`2026-08-07-089`), the Q1 sentinel precondition stands, and the all-Pariwars rollout scope stands.
- **The routing note is unaffected** — it is the question set, and the Decision entries govern.

**References:**
- Decision **2026-08-09-093** — the entry whose Status line is corrected; clauses 1–3 ratified, 4–6 author-committed
- Decision **2026-08-09-094** — the entry whose "clauses 1–5" phrasing is corrected; its own clauses stand
- Decision **2026-08-08-092** — the same failure class one layer down; the precedent for correcting a record without reopening it
- Decision **2026-08-07-089** — enablement authority, untouched
- `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/trustee-panel-routing-note-2026-08-09-story-10-23-ac14-mechanics.md` — the question set both entries rule on

### Decision 2026-08-09-094: Story 10.23 AC14 rollout scope is ALL PARIWARS — which is the `full` state, where the cohort is ignored entirely; this WITHDRAWS Decision `2026-08-09-093`'s "the Panel still owes a cohort" follow-up and SCOPES its clause 6 to the two intermediate states, which remain cohort-gated and cannot express "all"

**Decision type:** Other — scope clarification from the Trustee Panel, plus the record correction it forces in the author-written half of Decision `2026-08-09-093`. **Ratifies no enablement and authorizes no flip.**
**Status:** Trustee-ratified as to clause 1 (the scope). Clauses 2–4 are author-committed record corrections consequent on it. **Amends no other Trustee-ratified text** — `2026-08-07-088`, `089`, and clauses 1–5 of `2026-08-09-093` stand exactly as ratified.
**Author:** BigDev (Solo Builder)
**Ratifying trustees:** Trustee Panel, 2026-08-09.

**Context:**
Decision `2026-08-09-093` clause 2 ratified Q2 at Option (a) — one Decision per enabling version, naming its cohort explicitly. Its **first open follow-up** then recorded an author finding: that no cohort dimension can express the Panel's population direction, and that a `pariwar_id` cohort was therefore still owed by a future ruling. The Panel has clarified that the intended rollout scope is **all Pariwars**, which makes that follow-up wrong in its premise rather than merely incomplete. This entry corrects it at the source rather than leaving a withdrawn finding to be read as live.

**Decision:**

1. **The AC14 rollout scope is ALL PARIWARS** — Trustee-ratified. `pariwar_id` is **not** a selective cohort awaiting a future ruling, and no such ruling is owed.

2. **In the substrate, "all Pariwars" is the `full` state, and at `full` the cohort is ignored entirely.** `evaluateFlag` short-circuits `full` to `{ enabled: true, reason: 'state_full' }` before any clause is read (`packages/domain/src/feature-flags/evaluate.ts:132-133`). ✅ **Decision `2026-08-09-093`'s first open follow-up is WITHDRAWN** — at the terminal state there is no cohort to supply, and the concern it raised cannot arise there.

3. **Decision `2026-08-09-093` clause 6 is SCOPED, not repealed: its non-empty-cohort form requirement binds a `canary` or `rollout` enabling Decision ONLY.** Applied to the `full` Decision it is meaningless — the cohort is unread — and stating it there would imply a constraint the evaluator does not enforce. ⚠ Clause 6 is author-written; scoping it corrects the record and reinterprets nothing the Panel ratified.

4. **Decision `2026-08-09-093` clause 4 STANDS UNCHANGED and is reaffirmed.** The eligible population within each Pariwar continues to derive from `RESTORATION_OBLIGATION_KEYS` / clause data via `imposesRestorationObligation` (`rules.ts:368-374, 401`). ⛔ **No clause-ID branching and no R7(D)/R7(E)-specific branching** — such a branch violates Story 10.23's **D3** and breaks the property this clause affirms. Today that population is whoever R7(C)/(D)/(E)/(F) applies to; R7(G) is activated but imposes nothing (`never_excuses` is deliberately not an obligation key, per D4).

**Open follow-ups:**

- ⚠ **`off → full` is rejected, so all-Pariwars scope is reachable only THROUGH two cohort-gated states that cannot express "all".** `LEGAL_FLAG_STATE_TRANSITIONS` (`registry.ts:71-79`, enforced at `:711`) admits `off` only to `off` or `canary`. At `canary` and `rollout` the cohort **is** read, and the substrate offers no wildcard: operators are `in` / `eq` set-membership only (`COHORT_OPERATORS`), and any dimension other than `pariwar_id` is `malformed` in this job's context — `runRestorationDiscipline` supplies `pariwarId` alone (`restoration-discipline.ts:121`) — which falls back to `fallbackDefault: false`. So "all Pariwars" at those two states is expressible **only by enumeration**, bounded at `MAX_COHORT_CLAUSES × MAX_CLAUSE_VALUES` = 20 × 200 = **4000 Pariwars** (`registry.ts:495-496`, validated at write time). **This is not a reopening of clause 1 — it is the execution path clause 1 must travel, and the two intermediate enabling Decisions must account for it.**
- ✅ **The staleness in that window fails SAFE.** A Pariwar created after a `canary`/`rollout` version is written falls outside the enumeration → `no_match` → the writer stays **off** for it. Members there accrue no impositions until `full`. For a coverage-**removing** writer, being missed is the safe direction, which is why this is recorded as a follow-up rather than a risk.
- **The Q1 sentinel remains owed and unblocked**, and still gates the enabling Decision (`2026-08-09-093` clause 1).
- **Escalation 6's discharge remains OUTSTANDING**, routes unchanged per `2026-08-08-092`. ⛔ Nothing in this entry discharges it, authorizes a flip, or moves the flag off default-OFF.
- **The copy-truth defect remains separately owed.**

**References:**
- Decision **2026-08-09-093** — the entry this one corrects; clauses 1–5 stand, clause 6 is scoped, first open follow-up withdrawn
- Decision **2026-08-08-092** — precedent for a record correction that ratifies nothing new and reopens nothing
- Decision **2026-08-07-089** — enablement authority, unchanged
- `packages/domain/src/feature-flags/evaluate.ts:132-133` — the `state_full` short-circuit clause 2 turns on
- `packages/domain/src/feature-flags/registry.ts:71-79, 495-496, 711` — the ladder, the cohort size bounds, the transition check
- `apps/jobs/src/restoration-discipline.ts:121` — the Pariwar-only flag context
- `packages/validity-service/src/rules.ts:368-374, 401` — the obligation keys and predicate clause 4 reaffirms

### Decision 2026-08-09-093: Story 10.23 AC14 flag mechanics — Q1 and Q2 both ratified at Option (a); a projection-coverage sentinel is a PRECONDITION of any flip, one Decision authorizes ONE enabling version with an explicitly named cohort, and the imposed population follows the ACTIVATED restoration rules rather than any named rung

**Decision type:** Other — per-question option ratification, in the pattern of Decision `2026-08-07-084`. Governs the *instrument* of AC14 enablement. Ratifies no enablement and authorizes no flip.
**Status:** Trustee-ratified.
**Author:** BigDev (Solo Builder)
**Ratifying trustees:** Trustee Panel, 2026-08-09, on `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/trustee-panel-routing-note-2026-08-09-story-10-23-ac14-mechanics.md` (raised against the findings committed at `665b519`).

**Context:**
Story 8.14 (`0f72c37`) shipped the `alert.closed` producer, allowing Story 10.23's chain to be driven from real production code for the first time. Two observations followed, both about the AC14 flag rather than shipped behaviour, and both routed as the second Panel note on this story. The first note (ruled into Decisions `2026-08-07-088` / `089`) settled *whether* the writer is flag-gated and *who* may enable it. This entry settles what a single exercise of that authority covers, and what must be observable before it is exercised.

**Decision:**

1. **Q1 — ratified at Option (a). A projection-coverage sentinel is a PRECONDITION of any flip.** `deriveContributionFacts` returns `null` when coverage is absent (`packages/validity-service/src/producer.ts:508`), degrading every member to `producer_unavailable` so that no clause can apply — yet `runRestorationDiscipline` reports `unavailable: null`, byte-identical to a genuinely clean Pariwar. A third named producer on the `unavailable` field (`apps/jobs/src/restoration-discipline.ts:66`), shipped and pinned by a test, must exist and pass **before** the enabling Decision is authored. ⛔ **The A/B choice is closed and is not to be reopened.** The sentinel's *construction* remains implementer-owned under `2026-08-07-089`'s ownership table; only its sequencing was the Panel's to fix, and it is now fixed.

2. **Q2 — ratified at Option (a). One Decision authorizes ONE enabling version, naming its cohort explicitly.** Each widening (`canary → rollout → full`) requires its own `.decision-log.md` entry. The Panel authorizes only a cohort it has seen. Reaching `full` therefore costs three entries, and that cost is the ratified posture, not an obstacle to route around.

3. **The imposed population follows the ACTIVATED restoration rules — Panel direction, recorded as given:** *"Apply the mechanism to the members to whom the currently activated restoration rules apply. Do not narrow this wording to R7(D)/(E) only; the applicable population is determined by which restoration rules are currently activated."*

4. ⚠ **Clause 3 is satisfied by the shipped design and mandates NO code change — recorded explicitly so that no one later "implements" it.** The writer already derives its population from clause DATA via `imposesRestorationObligation` (`rules.ts:401`) reading `RESTORATION_OBLIGATION_KEYS` (`rules.ts:368-374`), never from a clause-id branch. Adding a rung-keyed condition to satisfy clause 3 would **violate Story 10.23's D3 ("no clause-id branch, ever")** and would break the very property clause 3 affirms. The activated set is `R7_ACTIVATED_CLAUSE_IDS` = R7(C), (D), (E), (F), (G) (`rules.ts:83-95`); of these **R7(G) imposes nothing**, because its `restoration` block is `{"never_excuses": true}` and `never_excuses` is deliberately absent from `RESTORATION_OBLIGATION_KEYS` (the ratified D4 invariant — a clause may influence trustee *understanding* without imposing a *holding*). R7(A)/(B) remain held on non-fact blockers. So the population today is whoever R7(C)/(D)/(E)/(F) applies to, and it moves automatically if the Panel activates or amends a clause.

5. ⛔ **What this entry does NOT do.** It does **not** discharge Escalation 6 — R7(D)/(E)/(F) packages still name a completion act no workflow in the system can perform (Decision `2026-08-08-092` clause 4). It does **not** authorize a flip, enable the flag, or name a cohort. It does **not** touch the copy-truth defect. `restoration_discipline_imposition` remains **default-OFF** with **Trustee-Panel-exclusive** enablement (`2026-08-07-089`). **Every precondition this entry creates is additional to — never a substitute for — the discharge.**

6. **Answer-independent and therefore restated as a form requirement on the entry itself:** an enabling Decision must state (i) the target state, (ii) its cohort clauses verbatim, and (iii) that the cohort is non-empty. An empty cohort resolves to `enabled: false` / `reason: 'cohort_empty'` (`evaluate.ts:164`), so a Decision omitting (ii) is not a narrower authorization — it is a **vacuous** one that will read afterwards as an authorized flip that failed.

**Open follow-ups:**

- ⚠ **The cohort dimension is NOT supplied by this ruling, and clause 3 cannot supply it.** `COHORT_DIMENSIONS` is exactly `pariwar_id, member_state, district, block, role, cohort_tag` (`feature_flag_versions.ts:56`), and `runRestorationDiscipline` populates the flag context with **`pariwarId` alone** (`restoration-discipline.ts:121`). No cohort clause can express "a member to whom an activated restoration rule applies" — that predicate is not visible to the flag evaluator. **The two layers are distinct and must not be conflated when the enabling Decision is drafted:** the *cohort* selects which **Pariwars** the writer runs for and must be expressed in `pariwar_id`; the *member population within* each such Pariwar is then determined by the activated restoration rules per clause 3. An enabling Decision that writes clause 3's wording into its cohort field would be unimplementable, and would fail clause 6 for the reason clause 6 exists.
- **The Q1 sentinel is now owed and unblocked** — implementer-owned, gating the enabling Decision.
- **Escalation 6's discharge remains OUTSTANDING**, routes unchanged per `2026-08-08-092`.
- **The copy-truth defect remains separately owed** and still needs a Story 2.2 tone sign-off owner.

**References:**
- `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/trustee-panel-routing-note-2026-08-09-story-10-23-ac14-mechanics.md` — the question set this entry rules on
- `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/10-23-restoration-discipline-lock-in.md` @ `665b519` — § *Post-merge Findings*, Findings 1 and 2
- Decision **2026-08-07-088** clause 4 — Escalation 6 binds the flag flip rather than story closure
- Decision **2026-08-07-089** — enablement authority; the ownership table clauses 1 and 5 turn on
- Decision **2026-08-08-092** — the "alone" transcription correction; the UNDISCHARGED finding clause 5 restates
- Decision **2026-08-06-080** — R7(F)/R7(G) ratified into §3.1; the D4 invariant behind clause 4's R7(G) treatment
- Decision **2026-08-07-084** — the per-question option-ratification format
- `packages/validity-service/src/rules.ts:83-95, 368-374, 401` — activated clause set, obligation keys, the predicate
- `packages/domain/src/schema/feature_flag_versions.ts:56` — `COHORT_DIMENSIONS`

### Decision 2026-08-08-092: Story 10.23's "closure is still gated" was a TRANSCRIPTION ERROR, not a ratified rule — Decision `2026-08-07-088` clause 4 moved Escalation 6's binding point to the flag flip *instead of* story closure, and a single added word ("alone") re-created the option the Panel rejected; status `done` stands, and the invariant remains UNDISCHARGED

**Decision type:** Other — record correction, reconciling downstream restatements against the ratified text they claim to carry. Ratifies nothing new and reopens nothing.
**Status:** Author-committed (record correction). **Supersedes the closure clause of Decision `2026-08-08-090`** (itself author-committed). **Amends no Trustee-ratified text** — Decisions `2026-08-07-088` and `2026-08-07-089` stand exactly as ratified.
**Author:** BigDev
**Ratifying trustees:** n/a — this entry resolves a transcription discrepancy in favour of the ratified source. It is not a governance question and was not routed as one.

**Context:**
Story 10.23 is recorded `done` in both places that carry status — the story header and `sprint-status.yaml` — flipped by the 2026-08-08b `bmad-code-review` pass, **which recorded no basis for the flip**. Several other passages assert that closing the story was not permitted:

- the story's in-scope table — *"Escalation 6's invariant blocks `done`"*;
- its Dev Notes — *"before 10.23 is `done`"*, *"that is why Escalation 6 blocks `done`"*;
- Task 11 — *"enabling the flag is not, and closing it is not, until the property holds"*;
- its Completion Notes — *"The story is `review`, **NOT closed**"*;
- its disposition block — *"the invariant binds the flag flip **as well as** closure"*;
- Decision `2026-08-08-090` — *"merging with it off is permitted, enabling it and **closing the story are not**."*

So the record simultaneously reports the story closed and reports closing it as a governance violation. This was found while tracing Escalation 6, not by the review that flipped the status.

**The finding — the divergence is one word, and it is traceable.** Decision `2026-08-07-088` clause 4 reads, verbatim:

> Only its binding point moves: it now binds the **flag flip**, where the harm actually begins, **rather than story closure**.

The story's Escalation 6 block transcribes this as *"rather than story closure **alone**"*. That added word converts an exclusive move ("binds the flip, **not** closure") into an additive one ("binds the flip **as well as** closure"), and every later restatement — the disposition block, the v0.2 Change Log row, and Decision `2026-08-08-090` — inherits the additive reading from it.

**Three independent grounds establish that the ratified reading is exclusive:**

1. **The words of clause 4.** *"Only its binding point moves"* describes a move, not an extension, and *"rather than"* is exclusionary. The Panel wrote no "alone".
2. **The option table it ratifies.** `trustee-panel-routing-note-2026-08-07-story-10-23.md` offers option **(d) — "Keep as authored — merge permitted, closure gated"**, described there as *"the status quo this note questions"*. It was **not** ratified. Option **(a)** was, and its consequence column reads *"10.23 merges and **is reviewable in full**; nothing is imposed until the Panel says so."* Options (a) and (d) are distinguished on precisely this axis; the additive reading collapses them and makes (d)'s rejection meaningless.
3. **The purpose of Q4.** The Panel's own finding was that a closure gate is the **wrong instrument** — it gates paperwork while every harm lands at merge (*"its placement let every harm it describes land one step before the gate"*). Ratifying (a) **replaced** a process gate with a technical one. Retaining both would preserve the instrument the Panel had just identified as ineffective.

The story's own operative sentence in the Escalation 6 block already agrees with the exclusive reading: *"Merging with the flag off is permitted; **enabling the flag is not**, until the property holds"* — closure goes unmentioned. The file contradicts itself; the ratified source does not.

**Decision:**
1. **Escalation 6's invariant binds the AC14 flag flip, and only the flag flip.** Story 10.23's status `done` is **correct as recorded** and required no authorization beyond the review that set it. The absence of a recorded basis was a reporting gap, not an unauthorized act.
2. **The additive restatements are transcription errors and are corrected in the story file**, each marked in place with a pointer to this entry. ⚠ **Historical Change Log rows are NOT rewritten** — they record what was believed when written, and are correct as history ([[feedback_supersede_never_reinterpret]]); a new row records this correction instead.
3. **This entry supersedes the closure clause of Decision `2026-08-08-090`.** That entry is author-committed, so superseding it needs no ratification. Its every other clause — including the UNDISCHARGED finding itself — stands.
4. ⛔ **What this entry does NOT do, stated so it cannot be misread as relief:** it does **not** discharge Escalation 6, does **not** authorize the AC14 flag, does **not** shorten or soften the gap, and does **not** touch any code. `restoration_discipline_imposition` remains **default-OFF** with **Trustee-Panel-exclusive** enablement (Decision `2026-08-07-089`), and R7(D)/(E)/(F) packages still name a completion act no workflow in the system can perform. **The only thing that changes is which artifact the invariant gates.**
5. **Recorded because an error in the safe direction is still an error.** The drift over-gated rather than under-gated, which is why it survived review — but it left the ledger accusing a correct status flip of being a governance violation, and it obscured which gate is load-bearing. A record that names two gates where one exists invites a reader who discovers one is spurious to discount the other. The real gate is the flag, and it is the only one.

**Open follow-ups:**
- **Escalation 6's discharge remains OUTSTANDING and its routes are unchanged**: a ratified catch-up / complete-all payment path (the expected vehicle), any other Trustee-ratified mechanism satisfying the invariant, or a Part 11 reinterpretation under which the packages are satisfiable by acts the system already supports. Authority is the Trustee Panel's, exclusively.
- **The copy-truth defect is untouched and still separately owed** — `suspension_disclosure.lock_in.what_it_does` **and** the `a11y` label that repeats it verbatim, both asserting completability to members with no completion path. Needs a Story 2.2 tone sign-off owner; not gated on the discharge, and not fixable by narrowing the disclosure's trigger.
- ⚠ **Process gap: the `bmad-code-review` status flip recorded no basis on a story whose own text labelled an escalation ⛔ BLOCKING against `done`.** The flip happened to be right. Whenever a story carries an escalation marked blocking on closure, the review that closes it should state which ruling permits the closure — otherwise a correct flip is indistinguishable from an unnoticed one, which is exactly the ambiguity this entry had to spend a full trace resolving.

**References:**
- Decision **2026-08-07-088** clause 4 + the Panel amendment (clause 5) — the ratified text this entry reconciles toward; **not amended**
- Decision **2026-08-07-089** — Trustee-Panel-exclusive activation authority; **not amended**
- Decision **2026-08-08-090** — its closure clause superseded here; all other clauses stand
- `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/trustee-panel-routing-note-2026-08-07-story-10-23.md` — the Q4 option table; option (d) ("merge permitted, closure gated") offered and **not** ratified
- `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/10-23-restoration-discipline-lock-in.md` — the corrected passages and the new Change Log row
- `packages/domain/src/feature-flags/registry.ts:205` · `apps/jobs/src/restoration-discipline.ts` — the flag and its sole production consumer, **unchanged and OFF**
- [[feedback_closure_language_precision]] · [[feedback_supersede_never_reinterpret]] — the conventions this correction follows
