### Decision 2026-08-08-091: Story 10.23 code-review finding — `shouldImpose`'s re-imposition bar matched on the wrong key (`episode_key`) and would have re-locked exactly the population Decision `2026-08-07-088` clause 3 protects; corrected to match on a pinned `completion_unsatisfiable` flag instead

**Decision type:** Other — implementation correction to a bug found during code review, before the AC14 rollout flag was ever enabled in any environment
**Status:** Author-committed (implementation correction). Does **not** amend the RATIFIED RULE TEXT of Decision `2026-08-07-088` clause 3 — "an expired imposition does NOT re-impose while the same unresolved episode's completion condition remains unsatisfiable" stands exactly as ratified. Only the code's MATCHING MECHANISM for "same unresolved episode" changes.
**Author:** BigDev
**Ratifying trustees:** n/a — a bug-fix to an already-ratified rule's implementation, not a new policy question

#### The bug

`episodeKeyOf` folds `skipsCurrentYear` into the episode identity, and `skipsCurrentYear` is scoped to the IST CALENDAR YEAR — it resets at the year boundary and increments on every further missed cycle, REGARDLESS of member action. For R7(D)/(E)/(F) (Escalation 6: no catch-up channel exists), a further missed cycle is not the member acting; it is the mechanical, near-guaranteed consequence of staying on the donor roster (AC6) while genuinely unable to comply. A three-layer adversarial code review (Blind Hunter / Edge Case Hunter / Acceptance Auditor) of Story 10.23's diff found that matching the re-imposition bar on this value would, once the AC14 flag is ever enabled, re-lock exactly the population the ratified rule exists to protect — reproducing, through skip-count drift, the "de-facto permanent, machine-imposed coverage removal" Story 10.17 was written to correct. The story's own test suite only pinned a fresh skip UNRELATED to an old, resolved gap; it never modeled a fresh skip accrued while still stuck in the same unresolved, unsatisfiable episode.

#### The fix

A new event-payload field, `completion_unsatisfiable` (boolean), is computed via `hasUnsatisfiableCompletionCondition(clausePayload)` and pinned at imposition — like `lock_in_months` and `concurrency_rule` (FR-8). `shouldImpose`'s re-imposition bar now matches on `i.completionUnsatisfiable` (cross-clause, preserving the original F→C drift precedent), never on `episode_key`. `episode_key` is retained on the event **as audit data only** — it still records the skip-count/anchor context at imposition for a trustee reading the record, but no longer gates the decision. `shouldImpose` drops its `episodeKey` parameter entirely (dead for decision purposes).

The documented discharge path is unchanged and still works with no backfill: `UNSATISFIABLE_COMPLETION_KEYS` (`write.ts`) shrinking is a global, code-level change, and the outer `hasUnsatisfiableCompletionCondition(clausePayload)` gate is re-evaluated LIVE against the CURRENT candidate's payload on every call — never against a stored historical value — so the bar releases automatically for every affected member the moment the registry changes, exactly as the constant's doc comment already promised. `shouldImpose` stays PURE (no I/O): the new field is read off already-loaded overlay data, never re-resolved from a historical clause version.

#### Blast radius

Zero in production: the AC14 `restoration_discipline_imposition` flag defaults OFF and requires the Trustee-Panel-exclusive discharge decision (`2026-08-07-089`) to enable, so no imposition has ever been written by the automatic writer. The fix landed in the same PR as the story's initial implementation, before merge.

#### Related

- Decision **2026-08-07-088** (the ratified rule this corrects the IMPLEMENTATION of, not the rule itself).
- Decision **2026-08-08-090** (D1–D8, the story's original implementation record — this entry supersedes D3/AC2's matching-mechanism detail only, not its trigger predicate or any other decision).

### Decision 2026-08-08-090: Story 10.23 implementation decisions D1–D8 — the restoration-discipline lock-in ships as a SECOND event-derived overlay with dual clause-version pinning, a shared imposition predicate, and a widened falsifiable-hold gate; catch-up remains an UNDISCHARGED governance gap

**Decision type:** Other — implementation-decision record for a `[PRIMITIVE]` story, capturing the eight load-bearing choices made while building §3.1's restoration lock-in
**Status:** Author-committed (implementation decisions); the four Trustee-ratified answers it implements are Decisions `2026-08-07-088` and `2026-08-07-089`. ⚠ **ONE CLAUSE SUPERSEDED by Decision `2026-08-08-092`** — the "What remains OPEN" bullet asserting *"enabling it and closing the story are not [permitted]"*. Closure was never gated: `088` clause 4 moved Escalation 6's binding point to the **flag flip** *rather than* story closure, and this entry inherited a downstream transcription of it that added the word "alone". Story 10.23 is correctly `done`. **Every other clause stands, including the UNDISCHARGED finding itself** — the flag remains OFF and the catch-up gap is unchanged.
**Author:** BigDev
**Ratifying trustees:** n/a for D1–D8 (implementation choices). ⚠ The governance questions this story raised were ruled on separately — see Decisions `2026-08-07-088` (four rulings) and `2026-08-07-089` (activation authority). This entry does **not** ratify anything; it records what was built and why.

#### Context

Niyamavali §3.1 prescribes a lock-in as the consequence of five of the seven R7 rungs — 3 months for R7(B)/(C)/(D), 5 for R7(E)/(F). Those durations have been seeded and live since Story 4.2, four of the five clauses have been evaluating in production since Story 10.24, and the ladder has been returning `lockin_3mo_plus_catchup` / `lockin_5mo_complete_all` verdicts against real members. **Nothing turned a verdict into a consequence.** Story 10.23 builds the instrument.

Two premises in the epic AC were false and are corrected in the story file: `lock_in_months` **is** consumed in production (`RESTORATION_OBLIGATION_KEYS` → `imposesRestorationObligation` → the Trustee-Lite violator-flag channel), and the per-ground duration already lives in the R7 clause rather than needing a new one.

#### The decisions

1. **D1 — event PLUS table, unlike Stories 10.25 and 10.26.** 10.25 shipped a pure derivation (no event, no table); 10.26 added an event but no table. Neither reading applies here, for one reason: **version pinning is not derivable.** A restoration lock-in's `expires_at` depends on `lock_in_months` *as it stood at imposition*, so under a pure derivation a Trustee re-tune would retroactively move every existing member's unlock date — precisely what FR-8 exists to prevent. The event is the authority and the replay source; `member_restoration_impositions` is the indexed read surface and carries the structural CHECKs.

2. **D2 — the R7 clause supplies the DURATION; a new `niy.restoration-discipline.policy` clause supplies the INSTRUMENT. Both pinned.** Duplicating `lock_in_months` into the new clause would create **two registry sources for one constitutional number**, forcing the Panel to amend two instruments to change one, and a single policy clause cannot express §3.1's per-rung table without re-encoding the ladder. The new clause carries only what no R7 clause can: the month-counting convention and the concurrency rule. Both `clause_version_id`s ride every imposition.

3. **D3 — `imposesRestorationObligation` is the imposition trigger, with `lock_in_months > 0` as a second, non-optional conjunct.** The predicate that decides *"does this clause accuse?"* (Story 10.26 D4) is the predicate that decides *"does this clause impose?"* — so R7(G) imposes nothing with no code aware of R7(G). ⚠ **The second conjunct is load-bearing:** R7(A) *does* impose a restoration obligation (`consecutive_required: 3`) while prescribing **no** lock-in (`lock_in_months: 0`), so the predicate alone would give every R7(A) member a spurious zero-length lock-in. Enforced semantically in `readLockInMonths` and structurally by a DB CHECK. No clause-id branch anywhere.

4. **D4 — the overlay REPRESENTS both clocks and never merges them.** `lockInStatus` is byte-unchanged; the restoration clock is a sibling payload sub-object with its own `imposedAt`/`expiresAt`/`state`. A merged `disciplineStatus` was rejected: it cannot represent concurrency, and the first thing anyone does with it is take a `max` or a `min` — subsumption by arithmetic.

5. **D5 — no reason code, no actor, no Tier-1 column.** Nothing decides; §3.1 applies and **the clause id is the reason**. Consequently there is no PII byte in the instrument, so — unlike `member_moderation_actions`, which needed migration 0092 to add a column-scoped UPDATE for the DPDPA rationale scrub — append-only holds absolutely with no carve-out.

6. **D6 — expiry by time only; the lift seam is shaped, not built.** No `…expired` event and no job: the state flips at read from `evaluatedAt >= expires_at`, the pattern `VALIDITY_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS`'s own doc comment already names. The fold is total over unknown event types so a future `…lifted` could be added without reshaping it — ⛔ but a lift is a governance act nobody has authorised, and the event is deliberately absent.

7. **D7 — the falsifiable-hold gate was WIDENED FIRST, and the widening exposed a real half-scoping.** `r7-activation-totality.test.ts` checked every held clause's `blockedBy` against `R7_SUPPLIED_FACT_KEYS` — the `contribution.*` producer's key set. R7(A)/(B) are held on `member.joining_discipline_state`, a `member.*` fact that can never enter that set, so **the assertion was vacuously green and would have stayed green at the exact moment this story satisfied the hold's stated reason.** The mechanization had been half-scoped since Story 10.24 and nobody noticed, because the second fact family did not exist. The gate now spans both families, `R7HeldClause` gained a `blockedByNonFacts` bucket (with an anti-cheat asserting nothing fact-shaped hides there), and the vacuity guard counts blockers of any kind. ⚠ Order was not cosmetic: the gate was widened and revert-proven **before** the fact was supplied, then went RED for real when it was.

8. **D8 — catch-up is named as ABSENT, and it is a GOVERNANCE GAP rather than a deferred implementation.** R7(D)'s `catch_up_required` and R7(E)/(F)'s `complete_all` have no mechanism: contribution flows only to an OPEN cycle (Story 7.6, fenced by 8.10), so no member can pay a closed one. `UNSATISFIABLE_COMPLETION_KEYS` in `write.ts` **is** that gap expressed as data, and is the single line a successor story edits.

#### What this story did NOT do

- **R7(A)/(B) were NOT activated.** Decision `2026-08-06-077` is explicit that Story 10.23 carries the fact-supply half only. Both holds were **narrowed** (`blockedBy: []`, the Part 11 amendment remaining as a non-fact blocker) and their owner moved from `story-10-23` to the **Trustee Panel**, since no story can satisfy what remains. Verified live 2026-08-07: §3.1 and the seed rows still carry the disclaimed proxy populations.
- **`architecture.md` was NOT amended** (Decision `2026-08-04-072`). The architectural record is the `AI-10-1` doc-comment block at the point of use — the first entry in the Epic-10 series.
- **`POOL_ASSIGNMENT_HASH_VERSION` was NOT bumped**, and the licence for that is a real re-spawn test, not a re-run of the hash function.
- **No disclosure copy was written and no UI shipped.** The `@twt/ui`, `apps/mobile` and `packages/i18n` source diffs are empty; the dormant Story 10.16 arm activates through the emitted `restoration_lock_in` flag alone.

#### ⛔ What remains OPEN

- **Escalation 6's discharge invariant is UNDISCHARGED** — *the completion condition of every restoration package this story imposes must be satisfiable through a ratified system workflow.* The AC14 flag (default OFF, Trustee-Panel-exclusive enablement) is the enforcement mechanism; merging with it off is permitted, enabling it and closing the story are not.
- **The copy-truth defect is separately owed** and is discharged by none of the above. ⚠ Refined during implementation: the story text says one of the four shipped strings is untrue; it is **two** — `suspension_disclosure.lock_in.what_it_does` and the `a11y` label, which repeats the same sentence verbatim, so a screen-reader user hears it too.
- **Escalations 2, 3, 4, 7 and 8 remain owed** exactly as authored; Escalation 4 (a member in the JOINING lock-in is `is_valid: true` while this instrument makes a restoration lock-in `false`) becomes live and member-visible at the flag flip.

#### Related

- Decisions **2026-08-07-088** (the four pre-implementation rulings this story implements) and **2026-08-07-089** (activation authority).
- Decisions **2026-08-04-072** (the `AI-10-n` record, not `architecture.md`), **2026-08-06-077** (the Part 11 owner), **2026-08-06-079** (non-subsumption), **2026-08-06-080** (R7(F)/(G) ratified), **2026-08-07-086** (the missed-cycle ruling).

### Decision 2026-08-07-089: The Q4 activation authority is OWNED — only the Trustee Panel may authorize enabling Story 10.23's imposition flag, acting through a formal `.decision-log.md` entry; implementer owns the mechanism, Operations owns deployment mechanics, neither owns activation. Closes Decision 2026-08-07-088's unowned open follow-up

**Decision type:** Other — separation-of-duties assignment for an activation authority; closes an open follow-up left by Decision `2026-08-07-088` without amending it
**Status:** Trustee-ratified
**Author:** BigDev, on Decision `2026-08-07-088`'s open follow-up *"The Q4 discharge decision does not yet exist and has no owner"*
**Ratifying trustees:** Trustee Panel

**Context:**
Decision `2026-08-07-088` clause 5 tied Story 10.23's imposition-flag enablement to the governance decision that discharges Escalation 6's invariant, and stated that authorization *"is not an operational act."* It did not say **who holds it**. Its own open follow-up recorded the gap in terms: *"The Q4 discharge decision does not yet exist and has no owner."*

That is the shape this project has already been bitten by. An obligation with a stated condition and no named holder is an un-gated re-commitment, and un-gated re-commitments decay ([[feedback_record_unattested_no_backfill]]) — R7 stayed dark for two epics behind exactly that pattern. It is also the specific failure mode `088` clause 5 was written to prevent one level down: a flag with an unstated default and an unnamed owner answers *how* the writer is held back while leaving *who may release it* open. Naming the default without naming the holder moves the ambiguity rather than removing it.

This entry is recorded as a **new decision rather than an amendment to `2026-08-07-088`**. `088` was correct when ratified; the log's own convention is that ratified decisions are never edited in place, and corrections or extensions are recorded as new entries referencing the prior one ([[feedback_supersede_never_reinterpret]]).

**Decision:**
1. **The Trustee Panel exclusively owns authorization to activate Story 10.23's imposition flag.** No other role holds it, and it is not delegable by the holder of any adjacent responsibility.
2. **The authorizing act has a required form: a formal entry in `.decision-log.md`.** A ticket, a PR approval on a configuration change, a deployment sign-off, a meeting note, or a verbal go-ahead is **not** an authorization and does not become one by being recorded afterwards. If there is no Decision entry, the flag is not authorized.
3. **Separation of duties is three-way and each role's boundary is stated affirmatively:**
   - **Implementer** — owns *building the mechanism*: the overlay, the writer, the flag and its gating, the default-off behaviour, and the tests that pin them. **Holds no authority to enable**, in any environment, including their own.
   - **Operations** — owns *deployment mechanics*: how configuration reaches an environment, how a flip is executed and observed, how it is rolled back. **Holds no authority to decide that a flip may occur.** Executing an unauthorized flip is not cured by having executed it correctly.
   - **Trustee Panel** — owns *authorization to activate*, and nothing about the mechanism's construction or delivery.
4. **The ordering constraint from `088` clause 5 is unchanged and now has a named holder:** authorization may not precede the decision that discharges Escalation 6's invariant. In practice the two may be the same Decision entry, but the discharge must be established in it — an entry that authorizes activation while the completion condition remains unsatisfiable is void, not merely premature.
5. ⚠ **Assigning the owner does NOT discharge the invariant, and must not be recorded as if it had.** The catch-up gap persists exactly as `088` and Story 10.23's Escalation 6 describe it: R7(D)/(E)/(F) packages still name a completion act no workflow can perform. This entry names **who may act**; it does not create the mechanism, shorten the condition, or reduce the gap's severity ([[feedback_closure_language_precision]] — this is an ownership assignment, not a closure).
6. **`deferred-work.md` and Story 10.23's Task 11 record this entry as the answer to "who owns the discharge decision"**, and continue to record the discharge itself as **outstanding**. Story 10.23's Task 11 previously required recording that the decision had no owner; it now requires recording this entry instead, with the gap still marked open.

**Open follow-ups:**
- **The discharge condition itself remains unmet and unscheduled.** Its routes are unchanged from Story 10.23's Escalation 6: a ratified catch-up / complete-all payment path (expected vehicle), any other Trustee-ratified mechanism satisfying the invariant, or a Part 11 reinterpretation under which the packages' completion condition is satisfiable by acts the system already supports.
- The copy defect recorded at `2026-08-07-088` clause 7 still needs a named Story 2.2 tone sign-off owner. It is **not** covered by this entry and binds while the gap persists.

**References:**
- Decision **2026-08-07-088** — the ruling this entry closes an open follow-up of; clause 5 is the provision this one supplies a holder for
- `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/10-23-restoration-discipline-lock-in.md` — **AC14** (the flag, its default, its enablement authority) and **Escalation 6** (the invariant, preserved verbatim)
- `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/trustee-panel-routing-note-2026-08-07-story-10-23.md` — Q4's option set and the call-graph finding behind it

### Decision 2026-08-07-088: Trustee Panel rules on Story 10.23's four build-gating governance questions — non-shortening concurrency, refuse-and-surface on unprovisioned Pariwars, no re-imposition of an undischargeable obligation, and the automatic imposition writer gated behind a default-OFF rollout flag whose enablement authority is the Q4 discharge decision itself

**Decision type:** Other — four pre-implementation Trustee-Panel rulings on an authored-but-unbuilt story, ratified together because each converts an implementation default into ratified policy before the code embodies it; includes one gate-placement correction (Q4) to a prior story-level disposition
**Status:** Trustee-ratified
**Author:** BigDev, via `trustee-panel-routing-note-2026-08-07-story-10-23.md`, on Story 10.23's Escalations 5, 6 and 9 and its AC2
**Ratifying trustees:** Trustee Panel

**Context:**
Story 10.23 (Restoration Discipline Lock-In) is authored off `main` @ `6783eba`, status `ready-for-dev`, **not yet implemented**. It carries nine escalations. Five were routed elsewhere and are untouched by this entry: **E1** is resolved by building it (the story's own Task 1); **E2** (the non-subsumption principle never reaching the ratified Niyamavali) is a Part 11 instrument already sequenced by `sprint-change-proposal-2026-08-04.md:150`; **E4** (join lock-in is `is_valid: true` via `VALID_STATES`, restoration lock-in will be `false`) has been open since Story 10.17 Escalation 3 and resolving it would move coverage for every existing member; **E7** (R7(C)'s ≥12-month gap drawing a *3*-month lock-in against R7(F)'s 6–11-month gap drawing *5*) is already named as a separate open question in Decision `2026-08-06-080`; **E8** (R7(A)'s unpublished Part 11 amendment) is an instrument no story can satisfy per Decision `2026-08-06-077`.

The remaining four are different in kind: each one changes what the implementer writes, and each is a governance choice in implementation clothing. The story hands three of them (Q1/Q2/Q3) to the dev agent with a recommended posture and a "decide and state it" instruction, and records them for `deferred-work.md` — but a record is not a ruling. **Absent ratification, the recommended posture would become the de facto policy embodied in the implementation**, binding on merge, discoverable only by reading the imposition site, and thereafter amendable only by changing code rather than by a governance act.

The fourth question arose from a call-graph finding made while preparing the routing note. `scanR7ViolatorCandidates` today has exactly **one** production consumer — `apps/api/src/modules/trustee-lite/handlers.ts:246`, an on-demand read that only *displays* violator flags. Story 10.23's Task 4 adds a **new writer** in `apps/jobs`, and the story named no feature flag or dark-launch posture for it. The story's own Escalation 6 states *"Merging 10.23 is permitted; closing it is not."* But on merge, and not on closure: members enter coverage-removing lock-ins with no human in the loop (the substrate's first such instrument); R7(D)/(E)/(F) members receive packages **no workflow can complete**, because contribution flows only to an open cycle (Story 7.6, fenced by 8.10) while a missed cycle is closed; and the already-shipped copy at `packages/i18n/locales/en/contribution.json:153` and its `hi` mirror tells them *"Contributing during this period counts toward completing your restoration"* — untrue for exactly those members. The invariant's content was correct; its placement let every harm it describes land one step before the gate.

**Decision:**
1. **Q1 — Option (a) ratified: the combined expiry of concurrent restoration impositions is the MAXIMUM over live impositions.** The overlay is in force while any imposition is un-expired; never the minimum, never a replacement, never a sum. §3.1 is silent on combination, and this is the only reading that neither shortens a live consequence (contrary to §1d's non-subsumption principle, and to Story 10.25 **D5** / Decision `2026-08-06-079`) nor invents a longer one than §3.1's per-rung table prescribes. **The rule is registry data, not a code constant:** it lands in the `niy.restoration-discipline.policy` clause payload per the story's **D2**, so the Panel can amend it as a governance act.
2. **Q2 — Option (a) ratified: on an unprovisioned Pariwar, do NOT impose; surface the gap as a named sentinel** following `R7_REGISTRY_UNPROVISIONED_PRODUCER` (`packages/validity-service/src/rules.ts:275`). The sibling `niy.lock-in.policy` provisioning obligation (`niyamavali-v1-clauses.sql:132-134`) states its failure mode as a member-facing 503; that does not transfer, because this is a background imposition with no request to fail. **Imposing under a code default is explicitly rejected**: it is not a fallback but coverage removal under a duration and month-counting convention no Pariwar ratified — an unratified sanction imposed by a machine.
3. **Q3 — Option (a) ratified: an expired imposition does NOT re-impose while the same unresolved episode's completion condition remains unsatisfiable.** Idempotency-while-live does not reach this case, and left unspecified the writer would re-impose on each scan, locking an undischargeable member continuously until the skip ages out at the IST calendar-year boundary (`istYearStartUtc`, `packages/domain/src/contribution/facts.ts`). §3.1 prescribes a **bounded** consequence; continuous re-imposition converts it into a de-facto permanent coverage removal imposed by a machine — structurally the failure Story 10.17 was written to correct, arriving through a different door. The answer is stated at the imposition site and pinned with a test that runs a member past expiry with the clause still applying.
4. **Q4 — Option (a) ratified: the automatic `apps/jobs` imposition writer is gated behind a rollout feature flag on Story 10.8's existing per-cohort substrate.** The Escalation 6 invariant is **preserved verbatim** — *"the completion condition of every restoration package Story 10.23 imposes must be satisfiable through a ratified system workflow"* — and is **not** reopened. Only its binding point moves: it now binds the **flag flip**, where the harm actually begins, rather than story closure.
5. **⭐ Panel amendment to Q4 — the flag's default and its enablement authority are part of the ruling, not deployment detail.** **The rollout flag defaults OFF in every environment except an explicit, trustee-authorized rollout.** Authorization to enable it is **not an operational act**: it is tied to, and may not precede, the same governance decision that discharges Q4's invariant. No environment-level, per-cohort, or convenience enablement substitutes for that decision, and enabling it in a non-production environment confers no authority to enable it in production. **Flipping the flag without the discharging Decision is a governance violation, not a configuration change** — a flag with an unstated default and an unnamed owner answers *how* the writer is held back while leaving *who may release it* open, and that gap is what this amendment closes.
6. **Rung-splitting (Q4 option (b)) was considered and NOT taken.** Imposing for the completable rungs (R7(A)/(B)/(C)) at merge while holding R7(D)/(E)/(F) would deliver value sooner, but sits uneasily with the story's **D3** (*"no clause-id branch, ever"*) — it would have to key on the clause payload rather than the clause id. Recorded so the option is not re-derived from scratch if the Panel later wants partial rollout; the flag ratified in (4) can express it without a code change to the trigger.
7. **The copy defect is a SEPARATE and additionally-owed obligation that none of the above discharges.** `suspension_disclosure.lock_in.what_it_does` asserts completability to members with no completion path — the same false-statement-to-a-member harm class Story 10.16's **D3** refused in writing. Two constraints bind any fix: **fixing the copy does not discharge the Q4 invariant** (honest copy about an unsatisfiable obligation leaves it unsatisfiable — necessary, not sufficient), and **the disclosure's trigger must NOT be narrowed** so R7(D)/(E)/(F) members see nothing (silence about a coverage removal is worse than an imperfect explanation, and it re-creates the exact gap Story 10.16 closed). A copy change requires a Story 2.2 tone sign-off and sits above Story 10.23; it is assigned by the Panel, not absorbed by the implementer.
8. **This entry rules on none of Escalations 2, 4, 7 or 8.** They remain open on their existing routes and are unaffected. In particular the **E4 coverage contradiction** — a member in join `lock-in` is covered while a member in restoration lock-in will not be — becomes **live and member-visible** when the Q4 flag is enabled, and is expected to return to the Panel at that point.

**Open follow-ups:**
- **Story 10.23's ACs are amended to cite this Decision before dev begins** — AC2 (Q3's answer + the re-imposition test), AC3/D2 (Q2's sentinel posture), AC5 (Q1's maximum rule, and its placement in the clause payload), and a new AC or AC4 amendment carrying Q4's flag, its default-OFF scope, and its enablement-authority tie. The story is `ready-for-dev` and unbuilt, so these are amendments to an unimplemented spec, not a change to shipped behaviour.
- **The Q4 discharge decision does not yet exist and has no owner.** It is the same decision that authorizes the flag flip. Its discharge routes remain those named in Story 10.23's Escalation 6: a ratified catch-up / complete-all payment path (expected vehicle), any other Trustee-ratified mechanism satisfying the invariant, or a Part 11 reinterpretation under which the packages' completion condition is satisfiable by acts the system already supports.
- **The `deferred-work.md` rows the story's Task 11 will write for Escalations 5 and 9 must now record the ratified answer and cite this entry**, not the open question.
- **The copy defect (item 7) needs a Story 2.2 tone sign-off owner assigned.** It binds while the gap persists and is not gated on the Q4 discharge.
- The routing note's *"What non-answer would have meant"* section is retained as the reasoning that motivated this ruling, superseded in effect by this entry.

**References:**
- `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/trustee-panel-routing-note-2026-08-07-story-10-23.md` — the question set, full option tables, and verified citations
- `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/10-23-restoration-discipline-lock-in.md` — AC2, AC3, AC5, Escalations 5, 6, 9; decisions **D2**, **D3**, **D8**
- Decision **2026-08-06-077** — Story 10.23 carries the fact-supply half only; does not inherit the Part 11 amendment
- Decision **2026-08-06-079** — Story 10.25 **D5**, the non-subsumption constraint Q1 turns on
- Decision **2026-08-06-080** — R7(F)/R7(G) ratified into §3.1; names Escalation 7 as a separate open question
- Decision **2026-08-07-086** — the ratified interpretive note (*"reconciliation or an authorized catch-up process"*) that converted catch-up from a deferred implementation into the governance gap behind Q4
- Decision **2026-08-07-084** — the per-question option-ratification format this entry follows
- `docs/legal/niyamavali.md` §1.3, §3.1, §3.3 — lock-in's definition, the per-rung table, the coverage effect
- `packages/validity-service/src/rules.ts:275` · `packages/domain/seed/niyamavali-v1-clauses.sql:132-134` — Q2's sentinel precedent and the sibling provisioning obligation
- `packages/domain/src/contribution/facts.ts` (`istYearStartUtc`) — Q3's year-boundary bound
- `apps/api/src/modules/trustee-lite/handlers.ts:246` — today's sole `scanR7ViolatorCandidates` consumer, read-only; the baseline against which Q4's new writer was assessed
- `packages/i18n/locales/en/contribution.json:153` (+ `hi` mirror) — the copy defect in item 7

### Decision 2026-08-07-087: Story 10.12's ESCALATION 5 grammar gap CLOSED — `ux-design-specification.md` §12 gains Group E, Pattern 12 (Dynamic Field Rendering), closing the "no form-builder / per-Pariwar settings grammar exists" gap; no renderer built

**Decision type:** Other — UX-specification addition (component grammar), closing a documentation gap; no code, no architecture, no PRD change
**Status:** Author-committed (BigDev, acting as the UX decision authority for this narrow addition — not a Trustee-Panel-scoped governance ruling, unlike Decisions 084/085/086)
**Author:** BigDev, via `bmad-ux`, on Decision 2026-08-06-082's ESCALATION 5
**Ratifying trustees:** n/a — UX grammar addition, not a governance decision

**Context:**
Story 10.12 shipped its admin authoring surface (`<CustomFieldsPage>`) but explicitly built no member-facing dynamic form renderer — AC8's ⛔: *"no member-facing dynamic form renderer is built."* `ux-design-specification.md` had zero hits for any form-builder, field-definition, or per-Pariwar settings rendering grammar (verified live), and §11 commits component grammar as tenant-invariant. The re-trigger recorded in `deferred-work.md` was explicit: *"a UX pass that adds the grammar."*

**Artifact-format note:** `bmad-ux`'s default workflow produces a new `DESIGN.md`/`EXPERIENCE.md` two-spine workspace under `ux-designs/`. This project has never used that format — its UX artifact is the single `ux-design-specification.md`, cited throughout `epics.md` and Story 10.12's own AC8. Rather than fork a second, disconnected UX documentation system for one pattern, this closure adds directly to the existing document, in its own established `§12 UX Consistency Patterns` format.

**Decision:**
1. **New `Group E — Tenant-Authored Rendering Patterns`, `Pattern 12: Dynamic Field Rendering`** added to `ux-design-specification.md` §12, positioned after Group D (Patterns 10-11) rather than inserted into Group B (Patterns 4-6, where it belongs conceptually) — avoids renumbering the committed 1-11 sequence and the "Pattern Application Summary"'s cross-references. "Pattern Application Summary" updated from "11 patterns" to "12 patterns."
2. **The grammar boundary is stated explicitly:** the type→primitive mapping, validation grammar, layout algorithm, and interaction behavior are tenant-invariant; only field existence, labels (copy layer), order, and bounds vary per Pariwar — consistent with §11's existing tenant-invariance commitment, not a new exception to it.
3. **The type→primitive table is NOT hard-pinned to today's seven types.** Initial draft said *"the seven types are Story 10.12's whole vocabulary, deliberately closed"* — corrected: that claim belongs to the domain/contracts layer, not the UX spec, and would make the pattern constitutionally wrong the day an eighth type is added. Revised to: *"The renderer maps each custom-field type supported by the domain contract to a fixed interaction primitive... at time of writing, the domain contract defines seven types."*
4. **Retired fields and unknown/malformed definitions are handled differently, not conflated.** Initial draft said *"unknown or retired fields are never rendered"* as one behavior — corrected: retired fields are not rendered for new data entry (expected, quiet); an unknown field type or malformed definition fails safely with dignified error handling (Pattern 4) — a renderer must never silently hide data because it encountered something unexpected.
5. **No implementation-sequencing language in the UX artifact.** Initial draft referenced the `getMemberCustomFields`/`setMemberCustomFields` API seam and said "deliberately not built yet" — corrected: the UX spec describes interaction grammar, not project sequencing; the pattern's Surfaces line now reads *"this pattern governs any future member or admin surface that renders values from tenant-authored custom-field definitions,"* keeping the artifact timeless regardless of when or whether a renderer is built.
6. **No renderer is built by this decision.** Values remain written through the API only, exactly as Story 10.12 shipped. This closes the *grammar gap*, not the feature gap — a future story builds the renderer against this pattern.

**Artifacts changed:**
- `ux-design-specification.md` §12 — new Group E / Pattern 12.
- `deferred-work.md` — Escalation 5 (10.12 batch) closed.
- `docs/adr/ADR-0037-per-tenant-custom-fields-jsonb.md` — failure-mode #5 annotated closed; new Changelog row. (This escalation was tracked in ADR-0037's own failure-modes list independently of "the three" that gated ratification in Decision `085`'s batch — not previously closed by that entry.)

**Open follow-ups:** None gated by this entry. The actual renderer is future engineering work with no assigned story or owner yet.

**References:**
- `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/ux-design-specification.md` §12, Group E, Pattern 12
- Decision **2026-08-06-082** — the Story 10.12 record raising ESCALATION 5 (distinct from Story 10.26's ESCALATION 5, Decision `086` — same label, different story, tracked separately)
- `docs/adr/ADR-0037-per-tenant-custom-fields-jsonb.md` — failure mode #5, as amended

### Decision 2026-08-07-086: Story 10.26's ESCALATION 5 CLOSED — a member may see their own missed cycles, sequenced after Story 10.23; `missed-closed-cycle-v1` is ratified as a governance fact (not a proxy) with its rename deferred; `cycle_ref` is KEPT as provenance, not causation

**Decision type:** Other — Trustee Panel ruling on six recorded questions (two blocking) + a governance-text interpretive ratification (Niyamavali §3.1, no substantive rule change)
**Status:** Trustee-ratified (Q1–Q6); the Niyamavali interpretive note is additionally subject to the standing legal-counsel-review requirement (Part 11) — **not verified live as reviewed; recorded as an open follow-up, not claimed done** [[feedback_verify_before_committing_governance_claims]]
**Author:** BigDev, via `bmad-correct-course` (sprint-change-proposal-2026-08-07-R4.md), on Decision 2026-08-06-081's ESCALATION 5
**Ratifying trustees:** Trustee Panel

**Context:**
Story 10.26's R7(G) personal-event assertion carries an optional `cycle_ref` that shipped unpopulated by any UI — no member surface shows a missed cycle at all. Six questions were recorded (`deferred-work.md`, originally `:23-112`), Q1 and Q4 blocking. During review, a load-bearing correction surfaced beyond the original six questions: `missed-closed-cycle-v1` (the internal policy determining a "skip") already re-evaluates live — a late, tail-reconciled or catch-up confirmation clears the skip — and R7(D)/(E)/(F) (`niyamavali.md:78-81`) explicitly treat a missed contribution as completable ("catch-up," "complete all missed contributions"), not closed the moment a cycle shuts. The catch-up mechanism itself, however, is unbuilt — Story 10.25 (D4) explicitly deferred it to Story 10.23 (Restoration Discipline Lock-In, still `backlog`).

**Decision — all six questions:**
1. **Q1 (BLOCKING) — YES**, a member may see their own missed cycles, **but the story is not created until Story 10.23 lands.** Showing an obligation with no visible resolution path would be an incomplete disclosure, not a kinder one.
2. **Q2 — RATIFY NOW, RENAME LATER.** `missed-closed-cycle-v1`'s underlying semantics are ratified into `niyamavali.md` §3.1 via an interpretive note (Section 4.1 of the source proposal) — it is a real governance fact under an already-ratified policy, **not a proxy** in the `prd.md:346` sense, and the "proxy" characterization is explicitly retired wherever it appears (`deferred-work.md`, `producer.ts`). The **identifier is not re-pinned now** — its full semantic boundary (payment, catch-up completion, obligation discharge, member-facing explanation, possibly cycle linkage) is only known once Story 10.23 defines the complete restoration lifecycle; a future rename must describe that whole lifecycle, not today's raw observation. The Niyamavali note is scoped to rule interpretation only — the constraint that member-facing copy must never imply "missed" is permanent or irredeemable is carried here and into the future story's AC, **not** written into the legal text itself.
3. **Q3 — a DISTINCT state is required** for causes the pool engine structurally cannot see (out-of-band contributions, assigned-but-never-notified, grace/moderation-overlay window). A single neutral label was rejected as recreating the false-accusation risk Q1/Q2 exist to avoid.
4. **Q4 (BLOCKING) — KEEP.** `cycle_ref` stays in `PersonalEventAssertedPayloadSchema` and the DTO. **Governing principle, recorded for citation:** *"Where a governance assertion concerns a specific contribution opportunity, its provenance shall identify that opportunity even if the assertion has no direct consequence of its own."* `cycle_ref` is **provenance, not causation** — the assertion still carries no consequence of its own (§3.1, unchanged); the provenance is preserved because a later governance decision (a future per-cycle restoration/discharge record) may legitimately need to understand what the assertion referred to.
5. **Q5 — NO, explicitly excluded.** Mirrors Decision `081`'s D4 (a clause may influence trustee understanding without influencing trustee suspicion) on the member's side: a trustee may NOT cite a member's visibility into their own missed cycles as an aggravating "on notice" factor in a suspension decision.
6. **Q6 — DISPLAY-ONLY.** No push notification. The conservative default the original entry assumed, unchanged.

**Artifacts changed:**
- `docs/legal/niyamavali.md` §3.1 — interpretive note added (rule interpretation only, no change to R7(D)–(F)'s substantive text).
- `packages/validity-service/src/producer.ts` — comment addendum only, no logic change, no payload-hash impact.
- `deferred-work.md` — Escalation 5 entry closed with all six answers, per its own stated closure condition.
- `epics.md` — **not touched.** The member-facing story is deliberately not created now; it is created via `bmad-create-story` only after Story 10.23 lands, carrying Q2/Q3/Q5/Q6's constraints into its AC.

**Open follow-ups:**
- Legal-counsel review of the Niyamavali interpretive note (Part 11's standing requirement) — flagged, not verified.
- Story 10.23 (Restoration Discipline Lock-In) is the hard dependency for the missed-cycle surface; its own scope was NOT expanded or redefined by this entry.
- Escalation 3 (10.26, R8 `contribution.compliance_percent` unowned) and Escalation 6 (10.26, Trustee-Lite scan un-attested at 4L) remain open and are unaffected by this entry.

### Decision 2026-08-07-085: Story 10.12's ESCALATION 4 CLOSED — the `[PRIMITIVE]`-with-admin-UI pattern is named as an exception to the one-slice-one-surface discipline, established by Story 10.8 and confirmed by Story 10.12; the original escalation had compared against the wrong sibling

**Decision type:** Other — engineering-process documentation correction (epics.md cross-cutting commitment, NOT the Trustee-Panel-gated Architectural Freeze Boundaries table)
**Status:** Author-committed; no Trustee Panel ratification required (confirmed: `epics.md:568`'s one-slice discipline is not one of the 15 rows in `## Architectural Freeze Boundaries`, `epics.md:510-535`, the table that requires an ADR or trustee-ratified proposal to amend)
**Author:** BigDev, via `bmad-correct-course` (sprint-change-proposal-2026-08-07-R3.md), on Decision 2026-08-06-082's ESCALATION 4
**Ratifying trustees:** n/a — process documentation, not a governance decision

**Context:**
Story 10.12 was labelled `[PRIMITIVE]` ("substrate building block consumed downstream") but ships a full admin UI, apparently in tension with `epics.md:568`'s one-slice-one-surface discipline. The original escalation (recorded at Story 10.12's closure) compared this against Story 10.6 (`[PRIMITIVE]`, bulk-ops, shipped no UI) and flagged a mismatch without resolving it.

**Decision:**
1. **The original escalation compared against the wrong sibling.** Verified live: **Story 10.8** (Feature Flags Per Cohort), also `[PRIMITIVE]`, also `done`, already ships a tenant-scoped admin authoring UI (`apps/admin/src/modules/feature-flags/FeatureFlagsPage.tsx` + `FeatureFlagsRoute.tsx` at `/p/:pariwarId/feature-flags`). Story 10.12's own AC8 states its admin page *"follows the feature-flags template exactly."* Story 10.12 is not an isolated deviation — it is the second deliberate instance of a pattern Story 10.8 established first.
2. **`epics.md:568` is amended with a named exception:** a `[PRIMITIVE]` registry whose only viable population mechanism is administrator-authored data (not downstream code) may include the minimal administrator authoring surface intrinsic to the primitive's existence — the UI must exist solely to author/maintain the primitive and must not evolve into an independent product surface. This does NOT reclassify either story to `[SURFACE]`: both stories' primary deliverable remains the primitive (schema, validation, governance, enforcement), with the UI as one AC among several, not the point of the story.
3. **Neither Story 10.8 nor Story 10.12's historical text is edited.** Their `[PRIMITIVE]` labels stand; Decision `082`'s original escalation text stands as the historical record of what was flagged at the time. Only the forward-looking cross-cutting commitment in `epics.md` changes.
4. **Option B (relabel to `[SURFACE]`) and Option C (leave unresolved) were both considered and rejected** — B would overweight the UI's share of each story's actual substantive work (one AC among eleven for 10.12); C would leave a now-twice-repeated pattern unnamed for the next tenant-authored-registry primitive to re-litigate.

**Open follow-ups:** None — this closes the escalation outright; no further action gated.

### Decision 2026-08-07-084: Trustee Panel rules on Story 10.12's ESCALATIONS 2 & 3 — the epic's worked example is rewritten to a Tier-3 case (not a relaxed guard, not a new Tier-2 host); the repo-wide JSONB-limit coverage gap is accepted as a named deferral with concrete re-triggers

**Decision type:** Other — two Trustee-Panel scope rulings on a shipped story's open escalations (a scope choice + a coverage-gap disposition, distinct in kind from Decision `083`'s architecture-text amendment)
**Status:** Trustee-ratified
**Author:** BigDev, via `bmad-correct-course` (sprint-change-proposal-2026-08-07-R2.md), on Decision 2026-08-06-082's ESCALATIONS 2 and 3
**Ratifying trustees:** Trustee Panel

**Context:**
With ESCALATION 1 closed (Decision `083`), two further open items from Decision `2026-08-06-082` remained: **ESCALATION 2** — the epic's own custom-fields worked example ("alternate ID number", `epics.md:3603`) is Tier-2 by direct analogy to §2.7's eHRMS classification, and fails the shipped Tier-3-only guard that `ADR-0037` already justified keeping (widening it would put an un-blind-indexed, government-adjacent identifier in plaintext JSONB on the certified PII-free `members` table). **ESCALATION 3** — §1.7 freezes that its three JSONB hard-limit classes bind *"every JSONB write path … no code path bypasses them,"* but verified live against the repo, only 1 of 21 files declaring `jsonb(`-typed columns imports `packages/domain/src/custom-fields/limits.ts`; the module's own header already states this as an honest coverage admission rather than a false claim.

**Decision:**
1. **ESCALATION 2 — Option A ratified: rewrite the epic's example, do not relax the guard, do not build a Tier-2 host now.** `epics.md:3603` now reads *"cadre grade"* in place of *"alternate ID number"* — Tier-3 by direct analogy to §2.7's existing `designation` classification, and an example this project's own `ADR-0037` Context section had already named as a valid alternative (alongside "school block code"). No architecture or guard change; `ADR-0037`'s prior rejection of a relaxed Tier-3 detector stands, unrevisited.
2. **A Tier-2 blind-index host story (drafted as a stub, "Story 10.27") was NOT commissioned.** Recorded so the stub is not lost: if a genuine product need for tenant-authored Tier-2 fields emerges, the stub's scope sketch (per-Pariwar HMAC-SHA-256 blind-index host, `pii_tier: 2` gated on its presence, full fence-review re-run per `ADR-0037`'s own precedent) is the starting point, not a fresh design.
3. **ESCALATION 3 — Option B ratified: accepted as a named, gated deferral, not commissioned as a story now.** Repository-wide mechanization is not rejected; it is deferred until there is evidence that another JSONB write path requires the same architectural guarantees §1.7 freezes. This is an evidence-gated deferral, not a scope-avoidance one.
4. **The prior re-trigger text was circular and is replaced.** `deferred-work.md`'s hard-limit-coverage entry previously read *"Re-trigger: ESCALATION 3's disposition"* — self-referential, since this ruling **is** that disposition. Replaced with: *"the first JSONB write path outside `custom-fields/` that is shown (through implementation, incident, or code review) to require architectural payload or nesting limits equivalent to §1.7 but lacks a mechanized enforcement."* The GIN-growth-ceiling entry's re-trigger (*"the first Pariwar whose `ginIndexBytes()` reading approaches the 256 MiB alarm threshold"*) was already concrete and is unchanged.
5. **If a `jsonb-limits` gate is commissioned at the re-trigger, its precedented home is Epic 14's `[GOVERNANCE]` closure pattern** (Story 14.4 FR-100 schema-diff continuous gate, Story 14.7 ADR backlog ratification) — a stub ("Story 14.8") was sketched during this ruling's review for that eventuality, not commissioned.
6. **Neither ruling touches architecture.md.** Distinct from Decision `083`: §1.7's text is already accurate about what it freezes (the *existence* of the limit classes, and the epic-vs-guard tension was never an architecture-text disagreement). Both rulings are scope/coverage decisions, not architecture amendments.

**Open follow-ups:**
- Story 10.27 (Tier-2 blind-index host) and Story 14.8 (`jsonb-limits` gate) exist only as drafted stubs in `sprint-change-proposal-2026-08-07-R2.md` §4 — neither is in `sprint-status.yaml`, deliberately, until their respective re-triggers fire.
- Escalations 4 (`[PRIMITIVE]` label vs. one-slice discipline) and 5 (UX grammar gap) from Decision `082`'s original batch remain open and are unaffected by this entry.

**References:**
- `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/sprint-change-proposal-2026-08-07-R2.md` — full impact analysis and both option sets
- Decision **2026-08-06-082** — the Story 10.12 implementation record raising ESCALATIONS 2 and 3
- Decision **2026-08-07-083** — ESCALATION 1's closure (architecture-text amendment; contrast with this entry's scope-ruling kind)

- architecture §2.7 L1507-1531 (PII tier classification) · §1.7 L943-991 (the frozen limit classes)

### Decision 2026-08-07-083: Trustee Panel ratifies the §1.7 amendment — Story 10.12's ESCALATION 1 CLOSED; architecture §1.7 now names the registry-table medium the codebase actually implements

**Decision type:** Other — architecture-text amendment (ratifies a declared deviation, does not create a new one)
**Status:** Trustee-ratified
**Author:** BigDev, via `bmad-correct-course` (sprint-change-proposal-2026-08-07.md), on Decision 2026-08-06-082's ESCALATION 1
**Ratifying trustees:** Trustee Panel

**Context:**
Decision `2026-08-06-082` (Story 10.12, D1) recorded a declared deviation from architecture §1.7: the ratified text named a versioned code file (`packages/domain/per-pariwar/<id>/schema-v<n>.ts`) as the medium for per-Pariwar custom-field definitions, while the shipped design (ADR-0037 D1) uses an append-only registry table instead, because a code-file edit is a release and FR-54's stated point is *"variation without schema migrations."* That decision raised the amendment as **ESCALATION 1** rather than editing §1.7 unilaterally, per this project's standing rule that architecture is amended by proposal, never by a story's convenience [[feedback_supersede_never_reinterpret]]. This entry ratifies that proposal.

**Decision:**
1. **Architecture §1.7 "Custom-field evolution" is amended** to describe the table-medium design: field definitions live in the append-only, versioned, RLS-scoped `pariwar_custom_field_definitions` registry, keyed by the immutable composite identity `(pariwar_id, host_entity, field_key, version)`; the type allowlist, forbidden-key patterns, and system-level hard limits remain CODE in `packages/domain/`; functional B-tree indexes on JSON paths remain first-class drizzle-kit migrations recorded in `per-pariwar/<id>/index-inventory.ts`. All four of §1.7's original substantive commitments — versioned, no silent renames, deprecation window, migration-gated DDL — are preserved under the new text, not relaxed.
2. **Condition of approval, applied:** the Trustee Panel required an explicit lead sentence stating that *only tenant-authored field definitions live in the registry, while every governance constraint on what a definition may declare is code-owned, never tenant-authored* — landed as §1.7's new first bullet, stated as the section's framing invariant rather than left implicit across sub-clauses.
3. **The illustrative directory tree** (`architecture.md`, per-pariwar/bihar/ listing) is corrected from `schema-v<n>.ts` (never created) to `index-inventory.ts` (the file the shipped design actually places there), matching live repo state.
4. **This ratifies the medium, not the story.** Story 10.12's engineering was already `done` and unaffected by this decision — nothing about the shipped code, tests, or migrations changes. What changes is that the ratified architecture text and the shipped system now agree.

**Open follow-ups:**
- `docs/adr/ADR-0037-per-tenant-custom-fields-jsonb.md` — its Alternatives-Considered note ("Residual risk: the deviation is real and needs the §1.7 amendment") gets a one-line pointer to this decision, not a rewrite.
- `docs/knowledge-transfer/adr-index.md` — ESCALATION 1 note flips from open to closed, pointing here.
- `deferred-work.md` — checked: the Story 10.12 batch (2026-08-06) gates Escalations 2/3/5 only; Escalation 1 was never tracked there (it lived in `.decision-log.md` / ADR-0037 alone), so there is nothing to close in that file.
- Decision `2026-08-06-082` itself is **not** edited — it remains the correct historical record of what was true when Story 10.12 shipped; this entry supersedes only the *architecture text's* prior disagreement with it.
- Escalations 2, 3, 4, 5 (from the same Decision `082` batch) are **unaffected** and remain open — this entry closes ESCALATION 1 only.

**References:**
- `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/sprint-change-proposal-2026-08-07.md` — the full impact analysis and drafted diff
- Decision **2026-08-06-082** — the Story 10.12 implementation record raising ESCALATION 1
- Decision **2026-08-06-080** — the precedent this entry follows (Trustee Panel ratification before editing ratified text, applied there to Niyamavali §3.1)
- `docs/adr/ADR-0037-per-tenant-custom-fields-jsonb.md` D1 — the shipped design this amendment documents
- architecture §1.7 (as amended) — `packages/domain/src/per-pariwar/bihar/` (live repo state confirming `index-inventory.ts`, not `schema-v<n>.ts`)

### Decision 2026-08-06-082: Story 10.12 IMPLEMENTATION RECORD — the definitions registry is a TABLE (a declared §1.7 deviation); the epic's cited 1.16c enforcement DID NOT EXIST and is supplied here as a three-layer fence; v1 is Tier-3-only, which makes the epic's own worked example unbuildable

**Decision type:** Other — implementation-posture record for a shipped story, carrying THREE open escalations
**Status:** Implemented (substrate); ADR-0037 `drafted`, NOT ratified
**Author:** BigDev
**Ratifying trustees:** n/a at author-commit — ⚠ ADR-0037 ratification and Escalations 1/2/3 are open Trustee-Panel items, not formalities

**Context:**
FR-54 commits per-Pariwar extensibility whose stated point is *"variation without schema migrations"*.
Story 10.12 is the substrate. It is also the FIRST extensibility mechanism in this system where a
**tenant** authors a shape the engine then honours — every other one (rule clauses, routing policies,
feature flags, permission keys) is authored by a human through code review. The registry is the easy
half; the fence is the hard half, and it had no owner.

**Decisions recorded:**

1. **⭐ D1 — the definitions live in an append-only registry TABLE, not in `schema-v<n>.ts`. This is a
   DECLARED DEVIATION from architecture §1.7, escalated rather than absorbed.** §1.7 names a CODE FILE
   (`packages/domain/per-pariwar/<id>/schema-v<n>.ts`); `epics.md:3603` names a
   `pariwar_custom_field_definitions` registry with an admin UI. They are different mechanisms and one
   had to give. A code-file edit is a release, so the §1.7 medium cannot satisfy the requirement it was
   written to serve. The split shipped is **by what actually needs a migration**: definitions → table;
   the type allowlist, forbidden-key patterns and hard limits → **code** (a tenant must never author
   the fence); functional indexes → drizzle-kit migrations; the index inventory → `per-pariwar/<id>/`,
   exactly where §1.7 puts it. §1.7's substantive properties all survive (versioned ✓, no silent
   renames ✓ — impossible *by construction*, since the key is part of the version pin — deprecation
   window ✓, migration-gated where DDL is involved ✓). **ESCALATION 1** raises the §1.7 amendment;
   the deviation is stated in ADR-0037's own section and in the schema header
   [[feedback_supersede_never_reinterpret]]. Architecture is amended by proposal, never by a story's
   convenience.

2. **⭐ D-fence — `epics.md:3605`'s cited enforcement DID NOT EXIST, and this story supplies it.** The
   epic's third AC says a `payout_destinations` custom field *"is rejected by Story 1.16c CI gate"*.
   Verified false against the shipped gate: 1.16c is `schema-diff`, an **invariant scan of committed
   repo state** across four roots (drizzle migrations, API route literals, contracts Zod exports,
   snapshot tables/columns), with `allow: []`. A custom field is **a key inside a JSONB payload,
   authored at runtime, into a database row** — none of the four. `pnpm schema:check` would have
   passed, green and useless, while a Pariwar admin created the field. What ships instead is **three
   independent layers**, each with a revert-sanity test: (1) the runtime fence in
   `custom-fields/frozen-governance.ts`, normalizing case and `-`/`.`/whitespace so
   `Payout-Destinations` cannot launder past it; (2) the DB CHECK
   `pariwar_custom_field_definitions_frozen_key_ck` (migration 0095), per 0088's doctrine that *"an
   app-layer rule with no DB mirror is a rule that holds only for the callers who happen to go through
   the app layer"* — **empirically re-proven to FAIL when the constraint is dropped**; (3) the
   `custom-field-governance` CI gate, **honestly scoped** to what committed source can prove (denylist
   ⊇ fr-100 `forbidden_column`; `insert(pariwarCustomFieldDefinitions)` only in the sanctioned writer),
   with the scope limit stated in its README rather than overclaimed
   [[feedback_gate_scope_semantic_coverage]]. The epic's AC is not "wired up"; there was nothing to
   wire.

3. **⚠ D4 — v1 accepts `pii_tier: 3` ONLY, which makes the epic's OWN worked example unbuildable.**
   `members` is a certified PII-free table (Story 3.1, the lifecycle anchor), and hanging tenant-authored
   data on it is the most likely objection to this design — so the mitigation is structural, not a
   promise: every definition declares its tier (restoring §2.7's declaration moment for a
   runtime-authored field), only tier 3 is accepted, and a naked-PII key/label detector catches
   mis-declaration regardless of the declared tier. **The cost is real and is not papered over:
   "alternate ID number" (`epics.md:3603`), the canonical use case, is Tier-2 by direct analogy to
   §2.7's classification of the eHRMS ID, and therefore does not pass.** Shipped as specified rather
   than widened, because widening means an un-blind-indexed government-adjacent identifier in plaintext
   JSONB on the PII-free table — a worse outcome than an unbuilt example. **ESCALATION 2** routes this
   to the Panel. If they rule the other way, the fix is a Tier-2 blind-index host, **not** a relaxed
   detector.

4. **D5 — `indexed: true` records a REQUEST and creates nothing. A tenant admin issues no DDL, ever.**
   The most dangerous shape this story could have taken is one where a form submission causes DDL —
   handing a tenant a lock on a hot table, an unbounded index-growth vector and a migration-history
   bypass in one click. A human authors the functional B-tree index as a migration partial on one
   `pariwar_id` and records it in `per-pariwar/<id>/index-inventory.ts` (empty at v1, correctly: no
   access pattern has been observed). The admin form says plainly that ticking the box changes nothing
   by itself, and a render test pins that copy.

5. **D3 — a hand-written imperative validator, never a Zod schema compiled from a DB row.** No
   precedent exists in this repo for building a Zod schema from data; `zod-to-json-schema`/`ajv` are
   dependencies of no package and would each need their own ADR; and the two nearest analogues
   (`validateRoutingPolicyRules`, `validateFlagVersionInput`) are both hand-written. Adding an
   interpreter over tenant-authored input on the request path was the thing to avoid.

6. **D6 — unknown keys are REJECTED, never dropped.** Silently ignoring an undefined key turns a client
   bug into invisible data loss and a retired field into a value that vanishes untold. The JSONB
   analogue of the contracts layer's `.strict()`.

7. **D2 — no code-resident default; versions start at 1.** The ONE deviation from both registry
   precedents (10.1, 10.8). Those need a default because they must always resolve to something; a
   Pariwar with no definitions has **no custom fields**, a perfectly good state with no document to
   represent. A `DEFAULT_CUSTOM_FIELD_SET` would be inventing an empty thing to be the default of.

8. **The read/write permission split is deliberate, and `district_admin` is NOT granted.** Two keys
   (`pariwar.view_custom_fields` / `pariwar.manage_custom_fields`, catalog 28 → 29 in one bump),
   because a definition set is the tenant's DATA CONTRACT: an auditor must be able to read what a
   Pariwar collects, and at what declared tier, without holding the authority to change it — the 10.8
   doctrine, *"if these ever collapse to one key, the transparency property goes with it."*
   `district_admin` is excluded because a district-ceiling grant can never satisfy a pariwar-dimension
   check, so the grant would be an INERT capability — the Story 10.3 finding
   [[project_rbac_geo_scope_containment]], re-encoded rather than re-learned.

**Two defects the tests caught, recorded because both would have shipped silently:**
- **A depth off-by-one that would have rejected EVERY write containing a `string_array`.**
  `jsonDepth({ values }) + 1` double-counted the envelope, making the effective ceiling 2 against a
  documented limit of 3. Caught by the seven-types integration test, not by any unit test.
- **A prefix matcher that rejected `stateless_note`.** A bare `startsWith('state')` refuses ordinary
  tenant field names; a fence that refuses reasonable names is one authors route around. Fixed by
  giving each denylist entry an explicit match MODE (`prefix` vs `segment`) rather than inventing a
  universal predicate — `payout_destinations` must match and `stateless_note` must not, and the two are
  structurally identical, so no clever rule separates them.

**One shipped governance artifact was modified, and it is flagged rather than buried:**
`scripts/schema-diff/lib.ts` (Story 1.16c) gained a `scannableDdl` narrowing — SQL comments and
single-quoted literals are not identifiers. Migration 0095's CHECK **forbids** a `payout_destination*`
custom field and its comment explains why, and the FR-100 gate flagged both as though each were a
payout-destination column: **the gate reported its own enforcement as a violation of itself.** The
rejected fixes are recorded in the function header (an `allow` entry would DECLARE a
payout-destination artifact permitted — the opposite of the truth). Guarded by four new
revert-sanity fixtures, including one proving dynamic DDL inside a literal is still scanned.

**Open follow-ups (all gated in `deferred-work.md`):**
- **ESCALATION 1** — the §1.7 amendment for D1's registry-medium change. **OWNER: the Trustee Panel /
  architecture.** Until it lands, §1.7's text and the shipped code disagree and ADR-0037 is the record.
- **ESCALATION 2** — the epic's Tier-2 worked example. **OWNER: the Trustee Panel.** Either the epic
  carries a Tier-3 example, or custom fields get a Tier-2 blind-index host (its own story).
- **ESCALATION 3** — §1.7 freezes that all three JSONB limit classes bind *"every JSONB write path …
  no code path bypasses them"*; they bind **none** of the repo's ~20 other JSONB columns. This story
  covers only its own and lands `limits.ts` as the destination. Flag whether this warrants its own gate
  story. §1.7's "write-rate limit when approached" is likewise unbuilt (an observed signal ships).
- **ESCALATION 4** — the `[PRIMITIVE]` label vs one-slice discipline: the story is labelled
  `[PRIMITIVE]` but its AC names an admin UI, while `epics.md:568` says a story touches API **or**
  admin UI and the sibling 10.6 `[PRIMITIVE]` shipped no UI. AC8 keeps the surface minimal; the label
  mismatch is flagged rather than pretended away.
- **ESCALATION 5** — UX has no grammar for this. No form-builder or per-Pariwar settings pattern exists
  anywhere in the spec, and §11 calls component grammar tenant-invariant. The admin page is built to
  the feature-flags template as the nearest precedent; a member-facing renderer needs a UX pass first.
- **Claims and pools are unhosted** — a real FR-54 coverage gap (D7), gated. Claims need their own
  fence review; §1.9/§1.13 guard that aggregate against exactly this vector.
- **ADR-0037 ratification** — `drafted`, with the three escalations above open.

**References:**
- `docs/adr/ADR-0037-per-tenant-custom-fields-jsonb.md` (`drafted`) · `docs/knowledge-transfer/adr-index.md` (Section A row + reconciliation + breakdown 113→112 / 0→1)
- `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/10-12-per-pariwar-custom-fields-jsonb.md`
- architecture §1.7 L943-991 (the deviated-from text + the three frozen limit classes) · §2.7 L1507-1531 (PII tiers) · §1.8 L995-1023 (hot-table migration)
- `epics.md:3593-3605` (Story 10.12; :3603 the Tier-2 worked example, :3605 the unenforceable 1.16c citation) · `prd.md:843-845` (FR-54)
- `implementation-readiness-report-2026-05-28.md:680-704` (IR Item-16 — the Trustee-Panel limit-review path, applied to §1.7 and never to the epic; landed in `limits.ts`)
- `scripts/custom-field-governance/README.md` (the gate's stated scope limit) · `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/gate-inventory.md`
- Migrations `0095_per-pariwar-custom-fields.sql`, `0096_members-custom-fields.sql` (the repo's FIRST GIN index)

### Decision 2026-08-06-081: Story 10.26 IMPLEMENTATION RECORD — the assertion instrument (`member.*`, no free text, no table); R7(G) activated; and the VIOLATOR-FLAG EXCLUSION that keeps an honest disclosure from becoming a suspension signal

**Decision type:** Other — implementation-posture record for a shipped story
**Status:** Implemented
**Author:** BigDev
**Ratifying trustees:** n/a (implementation of ratified policy — see Decision 2026-08-06-080 and `docs/legal/niyamavali.md` §3.1)

**Context:**
`contribution.personal_event_excuse_claimed` was the ONE engine fact key that had never had a source
anywhere in the substrate. Unlike the other six it is not derivable from anything — it is not a
projection of events that already exist, it is a **member act that had never had a place to happen**
(`sprint-change-proposal-2026-08-04-R2.md:106`: *"No assertion path exists."*). Story 10.26 is the
last of the three-story contribution-governance decomposition (10.24 projection + R7(C)–(F); 10.25
restoration accounting; 10.26 the assertion).

**Decisions recorded:**

1. **D2 — the event is `member.personal_event_asserted`, on the MEMBER'S OWN stream.** Three reasons,
   one decisive. (a) Semantics: it is a statement a member makes about their own life, not a
   contribution transaction. (b) The reducer already has this shape — `member.address_updated` /
   `member.posting_updated` are shipped non-transition markers reduced as IDENTITY. (c) ⭐ **Decisive:
   cache invalidation comes free.** Migration `0036_member-validity-cache.sql:103-107` fires on
   `NEW.event_type LIKE 'member.%'` keyed `member_id = NEW.stream_id`, so an assertion evicts the
   member's validity-cache row with **no new trigger and no migration**. Any other namespace would owe
   one, hand-authored, in a subsystem where [[project_live_db_test_gotchas]] applies. Proved by a
   live-DB test rather than assumed. A `contribution.*` name was additionally foreclosed by Story
   8.10's `no-ingest-path` fence, which pins that vocabulary at exactly three.

2. **D3 — a BOUNDED vocabulary; NO free text, not optional and not later.** Free text here would be
   Tier-1 PII of the most sensitive kind (a member describing a death, an illness, a family crisis)
   landing in `events_log`, which is append-only and never redacted — requiring KMS envelope
   encryption, an RTBF path, a PII-scrape-gate exemption, and a tone review of a text box that exists
   to collect grief. **And it would earn nothing:** R7(G) is declarative, there is no reviewer, and
   the engine fact is a boolean. A free-text box with no reader is a false promise that someone is
   listening. Six values (`bereavement · illness · caregiving · displacement · financial_hardship ·
   other`); `other` retained deliberately, because forcing a member to mis-categorise their own life
   is worse than a coarse bucket. Members who need a human are linked to the Helpdesk.

3. **⭐ D4 — THE VIOLATOR-FLAG EXCLUSION, filtered UPSTREAM on clause DATA. The decision this story is
   really about.** `deriveViolatorFlags` maps EVERY R7 clause id it is given into a flag on the
   surface that feeds SUSPENSION decisions — no `applied` check, no outcome check. R7(G) applies
   exactly when a member **told the truth about their own life**. So the naive activation produces a
   system in which disclosing a father's death makes a member a suspension candidate. The ratified
   §3.1 makes that constitutional, not merely humane: the assertion *"carries no consequence of its
   own"*, and a flag on that surface **is** a consequence.

   > **The governing invariant, stated once so it can be cited:
   > A clause may influence trustee UNDERSTANDING without influencing trustee SUSPICION.**
   > `factsEstablishing[]` informs; `flags[]` accuses. R7(G) is granted the first and denied the second.

   Implemented as `imposesRestorationObligation(payload)` — a PURE predicate reading the clause's own
   `restoration` block (R7(A)–(F) prescribe `consecutive_required` / `lock_in_months` /
   `catch_up_required` / `complete_all`; R7(G) prescribes `never_excuses: true`, i.e. nothing).
   **Never a clause-id branch** (the engine's no-branch-on-registry-identity invariant), so a trustee
   amendment moves it and any future declarative clause is excluded automatically.
   `violator-flags.ts` stays **byte-frozen** except its header comment.

4. **⚠ A CORRECTION to the story's own AC5 — the two R7 producers are ASYMMETRIC, because their
   CONSUMERS are.** AC5 asked for the identical filter at both seams on the assumption that both feed
   violator flags. A source trace at implementation time showed they do not:
   `r7-candidate-scan.ts` → `deriveViolatorFlags` → the violator section (**accusation**, filtered);
   `evaluateAppliedR7ClauseSlots` → `assembleClauses` → `MemberValidityPayload` →
   `ui/member-status/presenter.ts` `buildRuleExplanations` (**the member's own record**, NOT filtered).
   Filtering the second would have deleted `memberStatus.rule.rule.no_exemption` — the one thing this
   story exists to put on the member's record (its own AC6) — leaving R7(G) activated but MUTE. That
   is not a relaxation of AC5; it is AC5's invariant applied to the channel that actually accuses.
   The asymmetry is only safe while it stays true, so it is **MECHANIZED** by
   `tests/violator-accusation-channel.test.ts`, which fails if any production module outside the
   trustee-lite handler routes a clause list into `deriveViolatorFlags`. Both the filter and the fence
   were revert-probed and the probes recorded verbatim.

5. **D5 — the fact is a LIFETIME as-of existential, and it is MONOTONE.** `false → true`, never back:
   **a member cannot un-assert.** That is acceptable ONLY because the fact can never harm them (D4).
   **The two decisions are load-bearing on each other: if the D4 exclusion is ever relaxed,
   monotonicity becomes a defect.** Recorded at the derivation site as well as here.

6. **D6 — why activating R7(G) is legitimate where activating R7(A)/(B) is not.** R7(G)'s blocking
   fact had exactly one owner and this story is it; its population is *"a member who asserted"*, which
   **IS** the constitutional fact rather than a proxy for one (so `prd.md:346`'s proxy prohibition does
   not reach it — `:346` exempts R7(C)–(G) by name); it is RATIFIED into §3.1 with no outstanding
   registry amendment; and activating it moves NO eligibility. Deleting its `R7_HELD_CLAUSES` entry
   (rather than narrowing it, as 10.25 correctly did for R7(A)) is right **here and only here**.

7. **D7 — no table, no projection, no migration.** 10.24's D8 ruled that projections do not need
   events; this is the MIRROR case and the rule points the other way — **a new fact needs an event
   exactly when nothing in the system already knows it.** The event IS the record; the existential is
   read straight off `events_log`.

**What this decision explicitly does NOT do:**
- **It does not grant, waive or excuse anything.** `on_pass: no_exemption`, `never_excuses: true`. No
  approval field, no status, no reviewer, no state machine, nothing to reverse — and no `GET`, because
  a member has nothing to check back on. The response's `grantsRelief` is a `z.literal(false)` so the
  WIRE asserts it and a future handler cannot ship `true` without a contract change.
- **It does not activate R7(A)/(B).** Their holds are unchanged, and their blockers are NOT facts.
- **It does not bump `POOL_ASSIGNMENT_HASH_VERSION`.** The roster reads `isAssignable` only;
  `assignment-version-pin-replay.spec.ts` is byte-unchanged and green.
- **It does not edit the R7(G) clause JSONB.** The seed's comment block was updated; the payload was
  not. Decision 2026-08-06-080 already did the data-side flag drop.
- **It does not close Escalation 5** (no member surface shows a MISSED cycle) — recorded in
  `deferred-work.md` with an owner still to be assigned.

**Open follow-ups:**
- **Escalation 5** — no member surface shows a missed cycle; `cycle_ref` ships unpopulated.
  **OWNER: the TRUSTEE PANEL** (assigned 2026-08-06 by BigDev). They own the DECISION, not the
  surface; implementation ownership is assigned by their answer, and if the answer is "a member may
  not be shown missed cycles" or "the cycle anchor is decoration" there is nothing to implement.
  **Six questions are recorded verbatim in `deferred-work.md`, two of them BLOCKING** (may a member
  be shown their own missed cycles at all; does the assertion's cycle anchor have constitutional
  meaning or should `cycle_ref` be removed). ⚠ **Decaying, not dormant:** R7(G) is live and
  assertions are being recorded now with no cycle anchor — if cycle-scoping is later ruled to matter,
  those cannot be backfilled ([[feedback_record_unattested_no_backfill]]).
- **Escalation 3** — `contribution.compliance_percent` (R8) is unowned AND un-mechanized; with
  `R7_HELD_FACTS` empty, nothing watches it any more.
- **Escalation 6** — the Trustee-Lite Pariwar scan remains UN-ATTESTED at 4L.
- **Escalation 4** — no UX-spec coverage for this surface; the tone review is recorded in
  `deferred-work.md`.
- The ≤60 s deploy window + the `invalidate-all` lever, for the third time.

**References:**
- `docs/legal/niyamavali.md:81` (§3.1) — the ratified R7(G) text binding D4
- `_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/10-26-r7g-personal-event-excuse-assertion.md`
- Decision **2026-08-06-080** — the ratification this story implements against
- Decision **2026-08-06-079** — the 10.25 record whose R7(G) fact-hold follow-up this discharges

### Decision 2026-08-06-080: Trustee Panel ratifies R7(F) and R7(G) into Niyamavali §3.1/Appendix A — moderation brief D9 and Story 10.26 Escalation 1 both discharged; the seed's `provisional`/`policy_review_required` flags dropped on those two clauses only

**Decision type:** Other — governance-text ratification (Part 11 amendment) + implementation-flag consequence
**Status:** Trustee-ratified
**Author:** BigDev, on the moderation-model-decision-brief's D9 and Story 10.26's Escalation 1
**Ratifying trustees:** Trustee Panel

**Context:**
Two clauses were live in the seeded engine registry (`packages/domain/seed/niyamavali-v1-clauses.sql`)
and named in the PRD as part of "R7(A–G)," but absent from the ratified legal text: `docs/legal/niyamavali.md`
§3.1 documented only R7(A)–(E), and Appendix A's rule index range read `R7(A)–(E)`. This was a named
drift in `moderation-model-decision-brief.md` (§2.2 table, `:398-411`) — the brief's **D9** recommended
ratifying R7(F) as a real rung and documenting it; Story 10.26's **Escalation 1** raised the identical
gap for R7(G) and recommended the Trustee Panel "acknowledge or amend R7(G)'s §3.1 status as a Part 11
item" without treating it as a blocker to that story's (still-unstarted) implementation.

**Decision:**

1. **R7(F) and R7(G) are ratified as real Niyamavali clauses.** `docs/legal/niyamavali.md` §3.1 now
   states R7(F) (a 6–11 month gap → 5-month lock-in + complete-all) and R7(G) (an asserted personal
   event → no exemption, declarative, no restoration consequence). Appendix A's index now reads
   `R7(A)–(F)` for the restoration ladder with R7(G) listed separately (it is not a restoration path).
2. **R7(C)'s threshold is stated explicitly as a corollary, not a new ratification.** D9's two-rung
   ladder (R7(F): 6–11 months; R7(C): 12 months or more) only reads coherently if both thresholds are
   stated together, so §3.1's R7(C) row now names its 12-month-or-more threshold. **This is textual
   clarification only** — R7(C)'s `provisional`/`policy_review_required` seed flags are **unchanged**
   (still `true`); its own ratification was not confirmed in this pass and must not be inferred from
   R7(F)'s.
3. **The seed payload flags follow the ratification, for R7(F) and R7(G) only.** Both rows in
   `niyamavali-v1-clauses.sql` now carry `"policy_review_required":false,"provisional":false`, matching
   the mechanism D9 already named for R7(C) ("record 12 months as the ratified R7(C) threshold so it
   can drop `provisional: true`"). R7(A)/(B)/(C)/(D)/(E) are untouched.

**What this decision explicitly does NOT do:**
- **It does not activate R7(G) in the engine.** `R7_ACTIVATED_CLAUSE_IDS` / `R7_HELD_CLAUSES` /
  `R7_SUPPLIED_FACT_KEYS` are unchanged; `contribution.personal_event_excuse_claimed` remains held
  exactly as Decision 2026-08-06-079's open follow-ups record (`.decision-log.md:136` at time of
  writing) — that line is about the FACT PRODUCER and clause activation, a Story 10.26 code-delivery
  concern, and is **NOT discharged by this entry**. Do not collapse "documented in the legal text" with
  "evaluated by the engine" — [[feedback_closure_language_precision]].
- **It does not touch the clause JSONB's operative fields** (`all_of`, `on_pass`, `on_fail`,
  `restoration`, `precedence`) — only the two review-status flags. The rule the engine evaluates is
  unchanged.
- **It does not resolve CR-4.2-D3** (`deferred-work.md`, code review of 4-3-r8-90-percent-rule) — the
  R7(C)-vs-R7(F) lock-in-duration asymmetry (3 months vs 5 months) that entry flags as a separate,
  unaddressed Trustee-review question. Its re-trigger ("Trustee Panel review of FR-9 R7(C) vs R7(F)
  lock-in parameters") may now be closer, but was not itself confirmed reviewed in this pass — left
  open, not claimed closed.
- **It does not amend Decision 2026-08-06-079 or any other prior entry.** Per Decision 2026-08-06-078,
  superseded decisions are recorded as new entries, never edited in place.

**Open follow-ups:**
- Story 10.26's own Escalation 1 is annotated at its definition site
  (`_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/10-26-r7g-personal-event-excuse-assertion.md`) pointing back
  here, so a future dev-story pass does not re-raise it.
- CR-4.2-D3 (the C/F lock-in asymmetry) remains open in `deferred-work.md` — unaddressed by this
  ratification.
- R7(A)/(B)/(D)/(E) remain `provisional`/`policy_review_required: true`; no action taken on them here.

**References:**
